# REMEDIATION_INDEX.md — Fast Lookup

**105 remediation tasks + 15 accepted no-action items**, consolidated from 686 raw per-file findings
plus the audit's 6 synthesis documents. Full detail for every ID is in `MASTER_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md`.

> ## CAMPAIGN EXIT STATE (2026-08-04) — **PHASE-1 CHECKPOINT TAKEN; blocker set EMPTY**
>
> Authoritative exit record: **`REMEDIATION_EXIT.md`** — **OUTCOME A, READY**. Signed annotated tag
> **`cna-post-audit-remediation-phase1`** on `feature/audit`, local only, **not pushed**.
> **This is phase 1, not the completion of all CNA work.**
>
> Branch inventory: **`INTEGRATION_BRANCH_INVENTORY.md`** — dynamic, re-derived after
> `git fetch --all --prune --tags`: **21 logical pending integration lanes**, and N may change before
> integration begins. **Do not restate 19** — that figure predates a fetch and survives only as a
> labelled historical snapshot. Magnum and Wicked are **audit-stacked lanes** (13 and 10 own commits
> on `feature/audit` @ `2338b44f7`), not develop-forked backends. `feature/direct2d` is the **final
> actively-developed lane** and must be frozen at a known head before integration begins.
>
> **Post-exit update 2026-08-08: `REMED-CONTENT-007` and `REMED-CONTENT-008` are DONE.** The two
> HIGH/P1 content-root-containment findings named here at phase-1 exit were reproduced with
> synthetic red-first public-caller tests, fixed through the shared component-aware path primitive,
> and verified on the final integration tree. The bounded same-pattern audit found and closed
> **`REMED-CONTENT-011`** for additional omitted Model/SkinnedModel and indirect manifest fields.
> Full contract, symlink boundary, oracles, caller coverage, audit classification, and sanitizer
> evidence are in `REMEDIATION_PROGRESS.md`. The frozen 105-task/15-no-action counts below predate
> these discovered-during-remediation IDs and are deliberately not renumbered. The historical
> phase-1 statement that no security-clean claim was made remains true for that earlier checkpoint.
>
> **`WEBGPU-115` — RESOLVED 2026-08-04.** It was the last recorded checkpoint blocker: WebGPU
> reported `GraphicsCapability::WireFrame` as `true` (inherited `IGraphicsBackend` default; the
> backend never overrode it), accepted a `FillMode::WireFrame` draw with no throw, warning or log,
> created and **natively submitted** a distinct pipeline keyed on `wireframe`, and returned a frame
> **byte-identical to Solid** (`total=18176 interior=1089/1089` for both). It now reports the
> capability as **`false`** and throws `System::NotSupportedException` from the first **polygon**
> draw that would consume it — before any command is queued, any pipeline key is computed, any
> `WGPURenderPipeline` is created, any pass is encoded or anything is submitted. Target unchanged,
> Solid recovery exact. Line and point topologies are deliberately **not** refused: they have no
> polygon interior for a fill mode to select and were measured byte-identical under both modes.
> `plan_webgpu.md`'s `WEBGPU-115` row is now **`✅`**.
>
> `REMED-GFX-219` (EasyGL **under**-reports `WireFrame` while rendering a correct one) is LOW/P3,
> blocker **NO**, DEFERRED. **Do not bundle it with `WEBGPU-115`** — their safety directions are
> opposite.
>
> **Corrected 2026-08-04 (first pass):** the "Discovered during remediation" table in

Severity and priority deliberately diverge in 9 tasks. The clearest cases: `REMED-BUILD-001` and
`REMED-BUILD-002` are HIGH but scheduled **P0** for leverage; `REMED-CONTENT-006` is MEDIUM but
scheduled **P0** because it is a reachable stack-exhaustion DoS; `REMED-GFX-031`/`-032`/`-048`/`-050`
are MEDIUM but scheduled **P3** because they are isolated and low-blast-radius.

### By owner lane

| Owner | Tasks | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **GRAPHICS** | 52 | Largest lane by far. Internally serialized for shader work — see `REMEDIATION_DEPENDENCIES.md`. |
| **BUILD_TEST_CI** | 19 | `BUILD` (9) + `TEST` (7) + `DOCS` (3) |
| **CORE** | 13 | |
| **NET** | 7 | Includes GamerServices |
| **CONTENT** | 5 | Includes Storage and XNB |
| **MEDIA** | 4 | |
| **DEVICES** | 3 | |
| **AUDIO** | 2 | Lane barely justified — see below |
| **INPUT** | 0 | **Lane not created** — see below |

**On the AUDIO and INPUT lanes.** The audit swept `Microsoft.Xna.Framework.Audio` (31 files, described
as "the most thoroughly self-audited subsystem encountered") and `Input` (44 files, plus a full
member-level xn65 cross-check that found zero discrepancies across `Buttons`' 32 bit values, `Keys`'
160 entries, and every dead-zone/clamp formula). Between them they produced **two LOW findings and one
tagging nit.** Standing up dedicated lanes for these would be ceremony, not capacity. `AUDIO` is kept
as an ID namespace for its two tasks; `INPUT` is not created at all, and its single finding is folded
into `REMED-CORE-012`. This is a real result about the codebase, not an omission.

| `REMED-GFX-065` | Bgfx per-view rect can't represent 2+ viewports on one view in a frame (affects backbuffer too). **DONE** — bgfx view state (`setViewRect`/`setViewTransform`) is per-view/last-wins, so two different `GraphicsDevice.Viewport`s drawn to one target in one frame collapsed onto its base view id and the first draw was lost (3D + SpriteBatch). Fix = ordered per-frame **view segmentation**: the FIRST viewport uses the target's base view unchanged (a custom viewport still shrinks it, GFX-063 — single-viewport frames byte-identical to pre-fix); a SECOND, different viewport allocates an ordered segment view (`SelectViewportSegment`, id in a reserved `[192,255)` range above every RT base id), so bgfx's ascending-view-id execution keeps base→segments in submission order (A→B→A works; the re-appearance of A is a NEW segment, never merged). Each segment clears its OWN depth+stencil to the frame's clear values (bgfx's per-view clear was scoped to the first viewport's rect, leaving later viewports' depth stale → 3D draws would fail the depth test) while preserving colour; sprite segments clear nothing. Segment ids recycle every frame (`EndFrameSegments`); exhausting the range throws a deterministic error (no silent view-id wrap). RT/MRT/cube base + framebuffer tracked via `ResetSegmentTarget`. New `Bgfx_MultiViewport` **9→15/15** (3D + SpriteBatch two disjoint viewports, A→B→A ordering, RenderTarget2D, same-viewport reuse, 16-viewport stress) deterministic on bgfx-OpenGL 2.1 AND bgfx-Vulkan (real GPU). Regressions green on both renderers: GFX-063 10/10, GFX-066 13/13, GFX-067 72/72, GFX-072 10/10, GFX-078 32/32, viewport_subregion 5/5; full Bgfx shard 120/124 (4 pre-existing bgfx-Vulkan→GL2.1-fallback MSAA/mip/cube env failures, confirmed identical with the fix reverted). No shader diff. Spawned **GFX-084** (SpriteBatch `setViewTransform` last-wins for same-viewport/different-transform batches — a residual view-global-state gap the viewport-keyed segmentation doesn't cover). test `104025d2`, fix `d40375c3` | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-066` | Bgfx per-draw `bgfx::setScissor` on FBO/render-target views — **NOT A DEFECT** (false finding; RT scissor clips correctly on bgfx-GL + Vulkan; regression added) | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-067` | Bgfx RenderTarget2D sampled back through `SpriteBatch` reads **vertically mirrored** — the "size dependency" was FALSE (uniform across all sizes; earlier flaky size-correlated observations were Y-blind/position-only content + first-read screenshot-pipeline latency). Root cause: on originBottomLeft renderers (OpenGL) an FBO color attachment stores its texels **bottom-up**, so sampling with the ordinary top-down V mirrors it; ordinary Texture2D (top-down) and the backbuffer readback (screenshot `_yflip`) are unaffected; bgfx-Vulkan (originBottomLeft=false) already correct. **DONE** — `SubmitSprite` flips the sampled V of render-target sources when `caps->originBottomLeft` (new `IBgfxSamplable::IsRenderTargetColorSource()`); `Bgfx_RenderTarget_Orientation` 72/72 on bgfx-OpenGL AND bgfx-Vulkan, GFX-063/066 stay green, zero shader diff. Spawned **GFX-078** | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-078` | Bgfx generic 3D-effect texture binding (`DrawPrimitivesEx*`/`FillGpuDrawParams` path) uses `static_cast<const BgfxTextureBackend&>(*params.textureN)` on every effect sampler slot (texture0/1, PBR normal/MR/emissive/occlusion, dual-texture, env-map 2D). A `RenderTarget2D` set as an effect texture (`effect.Texture = renderTarget`) is a `BgfxRenderTargetBackend` — an unrelated sibling of `BgfxTextureBackend` — so this static_cast is **UB** reading the wrong pooled handle (the exact bug class Task 873 fixed for `SpriteBatch` and Task 907 for cubes, never fixed for the 3D 2D-texture path). Means the ONLY working RT-sample consumer on Bgfx is `SpriteBatch` (why GFX-067's fix is scoped there). Surfaced by GFX-067 Phase 27. **DONE** — new `BindSamplerSlot()` resolves every slot's handle through `IBgfxSamplable` (all 27 casts removed) and records render-target color sources per slot into `u_rtFlipV`; the 10 2D-sampling fragment shaders V-flip flagged slots only when `caps->originBottomLeft` (generic-effect counterpart of GFX-067's SpriteBatch flip, per-slot so DualTextureEffect's two layers flip independently). UB proven by `-fsanitize=vptr` on the exact cast shape; `Bgfx_RenderTarget_EffectTexture` 13/32 → 32/32 on bgfx-OpenGL AND bgfx-Vulkan; ordinary Texture2D byte-identical; GFX-067/063/066 + all effect families green; 40 regenerated shader arrays (10 fs × 4 profiles), all else byte-identical. Cube path already safe (Task 907). | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-064` | SdlGpu + D3D12 never wire `GraphicsDevice.Viewport` (base no-op `SetViewport`; full-target hardcoded) — **DONE** (SdlGpu per-draw capture; D3D12 store+consume) | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-068` | SdlGpu per-pass scissor reset to full-**backbuffer** on RT unbind (deferred-model, analog of Vulkan GFX-013) — **DONE** (per-draw scissor capture, mirrors GFX-064 viewport) | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-069` | SdlGpu never applies `BlendState.BlendFactor` (constant blend color): pipeline maps `Blend.BlendFactor`/`InverseBlendFactor`→`SDL_GPU_BLENDFACTOR_CONSTANT_COLOR` but no `SetBlendFactor` override / `SDL_SetGPUBlendConstants` call, so constant-color blends used SDL's driver-default constant (observed `(1,1,1,1)`/white on llvmpipe) — **DONE** (per-draw BlendFactor capture, mirrors GFX-064 viewport / GFX-068 scissor; `0/8 → 8/8`) | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-070` | Vulkan applies `vkCmdSetBlendConstants` only ONCE per frame (at backbuffer-pass begin, from the live member) although pipelines declare `VK_DYNAMIC_STATE_BLEND_CONSTANTS`: RT-pass constant-color blends get an undefined/stale constant (also a real `VUID-vkCmdDraw-None-07835` for blend-enabled RT draws) and multiple `BlendFactor` values in one frame collapse to last-wins — the Vulkan analog of GFX-069 (viewport/scissor were made per-draw by GFX-062/013 but blend constants were not). **DONE** (per-draw/per-batch BlendFactor capture in `Pending3DDraw`/`BatchSnapshot`, replayed via `vkCmdSetBlendConstants` in `draw3DFor`/`drawSpritesFor`; `0/8 → 7/7`, zero validation errors) | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-071` | Vulkan 2D **sprite** pipeline (`GetOrCreatePipeline2D`/`2DMsaa`) hardcodes `SRC_ALPHA`/`ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` and takes no blend params, so `SpriteBatch` **ignores the custom BlendState entirely** — `Additive`/`NonPremultiplied`/`BlendFactor`/separate factors/functions are all silent no-ops for sprites (the 3D path was fixed by Task 868 and honors them). Discovered during GFX-070 Phase 16: the per-batch blend-constant capture is correctly wired but inert because the sprite pipeline never selects `CONSTANT_COLOR`. Separate/broader than the blend constant. FIXED (`2db09690`): batch's BlendState captured by value into `BatchSnapshot`, plumbed into the 2D pipeline cache key + `FillBlendAttachmentState`; GFX-070 constant now live for sprites; `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_BlendState` 2/17→17/17. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-016` | EasyGL MRT second attachment never drawn | HIGH | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-017` | Bgfx public `CullMode` winding contract — original failure disproved: the production state-bit mapping was already correct; the failing shared checks read the Bgfx framebuffer twice and sampled different frames. One-frame paired-winding regression passes 81/81 on both OpenGL and Vulkan routes. | HIGH | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-018` | Bgfx `Clear` ignored `ClearOptions`. **DONE** — public `GraphicsDevice.Clear(options, colour, depth, stencil)` now maps the seven non-empty masks exactly to bgfx colour/depth/stencil clear bits; zero flags are a no-op, and unavailable depth/stencil are ignored on depthless targets after public depth validation. Root cause was the production drop in `EnsureViewState`: every base view unconditionally received `BGFX_CLEAR_COLOR\|DEPTH\|STENCIL` while segment views received none, so the requested mask disappeared (`ClearDepth` was also value-only). Each clear is now a full-target ordered view operation with its exact mask and values; draw-only GFX-065/GFX-084 segments load all attachments, repeated clears and A→B→A target transitions cannot collapse through bgfx's per-view last-wins state, and viewport/scissor do not restrict `Clear`. Permanent `Bgfx_GraphicsDevice_ClearOptions` covers all 8 masks, public overloads, distinct preserved colour/depth/stencil, backbuffer, `RenderTarget2D` (`None`, `Depth24`, `Depth24Stencil8`), ordered/repeated clears, target isolation/transitions, and one snapshot per observable result: **232/232** on both active OpenGL 2.1 and Vulkan routes; Vulkan validation clean. GFX-065 **15/15**, GFX-084 **21/21**, GFX-017 **81/81** remain green on both routes; cross-backend controls pass and all 14 backend libraries compile with at most `-j4`. regression `7d176292`, fix `aad8ed4f`, depth-probe strengthening `72cf5fb7` | HIGH | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-019` | WebGPU `SpriteBatch` clip space backbuffer-relative: `QueueSprite()` baked NDC from `ComputeLogicalViewport()`+`physicalWidth_/physicalHeight_` with no bound-render-target branch, so `SpriteBatch.Draw()` into a differently-sized RenderTarget2D/cube face mis-mapped its destination rectangle (half-scaled toward the origin for a 2×-wider backbuffer) — the only backbuffer-hardcoded backend of 6. **DONE** (identity-viewport `currentRenderTarget_`/cube-face branch using the target's own size, mirrors SdlGpu; `WebGPU_SpriteBatch_RenderTarget` `12/23 → 23/23`, EasyGL parity 6/6, zero validation errors, zero shader diff). Spawned **GFX-072** | HIGH | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-072` | SpriteBatch clip space ignored a custom `GraphicsDevice.Viewport` sub-region: the sprite NDC/ortho divided by the full target, so a custom sub-viewport **squished** sprites (WebGPU/Vulkan/SdlGpu/D3D12) or was **ignored** (EasyGL/Bgfx). XNA/FNA build the SpriteBatch ortho from `Viewport.Width/Height` (viewport-local; D3D11/D3D9 already correct via live viewport). **DONE** — fixed all 6 affected backends (7 SpriteBatch backends total; D3D12 found as a 7th by the survey); every backend + the unmodified D3D11 oracle now render the canonical scene to the byte-identical footprint `x[24,40] y[15,25] n=187`. Runtime-verified Vulkan/SdlGpu/EasyGL/Bgfx/WebGPU (Xvfb :99) + D3D11 oracle (Wine+DXVK); D3D12 build-verified. Spawned **GFX-073**, **GFX-074** | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-073` | Software (CPU-raster) backend SpriteBatch ignored `GraphicsDevice.Viewport`: sprites rasterized at raw framebuffer pixels and `SetViewport` was a no-op, so a custom viewport neither positioned nor clipped 2D. Not the GFX-072 mis-scale (Software never divides by a target size). **DONE** — `SetViewport` now stores the viewport; SpriteBatch adds the viewport origin after `transformMatrix` (viewport-local composition) and clips to the viewport rect via a new `RasterClipRect` on `RasterizeTriangleShaded`; the 3D path passes the full-framebuffer clip so it stays byte-identical, and the default full viewport is byte-identical. New `Software_SpriteBatch_CustomViewport` `5/17 → 19/19` (incl. a RenderTarget2D-target control), canonical scene renders to the GFX-072 byte-identical footprint `x[24,40] y[15,25] n=187`; full Software shard 7/7; UBSan clean; zero shader diff. Correcting the original note: the Software **3D** path does NOT consume a custom viewport either (it maps NDC over the full framebuffer via `GetViewportSize`, no X/Y offset / W/H sub-scale / clip / depth-range) — spawned **GFX-079**. `SetScissorRect` is also a no-op — spawned **GFX-080**. test `d7521c8f`, fix `280f7bdd` | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-079` | Software (CPU-raster) backend **3D** path (`DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`/`DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx`) ignores a custom `GraphicsDevice.Viewport`: `ClipVertexToRasterVertex` receives `GetViewportSize()` (= the full current framebuffer, NOT the stored viewport) and applies no `Viewport.X/Y` offset, no `Width/Height` sub-scaling, no viewport clip, and no `MinDepth/MaxDepth` depth-range remap. Correct for the default full-target viewport; a custom sub-viewport is silently ignored. Sibling of GFX-073 (2D); its stored-viewport state can be reused. **DONE** — `ClipVertexToRasterVertex` now applies FNA's `Viewport.Project` transform (X/Y offset + W/H sub-scale + `MinDepth/MaxDepth` remap) via a `ViewportTransform`; both 3D paths clip to framebuffer ∩ Viewport via `ViewportClip` (`RasterizeTriangle` gained the `RasterClipRect` the shaded variant already had); new `GetActiveViewportRaster` resolves the active viewport once for all four entry points. Default full-target viewport byte-identical. `Software_3D_CustomViewport` 4/25 → 25/25 (A `x[19,59] y[11,39]`, RT-relative, MinDepth/MaxDepth remap, indexed==non-indexed, multi-viewport, winding preserved); Software shard 8/8; GFX-073 SpriteBatch still 19/19; UBSan clean; zero shader diff. test `4cd728a1`, fix `9e80ebd2` | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-080` | Software (CPU-raster) backend `SoftwareGraphicsBackend::SetScissorRect(...)` is a no-op (`{}`) **and** `ApplyRasterizerState` discarded its `ScissorTestEnable` argument, so `GraphicsDevice.ScissorRectangle` (with `RasterizerState.ScissorTestEnable`) never clipped either the 2D sprite or the 3D raster path — the whole target was always writable. Independent of GFX-073/079's viewport clip (a separate raster-state gap). **DONE** — `SetScissorRect` now stores the rectangle (independent of the enable flag), `ApplyRasterizerState` captures `ScissorTestEnable`, and a new `ScissorClip` folds the framebuffer/target-space scissor into the existing `RasterClipRect` at all 5 raster sites (SpriteBatch + 4× 3D) → effective clip = `framebuffer ∩ Viewport ∩ (Scissor when enabled)`. Scissor is framebuffer-space, not viewport-local (no double `Viewport.X/Y` offset); disabled scissor is byte-identical to pre-fix. | `REMED-GFX-076` | Vulkan per-frame effect descriptor-set caches (`dualTex/envMap/litTextured/fogTex3D/skinned/pbr/pbrSkinned DescSets_`) key on a hash of raw `VkImageView` pointers and persist across frames with no free path until teardown. GFX-075's retirement queue fixes the UAF (a dying view stays alive through the deferred-consume window), but once that view is finally freed its handle value can be reused by a new texture whose hash collides → a stale cached descriptor set samples the wrong (destroyed) image. Effect pools gained `VK_DESCRIPTOR_POOL_CREATE_FREE_DESCRIPTOR_SET_BIT` + a per-pool fenced free path; the two cube destructors (`TextureCube`/`RenderTargetCube`), which evicted from NO cache before, now evict their cube view. Sign matches XNA/D3D/GL/Vulkan (positive → +depth → away from camera; verified live against the EasyGL/Vulkan oracle: negative → toward camera). Biased depth clamped to [0,1] only when a bias is active; zero-bias takes no offset/clamp branch and is **byte-identical** to pre-GFX-083 (slope math runs only when SlopeScaleDepthBias≠0; a flat polygon gets no slope term). Software's fixed LessEqual depth compare (a separate pre-existing v1 boundary) makes its coplanar baseline differ from EasyGL's strict-Less, but the bias sign is identical. | `REMED-GFX-117` | **SDL_GPU indexed draws drop `startIndex` and `baseVertex` — DONE (2026-07-27).** All eight `SDL_DrawGPUIndexedPrimitives` sites read byte-identically `SDL_DrawGPUIndexedPrimitives(pass, command.indexCount, 1, 0, 0, 0)`: the eight `Queue*Draw` functions read only `params.vertexStart` (the non-indexed offset) and never `params.startIndex`/`params.baseVertex`, and the eight draw-command structs had no field to carry them, so only the topology-derived index count survived into the native draw. The SDL_GPU counterpart of REMED-GFX-020/060/107. Separated with decoy geometry on both sides of the requested range through `RenderTarget2D::GetData` (this backend implements no backbuffer readback): a zero-offset control rendered exactly, `startIndex` alone drew the element-zero prefix, and `baseVertex` alone drew the *unbased* decoy — 888 exact pixels — while the double-based decoy stayed black, proving a dropped offset rather than a doubled one. All eight sites now resolve their native arguments through one shared `ResolveIndexedRange`: `first_index = startIndex` as an index **element** offset (SDL 3.5.0 defines it that way, not as a byte offset), `vertex_offset = baseVertex` as `Sint32` applied exactly once, and `num_indices` the exact topology-derived count, all carried by value on the draw command so deferred draws keep their own arguments. Ranges are derived in 64-bit and rejected — never clamped — for negative `startIndex`/`baseVertex`/`minVertexIndex`/`numVertices`, a non-positive `primitiveCount`, an index range leaving the bound index buffer, a declared decoded-vertex range leaving the bound vertex buffer, and anything the `Uint32`/`Sint32` native types cannot represent. `minVertexIndex` is never used as an address. DrawUser keeps zero native offsets by design (GraphicsDevice already copies and rebases its data; both its offsets are exercised so a double-apply would show) and SDL_GPU has no instanced indexed path — `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` is not overridden and the base class throws. New permanent regression **2/27 → 27/27**, the two pre-fix passes being the zero-offset control and DrawUser. 16- and 32-bit indices, static and dynamic buffers, all five topologies, all eight pipeline families (colored/textured/colored-textured/lit-textured/alpha-test/dual-texture/env-map/skinned/PBR), A→B→A across two buffer pairs and two render targets, post-queue mutation, source disposal, depthless and depth-backed targets, and explicit Viewport/Scissor/Rasterizer (incl. GFX-051 DepthBias)/Blend/DepthStencil state all pass. Full SDL_GPU `CnaTests` **5697 passed** with the same four pre-existing failures A/B-proven against a reverted build; cross-backend controls Vulkan **30/30**, WebGPU **50/50**, EasyGL **44/44**, Bgfx **60/60**, Software **19 + 1 documented skip**, D3D11 **26/26**, D3D9 **26/26**, Headless **4/4**; ASan **33/33** and UBSan **33/33** clean. No shader or generated artifact changed. test `cf8eb39e`, fix `fcce95f7`, coverage `db4aa914`. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-118` | **Bgfx instanced indexed draws drop `baseVertex`, `startIndex` and the index count — DONE (2026-07-27).** `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` bound the whole-buffer `bgfx::setVertexBuffer(0, handle)` and `bgfx::setIndexBuffer(ib.handle)` overloads, so the public `baseVertex`, `startIndex` and topology-derived index count never reached the native instanced draw: every instance consumed the complete index buffer from element zero. REMED-GFX-107's defect class in the one Bgfx path it did not cover. The two-axis regression separated it cleanly — a three-of-seven-triangle request lit all seven slots (19750 pixels of decoy geometry) while the instance axis already passed, which excluded the shader, the per-instance stream, culling, the target and the readback. The binding is now `setIndexBuffer(handle, startIndex, IndexCountForPrimitives(topology, count))` and `setVertexBuffer(0, handle, baseVertex, vertexCount - baseVertex)`, the exact model REMED-GFX-107 established: `startIndex` is an index-**element** offset, `baseVertex` is the vertex stream's start element so every decoded index picks it up exactly once, and `minVertexIndex`/`numVertices` stay range hints. `instanceCount` never widens or narrows the range and the range never changes the instance count. The `FillMode.WireFrame` branch keeps binding from element zero because its expanded indices are already absolute. A new `ResolveIndexedRange` resolves and validates the pair in 64-bit before anything is allocated or submitted, so an out-of-buffer request throws `System::ArgumentOutOfRangeException` instead of being clamped and an overflowing `primitiveCount` is rejected rather than wrapped; the per-instance copy no longer takes `min(requested, available)`, so surplus instances can no longer read uninitialised transient memory. `GraphicsDevice::DrawInstancedPrimitives` gained the matching public guard its indexed sibling has had since REMED-GFX-107 — `minVertexIndex`/`numVertices` validated and forwarded instead of discarded, the index range checked against the bound index buffer, the declared vertex window against the bound vertex buffer after `baseVertex`, and `instanceCount` against the bound per-instance stream. No repacking, per-draw allocation, extra native handle, extra submission or shader change. New permanent regression **2/20 → 20/20** on Bgfx OpenGL and **3/20 → 19/20 + 1 explicit skip** on genuine Bgfx Vulkan (`active renderer: Vulkan` on all 20 device creations; zero Khronos validation messages for this suite). Cross-backend controls: Vulkan **16/16**, WebGPU **16/16**, D3D9 **16/16**, EasyGL **1/1** pin; D3D11 measured **3/16** with the guard temporarily widened and reverted. Bgfx range/point/indexed gates **80/80**, Bgfx binary shard **124/130** (the same six A/B-proven pre-existing failures), full Bgfx `CnaTests` **5748 passed** with the same four pre-existing failures, ASan and UBSan **80/80** each. | `REMED-GFX-119` | **Software non-indexed draws ignore `vertexStart` — DONE (2026-07-27).** `SoftwareGraphicsBackend::DrawPrimitivesEx` received the public `vertexStart` in `GpuDrawParams` (REMED-GFX-113 sets it) and never read it: it addressed the bound buffer with the raw loop ordinal `i * 3 + k`, so every non-indexed CPU raster draw rendered the element-zero prefix. Classification was measured, not assumed, on a 21-vertex fixture of seven single-triangle slots with decoy geometry on both sides: `vertexStart` 0 with `primitiveCount` 1/3/7 lit exactly slots 0 / 0,1,2 / 0–6, while `(9,3)`, `(6,3)`, `(18,1)` and `(3,5)` — expecting slots 3,4,5 / 2,3,4 / 6 / 1,2,3,4,5 — lit 0,1,2 / 0,1,2 / 0 / 0,1,2,3,4. `primitiveCount` was therefore already correct on its own (the consumed cardinality is exact in every case) and only the offset was lost; the suffix beyond the count never rendered. The same element-zero prefix appeared under CullNone/CullClockwise, depth test and write enabled, AlphaBlend, an explicitly restated full Viewport and Scissor and a render-target round trip, and both `DrawUserPrimitives` forms already passed, so neither the rasterizer, the render state nor the readback could account for it. The fetch is now `vertexStart + local` for `local` in `[0, PrimitiveElementCount(primitive, primitiveCount))`, with `vertexStart` a vertex-**element** offset and the stride multiply performed only after the element range is validated. Both non-indexed CPU paths share one `ValidateNonIndexedAddressing` — the counterpart of REMED-GFX-110's indexed helper — which rejects a negative `vertexStart`, a topology-count overflow and any range leaving the bound buffer, in 64-bit and before any storage access, throwing `System::ArgumentOutOfRangeException` instead of the old wrappable `primitiveCount * 3 > vb.GetVertexCount()` check; ranges are rejected, never clamped. `DrawColoredPrimitives` keeps addressing from element zero (it carries no `GpuDrawParams`, and its only caller has already copied exactly the requested source range), as do the five typed `DrawUserPrimitives` overloads, so no path applies the offset twice. Software's documented TriangleList-only v1 boundary is unchanged and the other four topologies keep their explicit rejection at both zero and nonzero offsets. No vertex copying, per-draw allocation, buffer recreation, extra frame or synchronization: a valid draw adds one 64-bit range check and one addition per fetch. Software non-indexed range suite **6/15 → 17/17**, including the 20-, 24- and 32-byte layouts requesting the identical element range (a hardcoded stride passes every 16-byte test and fails all three), a static+dynamic two-buffer A→B→A, and valid→invalid→valid reuse; indexed suite **33/34** with its one documented TriangleStrip skip; `ctest -L Software` **16/16**; full Software `CnaTests` **5663 passed of 5708** with three A/B-proven pre-existing failures. ASan and UBSan over the range, indexed, declaration, empty-buffer, depth, DrawUser, model and point suites **358/359** each, zero AddressSanitizer reports and zero runtime-error lines. Cross-backend controls unchanged: Bgfx OpenGL **17/17** and Bgfx Vulkan **17/17**, EasyGL **13/13**, WebGPU **13/13**, Vulkan **11/11**, D3D9 **11/11**, D3D11 **11/11**, SDL_GPU **1/1**, Headless **1/1**. All fourteen backend libraries compile incrementally; no shader or generated artifact changed. | `REMED-GFX-121` | **Bgfx's non-GLSL renderers transpose the per-instance world matrix — OPEN.** `vs_instanced3d.sc` builds it with the raw `mat4(i_data0, i_data1, i_data2, i_data3)` constructor. bgfx maps `mat4()` to GLSL's **column** constructor on the GLSL profile and to HLSL's `float4x4` **row** constructor on SPIR-V/HLSL/Metal/WGSL — which is exactly why bgfx supplies `mtxFromCols()` and uses it in its own 05-instancing example. On the Vulkan route the matrix is therefore transposed, a pure translation lands in `w`, and instance 0 (identity) renders correctly while every later instance is projected somewhere unpredictable (measured: geometry clipped off the top of the frame and stretched a full slot to the right). Proven pre-existing by A/B against REMED-GFX-118's fix. Closing it needs `mtxFromCols()` in `vs_instanced3d.sc` and regeneration of `bgfx_shaders.hpp` across all profiles — deliberately out of REMED-GFX-118's geometry-range scope. By the same rule it affects the Bgfx D3D11/D3D12/Metal renderers, which are not runnable here. Pinned by `InstancedDrawRangeTest.BgfxPerInstanceWorldMatrixIsAppliedOnGlslRenderersOnly`, which asserts the current per-renderer behaviour so the follow-up fix is a deliberate flip. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | OPEN |

The correction carries element offsets and frequency through `GpuDrawParams`, converts only at GL attribute/index-pointer construction, binds declaration-accurate integer/normalized attributes and exact divisors, uses the native element index offset and applies base vertex once, gives all stock EasyGL 3D programs an optional four-column instance transform at reserved locations 12-15, and restores VAO attributes/divisors plus the ordinary-draw gate after submission. The custom `ShaderEffect` route uses its declaration-contiguous locations. The permanent suite now runs on EasyGL and passes **17/17**, including zero/nonzero vertex offset, `startIndex`, `baseVertex`, 16-/32-bit indices, counts 1/2/3/4, instance offsets 0/1, frequencies 1/2, static/dynamic buffers, A→B→A, instanced→ordinary→same-layout return, backbuffer/`RenderTarget2D`, source updates, disposal and teardown. The custom public route returns exact asymmetric white/gray instance pixels; relevant public range/buffer tests pass **89/89**, stock-effect controls **7/7**, the serial EasyGL shard **21/21**, OpenGL diagnostics are clean, combined ASan/UBSan is clean with external Mesa leak scanning disabled, and Vulkan/SDL_GPU/Bgfx/WebGPU/Software/Headless controls pass. | `REMED-GFX-123` | **D3D11 and D3D12 instanced draws dropped every public offset — DONE (2026-08-02).** The recorded scope (`DrawIndexedInstanced(indexCount, instanceCount, 0, 0, 0)` discarding `StartIndexLocation`/`BaseVertexLocation`) was real on both backends, and classifying them independently found the two are **not** one root cause: the shared symptom sat on a different mechanism per backend, and each backend dropped three further offsets the ticket did not name. **D3D11** additionally hardcoded the `IASetVertexBuffers` offsets array to `{0, 0}` (both `VertexBufferBinding.VertexOffset` and the per-instance binding's own offset), hardcoded the per-instance stride to 64 instead of reading the bound stream's, and cached **one** `ID3D11InputLayout` with `InstanceDataStepRate` baked in as 1, so `InstanceFrequency` was ignored and a later frequency reused the earlier layout. **D3D12** has no offset field on a `D3D12_VERTEX_BUFFER_VIEW` at all — it bound two whole-buffer views, so the same two element offsets had nowhere to go — and baked the step rate into a **single** cached PSO. Both now convert element offsets to bytes with each stream's own stride exactly once at the native binding (a D3D11 byte offset; a D3D12 `BufferLocation` advance with `SizeInBytes` shrunk to match), pass `startIndex`/`baseVertex` to the native draw exactly as REMED-GFX-020 established for the ordinary indexed path, and key the layout/PSO cache on the step rate so an alternating frequency neither reuses a stale rate nor builds a native object per draw. `StartInstanceLocation` stays 0 on both — the instance stream's start is already in its binding, and adding it twice would double the public offset. D3D11 measured under Wine: **3/17 → 17/17**, with the pre-fix cell map separating the mechanisms by hand — a `startIndex`-only request lit the element-zero prefix (7405 stray pixels in the excluded columns), while the binding oracle lit slot 6 (the mesh prefix decoy) in **all four** bands at once, naming the ignored per-vertex offset, the ignored instance offset and the step rate of 1 in one picture. The runtime route was Wine's builtin D3D11, **not DXVK**: DXVK 2.6.0's `dxgi` still breaks SDL video init under Xvfb and `:0` was forbidden, A/B-proven with the untouched `cna_test_d3d11_smoke.exe` failing identically and SDL initialising cleanly the moment `dxgi` is Wine builtin. D3D12's windowed runtime stays blocked by REMED-BUILD-012 — **A/B-proven identical** (same abort, same exit 5) with this change reverted and rebuilt — so D3D12 is corrected by inspection plus a clean cross-build. Regressions: D3D11 range/declaration/buffer/validation gates **139/139**, full D3D11 `CnaTests` **5610 passed** with 14 failures that no `DrawInstancedPrimitives` call can reach (only two files in the tree call it). Controls: EasyGL **157/157**, Bgfx **176/176**, Vulkan **142/142**, SDL_GPU **102/102**, WebGPU 164/165, Software 129/131, Headless 99/100 (all three residual failures are the documented `SetRenderTargets_FourTargets` MRT gap), D3D9 **16/16** on genuine DXVK, EasyGL ASan **17/17** and Vulkan UBSan **16/16** with zero reports. REMED-GFX-122's EasyGL pin keeps its name and now shares its body as the oracle both routes assert. Spawned independent findings REMED-GFX-199 and REMED-GFX-200. Signed fix `bf92691e`. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-124` | **Software render targets could be neither read back nor sampled — DONE (2026-07-27).** Two independent defects sharing one architectural cause: the target's colour storage was reachable through no interface any colour CONSUMER used. **(1) Readback.** `SoftwareRenderTargetBackend` overrode no `ITextureBackend::GetData`, so the call reached the interface's default no-op. The recorded symptom ("silently leaves the caller's buffer untouched") was measured and is **wrong, in the worse direction**: `Texture2D::GetData`'s render-target fallback hands the backend a scratch `std::vector<uint8_t>` it **zero-initialized itself** and then converts those bytes into the caller's `Color` array, so a no-op backend returns a **fabricated, fully written, uniformly transparent-black frame**. The sentinel assertion "GetData overwrote my 0xCD destination" therefore PASSES pre-fix (0 of 512 sentinel pixels survived) — only the content assertions catch it, and any test whose expected frame is transparent black passes on an empty target. **(2) Sampling.** The same object is not a `SoftwareTextureBackend`, so both sampler entry points (`RasterizeTriangleShaded` and `SoftwareSpriteBatchBackend::Draw`) `dynamic_cast`ed it to null and shaded untextured — every probe read `(255,255,255,255)`. The cast is well-defined, so neither facet was UB. **Fix (architecture option B of four evaluated).** One shared read-only colour-storage capability, `SoftwareColorSurface` (`ColorWidth`/`ColorHeight`/`ColorPixels`), implemented by `SoftwareTextureBackend` (its `pixels_`) and `SoftwareRenderTargetBackend` (its framebuffer's `color`); the samplers and `SampleBilinear` bind to that capability instead of a concrete class, and the target gains a real `GetData` override reading the same buffer. Option A (deriving the target from `SoftwareTextureBackend`) was rejected — it adds a second `pixels_` nothing writes, exactly the duplicate unsynchronised colour buffer to avoid; option C (an explicit resolved texture view) was rejected — Software is single-sample and Color-only, so the framebuffer colour IS the sampleable representation. **One authoritative buffer per resource**: no resolve, no shadow copy, no copy on unbind, no copy per sample, no per-draw allocation, no extra frame; a `GetData` costs exactly the caller's own rectangle copy. The capability is exposed as a `const` reference, so holding it cannot bypass active-target validation. `GetData` validates null destination, negative and above-zero mip level (Software stores level 0 only), empty/negative rectangles, a rectangle leaving the target and an undersized destination — 64-bit throughout, throwing `System::ArgumentNullException` / `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` / `NotSupportedException` rather than leaving caller memory unchanged, and reading the colour attachment only (the depth buffer is never consulted; Software has no stencil storage at all). Rows are top-first, matching the framebuffer and `ReadBackbuffer`, so no flip. **Boundaries established, not assumed:** formats — only `SurfaceFormat::Color` exists framework-wide and any other is rejected at construction by `Texture::ValidateFormat`; `RenderTargetUsage` — `PreserveContents` survives unbind/rebind, `DiscardContents` is cleared to opaque black on rebind as its contract permits; multisampling — single-sample storage with nothing to resolve, so `GetData`/sampling are already final. **Verification:** new `Software_RenderTargetReadback` **88/88**, A/B **32/88** against the pre-fix backend by narrow source restoration, so 56 checks are genuinely fix-sensitive. Sampler behaviour is asserted as **parity with a plain `Texture2D` built from the expected pattern alone** across PointClamp 1:1 and magnified, LinearClamp, PointWrap with an out-of-texture source rectangle, LinearWrap over a sub-rectangle, both flips and an offset destination — proving the ordinary sampler is used without hard-coding this backend's documented always-bilinear/clamp-only simplifications. `ctest -L Software` **17/17**; full Software `CnaTests` **5687 passed of 5732** with three A/B-proven pre-existing failures. ASan over 11 Software suites and UBSan over 8: zero AddressSanitizer reports, zero runtime-error lines. Cross-backend controls unchanged: EasyGL, Vulkan, Bgfx, WebGPU, D3D9 (Wine) and D3D11 (Wine+DXVK) **1/1** each, SDL_GPU **61/61**, Headless **32/33** (same pre-existing MRT failure). All fourteen backend libraries compile incrementally; no shader or generated artifact changed. REMED-GFX-119's Software carve-out in `NonIndexedRangeHoldsOnRenderTargetAndBackbuffer` is removed — that assertion now runs on every backend. Spawned independent findings REMED-GFX-126, REMED-GFX-127 and REMED-GFX-128. test `4799e2df`, fix `fb9b429c`, coverage `a2ace39d`. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-125` | **The XNA vertex structs' static `VertexDeclaration` reported the C++ object size, not the GPU stride — DONE (2026-07-27).** All seven built-in types built `getVertexDeclarationStatic()` with `sizeof(T)`, but none of them is a blittable value type here: `Color` inherits a polymorphic `IPackedVector` base and the multi-element types inherit a polymorphic `IVertexType`, so every object carries a vtable pointer and alignment padding, while every element offset in the same declarations was already a packed stream offset — the declarations described the elements of one layout with the stride of another. Measured `sizeof` → declared → corrected stride: `VertexPositionColor` 40/40/**16**, `VertexPositionTexture` 32/32/**20**, `VertexPositionColorTexture` 56/56/**24**, `VertexPositionNormalTexture` 40/40/**32**, `VertexPositionNormalTextureSkinned` 64/64/**52**, `VertexPositionNormalTangentTexture` 56/56/**48**, `VertexPositionNormalTangentTextureSkinned` 80/80/**68**. New `CNA/Internal/Graphics/BuiltInVertexStreams.hpp` holds one packed structure per type — the stream itself, with `static_assert`s on its size and every member offset — and each declaration now takes its stride from `sizeof(stream)` and every element offset from `offsetof(stream, member)`, so the two can no longer diverge; `VertexBuffer`'s five typed packers and `GraphicsDevice`'s `DrawUser` packers use the same structures and one `Pack()` overload per type is the only object-to-stream conversion in the tree. Correcting the stride alone would have been a half-fix: the raw `const void*` `DrawUser` overloads consume caller bytes at the declared stride and an array of built-in objects is not a stream at any stride, so those overloads keep their unchanged caller-packed contract and eight new typed overloads (`DrawUserPrimitives` and `DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` with 16- and 32-bit indices, for `VertexPositionColor`, `VertexPositionTexture`, `VertexPositionColorTexture` and `VertexPositionNormalTexture`) convert the source range exactly once and hand the stream to the raw overload — the vertex offset applied in objects and never again in bytes, the index offset left in indices. The explicit-declaration paths also gained the validation they never had: an empty declaration, a non-positive stride or an element reaching past its own stride throws `System::ArgumentException`, and a negative `vertexOffset`/`numVertices`/`indexOffset` or a source byte range that would not fit in an `Int32` throws `System::ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, computed in 64 bits before any byte is read — a `vertexOffset` of `0x7FFFFFF0` previously formed an out-of-bounds pointer and dumped core. `VertexBuffer::SetData` was proven unaffected (it repacks and uploads its own stride), and its raw `SetDataRaw` path now accepts a hand-packed stream carrying any built-in type's own declaration — previously impossible for all seven. Pre-fix **5 passed / 14 failed / 1 crashed**; post-fix **24/24** on Software, EasyGL, Vulkan, WebGPU, bgfx (OpenGL and Vulkan renderers), D3D9 and D3D11, **14/14** SDL_GPU and **13/13** Headless. ASan and UBSan over the vertex, declaration and DrawUser suites **314/315** each with zero reports. Source impact additive: no public field, element order, format, usage, constructor or inheritance changed and no type's `sizeof` changed. The two tangent types have no `Pack()` and no typed overload — an explicit capability boundary. test `47339344`, fix `7403c96c`, coverage `48c8fe2e`. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-126` | **Software reports a `MultiSampleCount` it never implements — OPEN.** `SoftwareRenderTargetBackend::GetMultiSampleCount()` returns the constructor's `ClosestMSAAPower(preferredMultiSampleCount)` verbatim, so a target requested with 4 samples publicly reports `RenderTarget2D.MultiSampleCount == 4` while the rasterizer writes exactly one sample per pixel and stores no per-sample data. That contradicts `IRenderTargetBackend::GetMultiSampleCount`'s own documented contract ("the actual device-clamped count; 0 if none/not supported by this backend") and FNA's `FNA3D_GetMaxMultiSampleCount` semantics, which SDL_Renderer's sibling `HasRealDepthBuffer` override already follows for depth. Readback and sampling are unaffected — there is nothing to resolve — so this is a reporting defect, not a rendering one, and is independent of REMED-GFX-124's readback/sampling causes. Observed while establishing GFX-124's multisample boundary; the value is pinned by `Software_RenderTargetReadback` check H3 so it cannot be silently blessed. | LOW | GRAPHICS | OPEN |
| `REMED-GFX-127` | **`Texture2D::GetData` fabricated a fully written transparent-black frame from a no-op backend — DONE (2026-07-27).** The shared render-target fallback allocated `std::vector<uint8_t> pixels(total * 4, 0)`, handed it to `ITextureBackend::GetData` — whose interface default did nothing — and converted those bytes into the caller's `Color` array regardless, so a backend with no readback returned a complete, uniformly transparent-black frame instead of an empty or rejected one. Reproduced on **seven** backends at 25/33 with `sentinelSurvivors=0/128`, `fabricated(0,0,0,0)=128`, `exact=0` and no exception: SDL_RENDERER, BGFX, ASCII, DX3, HEADLESS, D3D9 (Wine+DXVK) and D3D11 (Wine+DXVK). The sentinel oracle passes on every one of them, which is why the reproduction records that fact (checks Z1/Z2) rather than merely avoiding it. **Design (option B of five evaluated):** `ITextureBackend::GetData` returns `bool` — true only when the COMPLETE requested region was written — with a `[[nodiscard]]` default of `false`; the public layer converts only on true and otherwise raises `System::NotSupportedException` with the destination byte-for-byte untouched. Making the method pure virtual (option D) was rejected: 13 of the 26 concrete implementations are plain-texture backends the public path never reaches, so it would have forced 13 dead overrides; a capability query (option C) was rejected as a second source of truth a backend could forget to keep in sync. **Real readback implemented on every backend that can reasonably perform one**, each using that backend's own established mechanism: SDL_Renderer (and ASCII, whose targets are its textures) `SDL_SetRenderTarget`+`SDL_RenderReadPixels` with the caller's target restored; Bgfx Task 914's blit-to-`BLIT_DST|READ_BACK`+`readTexture` on the reserved highest view id so bgfx's ascending-view-id order runs it after this frame's target and segment views, with REMED-GFX-067's `originBottomLeft` V-flip applied to the readback too; DX3 `IDirectDrawSurface::Lock`; D3D9 `GetRenderTargetData`+`LockRect`; D3D11 `CopySubresourceRegion` into a `USAGE_STAGING` texture + `Map`; D3D12 DX-144's READBACK-heap `CopyTextureRegion` + fence wait + `Map`; Canvas `getImageData` on that target's own off-screen canvas. Every staging resource is created and released inside the call, MSAA targets read the resolved single-sample copy, rows are top-first and channels R,G,B,A everywhere, and no extra frame or `Present` is added except bgfx's, whose only readback path is frame-deferred (the same cost `ReadBackbuffer` already pays there). **Headless states its refusal explicitly** — it performs no rasterization, so a target's colour content does not exist there and returning its zeroed `pixels_` would have been the same fabrication in backend clothing. **Two live fabrication sites inside backends that DID override GetData were also closed:** WebGPU's Texture2D and RenderTarget2D readbacks `memset` the caller's destination to zero and returned as if successful whenever the buffer could not be mapped or was too small. New shared `texture2d_getdata_contract_test.cpp` on all fourteen backends: **39/39** on SOFTWARE, EASYGL, VULKAN, WEBGPU, SDL_GPU, BGFX (OpenGL **and** Vulkan), SDL_RENDERER, ASCII, DX3, D3D9 and D3D11; **34/34** on HEADLESS's deterministic-rejection contract; D3D12 verified by five new checks inside the off-screen `d3d12_smoke_test` (**247/247**) against that file's independent readback oracle; Canvas compile-verified on the Emscripten toolchain. ASan+UBSan over Software/Vulkan/Bgfx/SdlGpu clean. `ctest`: Software 18/18, Ascii 7/7, D3D9 28/28, D3D11 18/18, D3D12 1/1, Vulkan 162/163, Headless 7/8 — the two misses A/B-proven pre-existing. GFX-074's recorded backend list is superseded (EasyGL had gained an override since). Four independent findings spawned: **GFX-129**, **GFX-130**, **GFX-131**, **GFX-132**. test `e6ceec10`, fix `9b85fcf6`, coverage `cfce6d10` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-128` | **`Texture2D::GetData`'s two overloads give `startIndex` opposite meanings — DONE (2026-07-30), as part of REMED-GFX-149: the same expression, the same defect, the same fix.** In `GetData(Color*, int startIndex, int elementCount)` the value indexed the SOURCE texture (`src = (startIndex + i) * 4`, destination written from `data[0]`); in `GetData(int level, const Rectangle*, Color*, int startIndex, int elementCount)` it indexed the DESTINATION (`dst = startIndex + row * w + col`). XNA/FNA define it as the destination array offset in both. REMED-GFX-149 was raised two days later, during REMED-GFX-147, without noticing that this row already recorded the identical expression including the `startIndex == 0 && elementCount == total` gate GFX-149 headlines — one defect recorded twice. They could not be closed independently: accepting a non-zero `startIndex` on a render target while leaving it a SOURCE offset on a plain texture would have produced a worse asymmetry than the one being removed, so the unification this row asks for IS the GFX-149 fix. Of the checks that pinned the divergence, D7/D8 (rectangle overload) were already correct and are unchanged, and E10 — which asserted the defect — now asserts the contract. Full record, evidence and cross-backend results in the REMED-GFX-149 row. | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-129` | **Vulkan delivered every `GraphicsDevice.Clear()` through the render-pass load action, so a clear was not an ordered command at all — DONE (2026-07-28).** `GetOrCreateRTRenderPass(depthFormat, discardContents)` chose `LOAD_OP_CLEAR` or `LOAD_OP_LOAD` from the target's `RenderTargetUsage`, `PendingClear` carried the values and `RecordCommandBuffer` handed them to `pClearValues`; there was no `vkCmdClearAttachments`, `vkCmdClearColorImage` or `vkCmdClearDepthStencilImage` call anywhere in the backend — measured, 0 of each. A pass has exactly one load action and it necessarily runs before every draw it contains, and **five distinct public failures follow from that one fact**: a `Clear()` on a `PreserveContents` target was dropped entirely (its pass uses `LOAD_OP_LOAD`, there is no value to hand a clear to); a `Clear()` issued AFTER a draw ran before it; two `Clear()`s in one bind cycle collapsed, because `NoteRenderTargetClearEXT` merged them *for that reason* — the data structure encoded the limitation; `ClearDepth` recorded nothing at all (`/* Vulkan depth-only clear not yet implemented */`), so a depth-only `Clear()` did literally nothing; and a depth- or stencil-only clear could not be expressed even in principle, since taking the load action means also taking the colour one the usage had chosen. **`ClearDepth` is variant 4 of the same missing mechanism on the same implementation path, not a separate finding.** Distinct from REMED-GFX-140 (pass collapsing), REMED-GFX-143 (backbuffer interleaving) and REMED-GFX-136 (usage semantics), all of which stay intact. **The contract this establishes**, taken from CNA's own cross-backend behaviour rather than one backend: `Clear()` is an ordered command at its exact public call position; `RenderTargetUsage` governs what happens when a target is BOUND and never whether `Clear()` works. `clearAfterDrawWins` was already declared **true** on ten backends before this task and false only on the three load-op-only ones. **Fix:** one monotonic `commandOrder_` stamps every deferred entry — `PendingBatch::order` at SpriteBatch `End()`, `Pending3DDraw::order` in `PushPending3DDraw`, `PendingClear::order` in `NoteRenderTargetClearEXT`, which no longer merges; `PassSegment` carries its ordered clear list and its earliest draw position, and each segment is replayed as `draws(cursor, clear.order) -> clear -> draws(clear.order, next)` with a half-open order range added to `drawSpritesFor`/`draw3DFor` (the shared arena cursors are untouched, slices are disjoint and cover the segment, so REMED-GFX-140's arena fix is not weakened — `P6`/`P7` stay green). `RecordOrderedClearEXT` emits one `VkClearAttachment` per bound colour attachment for `ClearOptions::Target` — the only place an MRT set's other attachments are ever written — plus one combined depth/stencil entry whose aspect mask is **intersected with what the format actually owns**, since asking for the stencil aspect of a `Depth24` target is invalid usage rather than a no-op. `vkCmdClearColorImage` was deliberately rejected: it cannot be recorded inside a render pass, so it would mean a pass split and a layout transition per `Clear()`. `draw3DFor`'s open occlusion query is hoisted to segment scope so a query cannot be ended and re-begun on the same pool index across a clear. **Load-op folding is kept but narrowed to what is provably identical**: only a clear that is the segment's first AND precedes every draw AND whose pass clears its colour anyway (`VulkanRTSource::ColorLoadOpIsClearEXT()`, false exactly for a non-MSAA `PreserveContents` target (**REMED-GFX-141**: false for a MULTISAMPLED preserving cube face too, once its render pass gained a real `LOAD_OP_LOAD` variant — the predicate is now `!preserveContents` on both legs and the ordered-clear rule is identical for a multisampled and a single-sample face)). Kept because the shared layer clears on EVERY `DiscardContents` bind — measurable that it still works: the pass-boundary battery's ~40 discarding cycles record exactly **4** clear commands, its four explicit `Clear()` calls. **Red-first, all A/B against pre-fix production with the FINAL test files:** new shared `examples/graphicsdevice_ordered_clear_test.cpp` **3/34 → 34/34** (the three pre-fix passes are controls), REMED-GFX-018's backend-agnostic `ClearOptions` suite, registered on Vulkan for the first time, **124/232 → 232/232**, and GFX-140's `X1`/`X2`/`X3` **41/44 → 44/44**. Every check queues its whole public sequence and reads once at the end — no `GetData`, `Present`, flush, fence wait, sleep or extra frame between two commands — and depth/stencil are proven by geometry the stored depth must reject or accept and geometry the stored stencil must gate, never by a readback and never by absence of validation messages. **Clear rectangle is a real cross-backend divergence, measured not inferred:** Software, Bgfx, WebGPU, SdlGpu, Vulkan and D3D11 clear the whole target; EasyGL's `glClear` is narrowed by `GL_SCISSOR_TEST`; D3D9's is narrowed by BOTH the viewport and the scissor, which is real XNA's behaviour since XNA ran on D3D9. Vulkan takes REMED-GFX-018's full-target contract — byte-for-byte what the load action it replaces did — and the two divergences are **declared per backend and recorded, not standardised away**. **Cardinality/performance, LD_PRELOAD counter of the real entry points, identical test files either side:** `vkCmdClearAttachments` **0 → 111 / 0 → 59 / 0 → 4** while `vkCmdBeginRenderPass`(=`End`) 46/178/85, `vkCreateRenderPass` 7/6/6, `vkCreateFramebuffer` 54/17/71, `vkCreateImageView` 105/30/121, `vkAllocateCommandBuffers` 166/9/214, `vkQueueSubmit` 168/132/218, `vkQueuePresentKHR` and `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` 3/124/5, `vkDeviceWaitIdle` 62/168/37, `vkQueueWaitIdle` 165/8/213, `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` 8/12/4 and `vkWaitForFences` 4/161/7 are all **unchanged**, and `vkCmdClearColorImage`/`vkCmdClearDepthStencilImage` stay at 0 — not one extra pass, permanent object, pipeline variant, command buffer, submit, present, acquire or wait per `Clear()`. Standard validation **clean** with `VK_LOADER_DEBUG=layer` proving the layer loaded; **synchronization validation zero hazards** over a new phase of `vulkan_swapchain_sync_test.cpp` driving every clear route in one command buffer (preserving target, clear-after-draw, several clears per cycle, depth-only and stencil-only on `Depth24Stencil8`, MRT, single-layer cube face, backbuffer-after-geometry) — **25/25 → 28/28**, REMED-GFX-144's acquire hazard still 0. **Four false-positive-capable files found and strengthened:** `bgfx_graphicsdevice_clearoptions_test.cpp`'s `ClipDepthForFramebufferDepth` read `CNA_BGFX_RENDERER` unconditionally — a variable only the two Bgfx routes set — so elsewhere it applied the OpenGL clip conversion regardless, putting both depth probes on the same side of the cleared value on Vulkan (bgfx A/B byte-identical, 232/232 and 217/232 either side); `backbuffer_pass_order_test.cpp`'s `C4`/`C6` declared this defect as Vulkan's contract and went red the moment the fix landed (27/29 → 29/29); `easygl_graphicsdevice_clear_stencil_test.cpp` split stamp from clear+compare across frames and documented the split as unavoidable — *"even with this task's fix correctly applied"* — so a new same-frame check `C` was added, A/B **2/3 → 3/3**; and `graphicsdevice_clear_depth_test.cpp` exempted `ClearOptions::DepthBuffer` alone, which is exactly why nothing there ever reached the dead `ClearDepth`. **Cross-backend controls, only Vulkan production changed:** VULKAN / EASYGL / BGFX / D3D9 / D3D11 **34/34**, SDL_GPU 31/31, WEBGPU 29/29, SOFTWARE 28/28, HEADLESS 25/25. Gates: GFX-140 44/44, GFX-143 29/29, GFX-144 28/28, GFX-018 232/232, GFX-136, GFX-134, GFX-093/095/096/112; `ctest -R '^Vulkan'` **171/172**, the miss `Vulkan_DepthBias` re-measured at its recorded 3/4 with the same failing check. ASan and UBSan clean over all five clear fixtures with both runtimes proved linked. All fourteen backend libraries build. Recorded separately and not fixed: the EasyGL/D3D9 clear-rectangle divergence, and `Bgfx_GraphicsDevice_ClearOptions_Vulkan` sitting at 217/232 (A/B-proven unchanged by this task, and bgfx does not link CNA's Vulkan backend at all). test `9f791921`, fix `c11f937a`, coverage `71b6fdef` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-130` | **`TextureCube`/`Texture3D` readback fabricated the content REMED-GFX-127 removed from `Texture2D` — DONE (2026-07-27).** `ITextureCubeBackend::GetData` and `ITexture3DBackend::GetData` kept the `void` no-op default that finding replaced on `ITextureBackend`, and `TextureCube::GetData`/`Texture3D::GetData` converted their own zero-initialized scratch buffer for the caller unconditionally — the identical five-step sequence, on two separate interfaces and two separate public classes. Reproduced red on **five** backends: HEADLESS 17/33 with `fabricated=64/64`, `sentinelSurvivors=0`, no exception (its `GetData` actively `std::fill_n`'d the caller's destination with zeros while `SetData` stored nothing); SOFTWARE 31/33 (cube mip 1 `fabricated=16/16` — SOFTWARE-82 stores no cube mips); SDL_RENDERER and DX3 19/33 and ASCII 32/56, all silent no-ops through a null backend, ASCII on **both** the cube and a `Texture3D` that constructed with no storage. EASYGL, VULKAN, BGFX, SDL_GPU, WEBGPU, D3D9 and D3D11 were already exact at 54/56 — only the disposed-resource contract was missing. **Design:** both methods now return `[[nodiscard]] bool`, true only when the COMPLETE requested region was written, default `false`; the public layer converts only on true and otherwise raises `System::NotSupportedException` with the destination byte-for-byte untouched. The shared scratch buffer is sized to the **requested region** rather than to `elementCount` (a larger `elementCount` used to return the buffer's untouched tail as content), a null backend is refused one step earlier, and `GetData` on a disposed resource throws `System::ObjectDisposedException` per IndexBuffer/Effect's established pattern. Every existing readback keeps its own mechanism and only gains an honest result; **three live WebGPU `memset`-to-zero-and-succeed sites** (TextureCube, RenderTargetCube, Texture3D) are closed, as is EasyGL's per-slice half-read. **Headless refuses explicitly** and its six zero-filled cube-face buffers and voxel buffer are deleted — diagnostic storage no `SetData` ever wrote is never offered as content. RenderTargetCube readback is unchanged in capability and now honest in reporting: SdlGpu and WebGPU implement it, the other seven inherit the false default and reject (recorded in `IGraphicsBackend.hpp`, spawned as **GFX-134** — *closed 2026-07-27, which implemented it on all six that own a rendered cube resource*). New shared `texturecube_texture3d_getdata_contract_test.cpp` on all fourteen backends: **56/56** on EASYGL, VULKAN, BGFX, SDL_GPU, WEBGPU, D3D9 and D3D11; **33/33** on SOFTWARE, HEADLESS, SDL_RENDERER, ASCII and DX3 (cube-only where Texture3D is refused at construction); D3D12 via four new checks in `d3d12_smoke_test` (**251/251**); Canvas compile-verified. ASan+UBSan clean over Software/Vulkan/SdlGpu/Bgfx. `ctest`: Software 5751/5754, Vulkan 5905/5910, Bgfx 5905/5916, EasyGL, SdlGpu 39/40, WebGPU 33/34, Headless 8/9, Ascii 8/8 — every miss A/B-proven or already-recorded pre-existing. Two independent findings spawned: **GFX-134**, **GFX-135**. test `b7aaa924`, fix `f2576ed8`, coverage `bbe8d9c0` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-131` | **WebGPU render targets were sRGB, so mid-tone channel values did not survive a round trip — DONE (2026-07-28).** `ConfigureSurface` preferred `BGRA8UnormSrgb`/`RGBA8UnormSrgb` over their non-sRGB counterparts, and both `WebGPURenderTargetBackend` and `WebGPURenderTargetCubeBackend` copy that `surfaceFormat_` into their own colour attachment so every pipeline stays format-compatible — so the hardware applied a linear-to-sRGB encode on every render-pass store and `GetData` handed those encoded bytes to game code. **The contract is not ambiguous:** `SurfaceFormat::Color` is documented as a plain 8-bit-per-channel format and `SurfaceFormat::ColorSrgbEXT` is the separate gamma-encoded one; `D3DFormatMapping` already maps the two to `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` and `R8G8B8A8_UNORM_SRGB`, and `VulkanGraphicsBackend::CreateSwapchain` resolves this exact choice the other way with a comment saying *"An SRGB swapchain format would apply an automatic linear-to-sRGB encode to every presented pixel regardless of content, which XNA/FNA does not do by default. Prefer UNORM."* WebGPU was the sole outlier. **Ordinary textures were already correct** — every plain `WebGPUTextureBackend`/`WebGPUTextureCubeBackend`/`WebGPUTexture3DBackend` uses `RGBA8Unorm` — so on this one backend plain textures and render targets did not share colour semantics, which the new fixture asserts as its own fact (check A5). **Measured transfer function, not guessed:** new `colorspace_midtone_contract_test.cpp` prints a per-channel input/normalized/identity/srgb-encode/srgb-decode/actual table, and R, G and B independently matched one standard IEC 61966-2-1 linear-to-sRGB ENCODE — 1→13, 16→71, 32→99, 64→137, 96→165, 127→187, **128→188**, 192→225, 224→241, 254→255, with 0 and 255 exact fixed points and 129/160 within ±1 of the prediction (8-bit hardware rounding). Alpha was exact everywhere, confirming it stays linear in an sRGB format. **Fix:** `surfaceFormat_` is now CNA's `SurfaceFormat::Color` mapping and is always non-sRGB; the new `surfaceConfiguredFormat_` is what the surface itself is configured with. The preference order puts the UNORM formats first, matching Vulkan. If a surface offers no non-sRGB format at all, it is configured with the sRGB one but lists the non-sRGB counterpart in `viewFormats`, and the backbuffer view is created explicitly in `surfaceFormat_` — so the byte semantics hold even there, with no extra pipeline, pass, texture, bind group, submit, staging buffer or per-pixel conversion. Only the transfer function is dropped, never channel order, so the BGRA readback swizzle is untouched. On this adapter the selected format moved from `BGRA8UnormSrgb` (28) to `BGRA8Unorm` (27) with `viewFormats` unused, and the sprite pipeline cache still measures 8→8 across a 64-`BlendFactor` sweep. **Four fixtures asserted the ENCODED values and were corrected**, which is the false-positive half of this finding: `webgpu_spritebatch_blendstate_test`'s `SrgbByte()`/`LinearAlphaByte()` became one `UnormByte()`; `webgpu_basiceffect_preferperpixellighting_test`'s 187/203 became 127/152, which is **exactly what EasyGL, Vulkan and Bgfx already read for the same scene**; `webgpu_skinnedeffect_world_normal_test`'s `LinearToSrgb()` was deleted; and the shared `rendertargetcube_plural_binding_test` lost its WebGPU-only `CNA_GFX096_EXPECT_SRGB_ENCODED` fork. Two more fixtures had documented the blind spot in a comment rather than closing it. New suite: **WEBGPU 18/18** (including `V1`, `GetUncapturedErrorCountEXT() == 0`), **SOFTWARE / EASYGL / VULKAN / BGFX / SDL_GPU 17/17**, **HEADLESS 14/14** on its declared rejection contract — only WebGPU production changed. WebGPU shard 48/49 WebGPU-labelled tests, the miss A/B-proven pre-existing at an identical 7/10. ASan+UBSan clean over the suite plus the Texture2D/RenderTargetCube readback contracts, MSAA, blend state and RenderTarget2D. Two independent findings spawned: **GFX-147**, **GFX-148**. test `359e7ec9`, fix `9cd4eb0f`, coverage `b172ca2b` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-132` | **`cna_reference_dump` cannot link under the ASCII **or bgfx** backend — OPEN; scope corrected 2026-08-04 at exit reconciliation.** Measured then: the tool binary is present in 6 of 8 build directories and **absent in exactly `cmake-build-ascii` and `cmake-build-bgfx`**, the bgfx failure being `undefined reference to vtable for Microsoft::Xna::Framework::Graphics::VertexDeclaration`. Pre-existing, not a regression — `cmake/Examples.cmake` was last touched `b60bbab9` (2026-07-20), and the binary has never existed under bgfx. **Tool-only:** `CNA`, `cna_backend_graphics_bgfx` and `CnaTests` all build exit 0 under bgfx. Checkpoint blocker **NO** (`REMEDIATION_EXIT.md` §4.3.2). Original text follows. **`cna_reference_dump` cannot link under the ASCII backend — OPEN.** `cmake/Examples.cmake` links it with a plain `target_link_libraries(cna_reference_dump PRIVATE CNA)`, with no `-Wl,--start-group CNA ${BACKEND_TARGET} -Wl,--end-group` — the very circular-dependency workaround `cmake/CnaLibrary.cmake`'s own comment says this target needs. `libcna_backend_graphics_ascii.a`'s `BuildAsciiFontAtlas` references `SpriteFont::SpriteFont(...)` from `CNA`, which the linker has already passed, so `cmake --build cmake-build-ascii` fails at that one target while every backend library, every ASCII test and `CnaTests` build. Surfaced by enabling `CNA_BUILD_TESTS` in that build directory for REMED-GFX-127; a build-system defect, not a graphics one. | LOW | BUILD | OPEN |
| `REMED-GFX-133` | **`headless_smoke_test` Check F catches the wrong exception type and aborts the executable — OPEN.** It issues a deliberately out-of-range `DrawIndexedPrimitives(TriangleList, 0, 0, 3, 0, 10)` and catches `HeadlessValidationException`, but REMED-GFX-110's shared `ValidateIndexedAddressing` now rejects the range in the public layer with `System::ArgumentOutOfRangeException` before the Headless backend's own validation ever runs, so the exception escapes `Draw()` and `Headless_Smoke` terminates. A/B-proven pre-existing (the pre-REMED-GFX-127 sources abort identically). Either the test should expect the shared rejection or Headless's mode dial should still be the one that decides — a contract question, not a one-line test edit, so recorded rather than absorbed. | LOW | GRAPHICS | OPEN |
| `REMED-GFX-134` | **`RenderTargetCube` colour readback existed on only two of nine backends — DONE (2026-07-27).** REMED-GFX-130 made the reporting honest (`ITextureCubeBackend::GetData`'s default is `return false`, so the shared layer raises `System::NotSupportedException` with the caller's destination untouched) but implemented nothing, so EasyGL, Vulkan, Bgfx, D3D9, D3D11 and D3D12 refused a rendered cube face while each already owned the exact mechanism its plain-`TextureCube` sibling uses in the same file. Red-first shared `rendertargetcube_getdata_contract_test.cpp` on all fourteen backends: **EASYGL / VULKAN / BGFX / D3D11 20/54 and D3D9 21/54**, every rendered-face read `threwNotSupported=1 exact=0/64 sentinelSurvivors=64`. The content producer is drawn geometry, never `Clear()` (REMED-GFX-129 owns that variable), painting an asymmetric five-region pattern whose six colours rotate by face index, from a 0/255-only palette that is an exact fixed point of sRGB encoding — so every comparison in the file is byte-exact even on the sRGB WebGPU cube target. Row order is measured, not assumed: check **O1** renders the same pattern into a `RenderTarget2D` and **F8** requires the cube face to match it byte for byte, with **R4/R5/R6** pinning the final row, final column and lower-right corner. **Implemented:** EasyGL (temporary FBO + `glReadPixels` with the bottom-up correction its own 2D target already documents), Vulkan (`FlushDeferredRenderTarget` on the face proxy + per-layer `TRANSFER_SRC` copy + BGRA→RGBA), Bgfx (`ReadTextureRegionEXT`, now layer-aware, so the blit runs on the reserved highest view id, plus REMED-GFX-067's `originBottomLeft` flip), D3D9 (`GetCubeMapSurface` + `GetRenderTargetData` into SYSTEMMEM + `LockRect` — a `D3DPOOL_DEFAULT` render target cannot be locked), D3D11 (staging copy + `Map`) and D3D12 (READBACK heap + placed footprint + fence), the last two sourced from the RESOLVED single-sample resource. **Five further defects the oracle exposed, all fixed:** bgfx dropped the FIRST cube face bound in one un-advanced frame (one shared framebuffer handle destroyed per bind while the base view was re-pointed — now one framebuffer per face, base view left alone once it has recorded work); D3D11 and D3D12 never called a cube target's `UnbindAsRenderTarget`, so the per-face `ResolveSubresource` and mip regeneration living there were unreachable and a multisampled cube's resolve resource stayed empty; SdlGpu answered a mip level the target never allocated; WebGPU threw a raw `std::invalid_argument` through the public XNA surface for the same request; and WebGPU's `GetData` WIPED the face it was reading when that face was still bound. Boundaries stay refusals: bgfx's blit reports success while copying untouched memory for a multisampled cube attachment and for auto-regenerated cube mips on its OpenGL renderer, and D3D9 allocates one level whatever `mipMap` asked for. Final: **EASYGL 55/55, VULKAN 56/56, BGFX 55/55, SDL_GPU 55/55, WEBGPU 56/56, D3D9 55/55, D3D11 55/55, HEADLESS 55/55, SOFTWARE / SDL_RENDERER / ASCII / DX3 21/21**, CANVAS compile-verified, D3D12 via four new `d3d12_smoke_test` checks under vkd3d-proton. Two false-positive-capable reductions removed: EasyGL's `RenderTargetCube` SetData entry moved from "did not throw" to byte-exact, and bgfx's cube-binding regression from the staged single face to the full **14/14** battery. ASan+UBSan clean over Vulkan/SdlGpu/Bgfx/Software. Shards: Bgfx 128/134, EasyGL 250/253, Vulkan 165/166, SdlGpu 70/71, WebGPU 35/36, Software 21/21, Headless 11/12, Ascii 10/10, SDL_Renderer 68/72, DX3 12/14, D3D9 31/31, D3D11 21/21, D3D12 1/1 — every miss already recorded. Spawned **GFX-136**, **GFX-137**, **GFX-138**, **GFX-139**. test `560089e7`, fix `b6392613`, coverage `69129c54` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-135` | **The cube/volume WRITE side had no completion contract — DONE (2026-07-27).** REMED-GFX-127/130 gave `TextureCube::GetData`/`Texture3D::GetData` exactly two outcomes; their `SetData` counterparts kept the pre-finding shape — a `void` backend method called behind `if (backend_)` — so an upload that stored nothing, or only part of the requested region, still returned normally. Reproduced red on every route the finding names: **HEADLESS 18/32** with `returned=1` while `readBack=0 readNotSupported=1` (the exact reachable state the finding was raised for — the caller is told the face cannot be read and never learns the write went nowhere either), **SDL_RENDERER 18/32** the same one step earlier through `CreateTextureCube`'s nullptr default, **SOFTWARE 30/34** where cube mip 1 returned normally with `exact=0/16` because `SoftwareTextureCubeBackend` allocated level 0 only while `TextureCube` reported the full `LevelCount`, and RenderTargetCube accepted-and-dropped through `IRenderTargetCubeBackend::SetData`'s `{}` body; **EASYGL 52/56** already exact on every content check, so the file measures the defect rather than the harness. **Design:** both interface methods now return `[[nodiscard]] bool` with **no default body** (`void` was the defect — it left an implementation no way to say "I stored nothing"), true only for a COMPLETE region; the public layer validates every argument before converting or dispatching, refuses a null backend one step earlier, raises `System::NotSupportedException` on false and `System::ObjectDisposedException` after `Dispose()`, and converts exactly the requested region rather than `elementCount`. Software gained real **per-mip cube storage** (the GetData contract file's SOFTWARE row moves from Unsupported to Exact for cube mips), so a mipmapped cube `.xnb` now both loads and reads back completely there. Every concrete backend gained the range checks the write path never had; Vulkan no longer `memcpy`s into a null staging mapping, WebGPU's four `throw std::out_of_range`/`std::invalid_argument` sites became results so no raw std exception escapes the public API, SdlGpu no longer `memcpy`s from a null source or addresses a nonexistent subresource, and bgfx is handed a `bgfx::copy()` block sized to the region. Content loading follows the contract instead of working around it: `TextureCubeReader`, the `.cnj` source-file path and `DDSFromStreamEXT` now fail outright on a backend that cannot store a cube face. New shared `texturecube_texture3d_setdata_contract_test.cpp` on all fourteen backends: **56/56** on EASYGL, VULKAN, BGFX (OpenGL), SDL_GPU, WEBGPU, D3D9 and D3D11; **34/34** SOFTWARE; **32/32** HEADLESS, SDL_RENDERER, ASCII and DX3; D3D12 and Canvas compile-verified. GFX-130's readback suite stays green everywhere. ASan+UBSan clean over Software, Vulkan, SdlGpu and Bgfx. `ctest`: EasyGL 6000/6005, Vulkan 5909/5915, Bgfx 5905/5915, Software 5750/5759, Headless 5702/5707, SdlGpu 40/41, WebGPU 34/35, Ascii 9/9, DX3 11/13 — every miss recorded pre-existing or A/B-proven. test `9bc5243f`, fix `0b5c95c0`, coverage `bbb6dbc2` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-136` | **`CreateRenderTargetCube` could not carry `RenderTargetUsage`, so Vulkan and WebGPU discarded a cube target on every bind — DONE (2026-07-28).** `RenderTargetCube` stores a real `usage_` and reports it publicly, then throws it away at the one call that builds the GPU resource: `IGraphicsBackend::CreateRenderTargetCube(size, depthFormat, mipMap, multiSampleCount)` had no `preserveContents` parameter, unlike `CreateRenderTarget2D`. The two backends whose native API forces the question both invented "always discard", each with a source comment naming the missing parameter — `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend` built all six face framebuffers against `GetOrCreateRTRenderPass(depthFmt, /*discardContents=*/true)`, and `WebGPUGraphicsBackend::RenderPendingDrawsToRenderTargetCubeFace` hardcoded `loadOp = WGPULoadOp_Clear`. **Fix:** `preserveContents` added in `CreateRenderTarget2D`'s identical position across the interface and its **nine** overrides (EasyGL, Vulkan, Bgfx, SdlGpu, WebGPU, Headless, D3D9, D3D11, D3D12; the five backends with no cube target keep the `nullptr` default), fed by **one** shared mapping, `RenderTargetUsagePreservesContentsEXT()`, that both public targets call. Its rule is FNA's own `usage != DiscardContents`, which also resolves a second contradiction this finding uncovered: `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTargets` only ever cleared a `DiscardContents` target (so the shared layer treated `PlatformContents` as preserving) while `RenderTarget2D` passed `usage == PreserveContents` (so the backend treated it as discarding) — whichever half won was a per-backend accident. Vulkan now selects `GetOrCreateRTRenderPass(depthFmt, !preserveContents_)` per instance (colour `LOAD`/`STORE`, `initialLayout = SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL`) and takes its up-front layout barrier whenever the target preserves, not only when `levelCount_ > 1`; WebGPU uses the same `clearPending || !PreserveContents()` choice its 2D sibling already had. Cost is **at most one extra cached `VkRenderPass` per depth format** — no per-target, per-face or per-bind resource, no full-face copy, no pipeline key change. The other seven backends consume the flag by deliberately ignoring it, each with the reason in source: none has a load action that could discard a bound face. Red-first `examples/rendertargetcube_usage_test.cpp` on all fourteen backends, reusing REMED-GFX-134's asymmetric byte-exact face producer and rebinding to draw only a 2x2 marker, so "the marker landed" can never pass for preservation. Exact A/B with the same final test file: **VULKAN 16/30 → 30/30, WEBGPU 15/30 → 30/30**, every pre-fix failure reading `(0,0,0,255)` where the producer's colour was required. **Twelve of its own checks were false-positive-capable and were strengthened before the fix landed**: `VulkanGraphicsBackend::RecordCommandBuffer` records one render pass per render-target source per flush, so a producer pass and a later partial pass in the same flush window collapse into one — eleven preservation checks passed on the discarding backend until a readback barrier (`Settle()`) separated them, and `M2` (one multisampled face) passed on EasyGL until `M3` interleaved a second face. One further real defect fixed because it hung the suite: SdlGpu's multisampled cube pass combined `cycle=true` with `SDL_GPU_LOADOP_LOAD`, which SDL_gpu forbids outright. Final: **EASYGL / BGFX / SDL_GPU / VULKAN / WEBGPU / HEADLESS / D3D9 / D3D11 30/30**, **SOFTWARE / SDL_RENDERER / ASCII / DX3 4/4**, CANVAS compile-verified, D3D12 via two new `d3d12_smoke_test` checks under vkd3d-proton. GFX-134 **55/55** and GFX-096 `plural_binding` **14/14** stay green; Vulkan validation clean; ASan+UBSan clean over Vulkan/SdlGpu/Bgfx/Software. Spawned **GFX-140** (Vulkan collapses bind cycles into one pass per flush), **GFX-141** (one multisample attachment shared across six faces defeats multisampled preservation on EasyGL/Vulkan/SdlGpu; D3D11 honours it), **GFX-142** (depth/stencil is outside the usage contract and the three load-action backends disagree). **Reconciled 2026-07-28:** the `Settle()` barrier its eleven preservation checks need was made necessary by TWO backends collapsing bind cycles, not one — Vulkan (REMED-GFX-140) and SdlGpu (REMED-GFX-145).
