Both are now fixed, its suite is unchanged at **30/30** on both, and `examples/rendertarget_pass_boundary_test.cpp` is the file that enforces the boundary with no barrier anywhere. **Reconciled again 2026-07-28:** its MSAA row is now obsolete — **REMED-GFX-141** gave every cube face its own multisample colour storage on all three backends, so this file's `msaaPreserves` is **true** for EasyGL and SdlGpu and its `M2`/`M3` require exact content instead of recording a boundary (30/30 unchanged on EASYGL, SDL_GPU, VULKAN, WEBGPU, BGFX and HEADLESS). test `0123bf53`, fix `166ba659` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-137` | **EasyGL stores a RENDERED cube face and an UPLOADED one in opposite row orders — OPEN.** The rasterizer fills a cube face bottom-up (which is why `EasyGLRenderTargetCubeBackend::GetData` normalizes, REMED-GFX-134) while `glTexSubImage2D` writes source row 0 into texel row 0 like every other backend's upload. `GetData` is now correct for the rendered case, verified against a `RenderTarget2D` reference of the identical draw — which leaves the two writers of one face disagreeing, so a rendered face and a `SetData` face SAMPLE mirrored relative to each other, and a rendered EasyGL cube face samples mirrored relative to the D3D/Vulkan backends. REMED-GFX-134's check **W1** records the asymmetry as an enforced fact (`mirrored=64/64`). Flipping the upload would only move the divergence onto the sampling path; correcting it belongs with cube sampling/camera conventions. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | OPEN |
| `REMED-GFX-138` | **Bgfx now exposes the completed resolved and generated public image of a `RenderTargetCube` — DONE (2026-08-01).** **HISTORICAL CLAIM RE-TESTED, NOT ASSUMED:** on current HEAD the two named failures still reproduced, each returning black through its old backbuffer oracle; with the final focused fixture but the production refusals restored, only the mip/MSAA reads failed (**260/416**), leaving sentinel destinations untouched. Removing the refusals diagnostically showed that direct public reads were already exact for all six faces, every mip, MSAA level 0, and combined MSAA+mips, while the old named backbuffer tests remained black. That differential refuted the ledger's native-capability diagnosis. **ONE EXACT CURRENT MECHANISM:** two adjacent stale guards in `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend::GetData` (`multiSampleCount > 0` and `level > 0`) still encoded GFX-134's pre-GFX-154 measurements. GFX-154 had since made the existing `CompleteFrameForResolveEXT()` ordered completion authoritative; bgfx ends the producer framebuffer there, resolves MSAA, runs per-face `BGFX_RESOLVE_AUTO_GEN_MIPS`, then the existing readback blit sees the completed single-sample image. **FIX:** delete only those two obsolete refusals. No new frame, `Present`, retry, wait, touch, dummy bind, CPU copy, allocation, view, submission, or ordering path was added. RenderTarget2D, ordinary/mipmapped cubes, plain `TextureCube`, RenderTargetCube sampling, view order and producer-consumer order retain their existing mechanisms; SDL_GPU, Vulkan, EasyGL, WebGPU, Software and DirectX production are untouched. The two legacy named fixtures now perform one direct public result read and contain no backbuffer retry or per-face dummy read. New Bgfx-only `Bgfx_GFX138_RenderTargetCube_Finalize` performs two producer rounds in interleaved A/B/A face order with no intermediate public synchronization, then checks same-frame `EnvironmentMapEffect` consumers and guarded direct reads for four configurations (ordinary, mip, MSAA, combined); unique two-colour face checkers make flat, zero, stale, aliased and wrong-face content unable to pass: **260/416 before the fix -> 416/416 after**. Re-armed GetData/usage/MSAA-face contracts require real resolved data rather than a capability skip. **RESULTS:** named failures **2/2 green**; focused/public group **6/6**; serial relevant Bgfx shard **27/28**, with only the unchanged pre-existing `Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_DepthFormat` colour mismatch; GFX-155/157/158/163/181 ordering, cube, RT2D and MSAA/mip controls all green. Diagnostics (`CNA_BGFX_TRACE_DIAGNOSTICS`, view-order, readback and envmap traces) reported **416/416**, strictly ordered producer/consumer views, explicit environment-map bindings, and completed per-face/per-level reads; only the established RenderDoc-unavailable/capability-probe/shutdown-residual warnings appeared. GFX-188 cross-backend controls: SDL_GPU **5/5**, Vulkan **6/6**, EasyGL **5/5**. Linked ASan and UBSan runs are **6/6 each**, with zero address and zero undefined-behaviour reports; ASan leak checking alone was disabled for the already-recorded bgfx/driver residual. Stable `cmake-build-*` directories, ccache and incremental builds were reused; GPU tests were serial; compilation paused at the thermal ceiling, and the final UBSan refresh used the user's temporary `-j16` authorization only after all other Codex/build lanes were confirmed idle. No temporary tree; `audit/` untouched. **ONE NEW FINDING:** `REMED-GFX-195`, the separately observable Bgfx cross-face multisample `PreserveContents` defect; recorded OPEN and not remediated here. **Bgfx-on-Vulkan remains untested** — the active renderer was OpenGL 2.1. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-139` | **D3D9's `RenderTargetCube` reports a mip chain it never allocates — OPEN.** `D3D9RenderTargetCubeBackend::Recreate()` calls `CreateCubeTexture(size, /*Levels=*/1, D3DUSAGE_RENDERTARGET, ...)` whatever `mipMap` asked for, while `RenderTargetCube::LevelCount` reports the full chain (4 for an 8x8 target) because the public class computes it from `mipMap` alone. REMED-GFX-134 made `GetData` refuse the levels that were never allocated instead of answering them from level 0, so the resource is honest at the readback boundary — but the `LevelCount` claim itself is still wrong. Making it true needs D3D9 mip-chain generation, which `D3D9RenderTargets.hpp`'s own header note already records as deliberately unimplemented for the 2D case too. | LOW | GRAPHICS | OPEN |
| `REMED-GFX-140` | **Vulkan collapsed every bind cycle of one render target into a single render pass per flush — DONE (2026-07-28).** `VulkanGraphicsBackend::RecordCommandBuffer`'s Phase 1 collected the UNIQUE `VulkanRTSource`s referenced this flush and gave each ONE `vkCmdBeginRenderPass`/`vkCmdEndRenderPass` holding every queued sprite batch and 3D draw for it. Two public bind cycles of the same target therefore shared one load action: the second cycle's shared-layer `DiscardContents` clear never happened, an explicit `Clear()` was folded into the first, and its viewport and scissor applied inside a pass that had already loaded the first cycle's content. Collapsing is INVISIBLE on a `PreserveContents` target — one `LOAD` pass holding both cycles' draws yields exactly what two `LOAD` passes would — which is why REMED-GFX-136 needed an artificial readback barrier before eleven of its checks could see a discarding backend; the decisive shape is `DiscardContents`, whose second bind must throw the first bind's content away. **Fix:** segment, not target identity, is the grouping key. `currentSegment_` advances on every assignment of `currentRT_` (`BeginRenderPassSegmentEXT` replaces all nine of them, so bind, unbind and rebind each open a new logical pass even for the same resource, face, mip or MRT set); every deferred entry carries its segment (`Pending3DDraw::segment` tagged in the single choke point `PushPending3DDraw`, `PendingBatch::segment` captured at SpriteBatch `Begin()` next to `activeRT_`, and `clearedRTs_` becomes `pendingClears_`, a per-segment record that also CARRIES the clear values — previously every pass began with the frame-global `clearR_`/`clearDepth_`/`clearStencil_` as they stood at record time, so the frame's last `Clear()` supplied the colour for every earlier pass). Phase 1 builds one `PassSegment` per id, sorts by id — exactly the public order the cycles were opened in — and records each in its own native pass, so A → B → A is three passes, not two; an empty segment produces none. **A second defect had to be fixed with it:** `drawSpritesFor`/`draw3DFor` restarted their shared per-frame vertex/index arena cursor at 0 on every call, and since every `memcpy` happens while recording and the GPU reads only after submit, a later pass overwrote the exact bytes an earlier pass had bound — so the earlier pass drew the LATER pass's geometry. Live and unnoticed for multiple targets in one command buffer, and unavoidable once one target's two cycles are two passes over one arena; the cursors are hoisted to the whole record. It was invisible to every existing fixture because they draw identical rectangles and vary only the source texture, which lives in the per-draw descriptor set — new check `P6` differs in GEOMETRY and catches it. Red-first shared `examples/rendertarget_pass_boundary_test.cpp` on all fourteen backends, with **no flush, readback or `Present` between any two cycles** — the only readback in each check happens after every command of that check is queued. **VULKAN 26/42 → 42/42.** Under an LD_PRELOAD counter of the real entry points, with the identical final test file: `vkCmdBeginRenderPass` **47 → 83** (S1's two logical cycles 1 → 2, S8's three 1 → 3), begins == ends in both, and `vkCreateRenderPass` **6 → 6**, `vkCreateFramebuffer` **69 → 69**, `vkCreateImageView` **117 → 117**, `vkAllocateCommandBuffers` **206 → 206**, `vkQueueSubmit` **210 → 210** — the boundaries the contract requires and not one extra permanent object, command buffer, submit or device wait. **REMED-GFX-129 re-measured, NOT resolved and left open with a narrower root cause** (checks X1/X2/X3), **since closed 2026-07-28** — that task added the `vkCmdClearAttachments` path this one deliberately did not, and this task's oracle is unchanged at 44/44 across it: a `Clear()` on a `PreserveContents` target is dropped in a single cycle just as much as in a second one, because `GetOrCreateRTRenderPass` delivers the clear colour only through the pass load op and a preserving pass uses `LOAD_OP_LOAD` — there is no `vkCmdClearAttachments` path — which is also why a `Clear()` issued AFTER a draw in one cycle cannot wipe that draw. Segmentation was never going to reach it. Backbuffer work kept its single trailing pass here (its load action, MSAA resolve, deferred readback and `PRESENT_SRC_KHR` final layout belong to the acquired swapchain image); the interleaving of backbuffer work BETWEEN two render-target segments was recorded as **GFX-143**, **since closed 2026-07-28** — backbuffer bind cycles are now segments in the same ordered stream, and this task's own oracle is unchanged at 44/44 across that change. Directly relevant false positives found and strengthened: `vulkan_render_target_usage_test.cpp` never looked at what frame 0's three-target shared record produced, and now does, byte-exact — A/B **4/5 → 5/5**, its third target reading the SECOND target's green pre-fix; REMED-GFX-136's `Settle()`/`Q1` documentation asserted the collapsing as current behaviour and called it legitimate, both corrected. Cross-backend controls: **EASYGL 41/41, D3D9 41/41, D3D11 41/41, WEBGPU 40/40, BGFX 39/39, SOFTWARE / SDL_RENDERER / ASCII / DX3 38/38, HEADLESS 30/30, SDL_GPU 28/28**, CANVAS and D3D12 compile-verified. Standard Vulkan validation clean; ASan and UBSan clean over Vulkan (new persistent `cmake-build-vulkan-ubsan`), Software and SdlGpu; full Vulkan `ctest` **5915/5921**, every miss pre-recorded or proven flaky in isolation. All fourteen backend libraries build. Spawned **GFX-143** (backbuffer/render-target interleaving), **GFX-144** (two pre-existing synchronization-validation hazard classes, unchanged 14 → 15 across the fix), **GFX-145** (SdlGpu collapses bind cycles the same way — since fixed, and its own oracle run is now 39/39). test `3c4d24d0`, fix `2ef24462`, coverage `d02efbc8`, contracts `be1dae6e` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-141` | **A multisampled `RenderTargetCube` shared ONE multisample colour attachment across all six faces on EasyGL, Vulkan and SdlGpu — DONE (2026-07-28).** Only one face is ever rendered at a time, so all three allocated a single multisample colour surface per cube target and pointed every face at it: `EasyGLRenderTargetCubeBackend::msaaColorRbo_` (one renderbuffer, attached once at construction and never re-attached, because a renderbuffer carries no face identity), `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend::msaaColorImage_` (one single-layer `TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT` image) and `SdlGpuRenderTargetCubeState::msaaTexture` (one single-layer scratch that had to be cycled every pass). That is enough to PRODUCE a face and leaves nothing per-face to load back, so `PreserveContents` could not be honoured — while the resolved single-sample cube layer still held the right content, which is why a full redraw and an immediate readback both passed and only a face revisited for a PARTIAL update after another face exposes it. **Three distinct pre-fix signatures, all measured**: EasyGL returned the OTHER face (60 of face 0's 64 texels were face 1's pattern); Vulkan returned opaque black because `GetOrCreateRTRenderPassMsaa` had no `LOAD` variant at all, so even a ONE-face multisampled target failed; SdlGpu returned transparent black from its cycled scratch. **Fix:** six per-face multisample surfaces on each — six renderbuffers re-attached by `BindAsRenderTargetFace` (EasyGL), one image with **six array layers** and one per-layer view per face plus a real `LOAD_OP_LOAD`/`STORE_OP_STORE` MSAA render pass keyed by `discardContents`, one up-front `UNDEFINED → COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL` transition of all six layers, and `TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT` dropped for a preserving target (Vulkan), six single-layer textures with `cycle = false`, the single-sample leg's own load rule and `SDL_GPU_STOREOP_RESOLVE_AND_STORE` instead of plain `RESOLVE`, which explicitly leaves the multisample contents undefined (SdlGpu — a real 6-layer MSAA array is impossible there, SDL_gpu forbids `sample_count > 1` on any array texture). `FaceProxy::ColorLoadOpIsClearEXT()` no longer forces a clear for the MSAA leg, so REMED-GFX-129's ordered clears behave identically on both legs. Depth/stencil keeps `CLEAR`/`DONT_CARE` in both Vulkan variants, so **REMED-GFX-142 is neither widened nor silently resolved**. Cost is one → six permanent multisample surfaces per cube target, `size * size * samples * 4 * 6` bytes, allocated once: no per-bind allocation, no per-bind copy, no fence wait, no extra submit or Present — which is exactly what D3D11 and D3D12 have always allocated (a six-slice multisampled array with one per-slice view), and neither is touched. Fixed by consequence: `VulkanMRTProxy` took one shared multisample view per cube, so an MRT set naming two faces of the SAME cube wired both colour attachments to identical storage — which is why it had to be rejected outright; it now takes the bound face's own view and the case is correct instead of refused (`vulkan_cube_mrt_binding_test` 15/15). New public oracle `examples/rendertargetcube_msaa_face_test.cpp` on all fourteen backends, with **no `Settle()`, `Present`, intermediate `GetData` or flush anywhere** — REMED-GFX-140/145 made every bind cycle its own native pass, so the whole sequence is queued and read once. **EASYGL 14/32 → 32/32, VULKAN 12/32 → 32/32, SDL_GPU 12/32 → 32/32**; D3D11 32/32 unmodified, BGFX 32/32 (every MSAA content check is the declared refusal, REMED-GFX-134/138), HEADLESS 32/32, WEBGPU 9/9 and D3D9 9/9 (cube MSAA never engages), SOFTWARE/SDL_RENDERER/ASCII/DX3 1/1, CANVAS compile-verified. One **independent defect fixed because it aborts the process**: bgfx's `isFrameBufferValid` refuses a multisampled depth attachment whose TEXTURE lacks `BGFX_TEXTURE_RT_WRITE_ONLY`/`BGFX_TEXTURE_MSAA_SAMPLE`, checking creation flags rather than the attachment resolve mode Task 951 already set — so every depth-backed multisampled `RenderTargetCube` aborted at its first bind, unnoticed because every existing bgfx cube MSAA check uses `DepthFormat::None`. Regressions: EasyGL 255/258, Vulkan 172/173, SdlGpu 74/75, Bgfx 130/136 — the same pre-recorded failures in each; GFX-134 55/55, GFX-136 30/30, GFX-129 34/34, GFX-140 44/44, GFX-143 29/29. Vulkan standard **and** synchronization validation clean (layer proved loaded), SdlGpu's Vulkan route likewise; ASan/UBSan clean on EasyGL, Vulkan, SdlGpu and Bgfx. Recorded separately, not fixed: the **same** missing `BGFX_TEXTURE_RT_WRITE_ONLY` in `BgfxRenderTargetBackend`'s 2D leg, and Vulkan's multisampled `RenderTarget2D` still discarding on `PreserveContents` (the same missing-load-variant half, on a target with no second face to alias). test `07171020`, fix `773dace7` | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-142` | **`RenderTargetUsage` governed colour only and the backends with a real load action disagreed about depth/stencil — DONE (2026-07-28).** The reference settles it in words: FNA3D's public header documents `FNA3D_SetRenderTargets`' last parameter as *"Set this to 1 to store the **color/depth/stencil** contents for future use"*, and all three FNA3D drivers agree (an OpenGL FBO attachment and a D3D11 DSV simply persist; the SDL_GPU driver loads both aspects unless a clear is pending and always stores). `DiscardContents` is not undefined either — FNA's `GraphicsDevice.SetRenderTargets` ends with `Clear(Target|DepthBuffer|Stencil, DiscardColor, Viewport.MaxDepth, 0)`, a deterministic colour `(0,0,0,255)` / depth `1.0` / stencil `0`. And cube depth is **per-TARGET**: FNA's `RenderTargetCube` allocates exactly ONE `glDepthStencilBuffer` for the whole cube, so all six faces share it — the opposite of REMED-GFX-141's per-face colour conclusion, which is why **no** per-face depth storage was added. **Five independent defects**, none the same as another: (1) the shared `DiscardContents` bind clear asked for `Target|DepthBuffer` and never `Stencil`, so a discarded target kept its stencil for ever; (2) `GraphicsDevice::Clear` decided "is there a depth buffer" by `dynamic_cast<RenderTarget2D*>` alone, so **every** `ClearOptions::DepthBuffer`/`Stencil` issued while a `RenderTargetCube` was bound was silently dropped, on every backend; (3) EasyGL's colour-only `Clear()` issued `ClearFlags::Color|Depth`, so clearing just the colour target wiped depth; (4) Vulkan used `LOAD_OP_DONT_CARE`/`STORE_OP_DONT_CARE` for depth+stencil in the *preserving* RT pass and cleared them unconditionally in the MSAA pass — llvmpipe happened to retain the memory, which is exactly the accidental retention that would fail on a tiler; (5) WebGPU's cube pass hardcoded `WGPULoadOp_Clear`. Fixing (4) surfaced a **sixth**: with depth genuinely shared between faces, REMED-GFX-074's readback flush filtered segments by `seg.rt == onlyRT`, right for per-face colour and wrong for one shared depth image, so reading face B replayed only face B's cycles and its own earlier depth clear landed after face A's depth writes — now grouped by `VulkanRTSource::DepthStencilOwnerEXT()` at all three record/reset/consume sites. **Zero** new render-pass variants, depth surfaces, per-bind allocations, copies or waits; one extra one-time construction submit for a preserving depth-backed Vulkan cube. New public oracle `examples/rendertarget_depthstencil_usage_test.cpp` on all fourteen backends: three vertical bands separate preserved depth from cleared depth, lost colour and a second pass that never drew, and a stamp/gate sequence does the same for stencil (preserved / cleared / ignored are mutually exclusive). Exact A/B with the same final file: **EASYGL 26/29 → 29/29, VULKAN 27/29 → 29/29, WEBGPU 27/29 → 29/29, BGFX 27/29 → 29/29, SDL_GPU 28/29 → 29/29**; SOFTWARE/HEADLESS/SDL_RENDERER/ASCII/DX3 29/29, CANVAS compile-verified, D3D9/D3D11/D3D12 compile+link only (SDL under Wine reports "No displays available" on every Xvfb, proven with the known-good `d3d11_smoke`). **EasyGL_RenderTargetCube_DepthFormat, a pre-existing failure, is fixed by consequence.** Vulkan standard **and** synchronization validation clean, SdlGpu's Vulkan route and WebGPU's error callbacks clean, EasyGL `MESA_DEBUG=1` clean; ASan+UBSan clean on Vulkan/SdlGpu/EasyGL/Software. Declared, recorded, **not** fixed: WebGPU never enables the stencil test at all (WEBGPU-83, so no stencil result is claimed there), SdlGpu and WebGPU deliver a mid-cycle `Clear()` through the pass load action so it cannot wipe an earlier draw (REMED-GFX-129's equivalent, unimplemented there), bgfx aborts on a multisampled depth-backed `RenderTarget2D` (REMED-GFX-141's recorded RT_WRITE_ONLY 2D leg), and Vulkan's multisampled `RenderTarget2D` leg hardcodes `discardContents=true`. test `6f659eef`, fix `a18df8fb`, tests `f2c322b3` | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-143` | **Vulkan and SdlGpu replayed a whole frame's backbuffer work AFTER every render-target pass, so backbuffer and render-target cycles could not interleave — DONE (2026-07-28).** Scope is BOTH backends and always was: this row recorded SdlGpu's identical class as *"deliberately left here"* and REMED-GFX-145's own record closes with *"REMED-GFX-143 is the open task for this class and this note extends it to SdlGpu"* — no separate ticket existed and none was allocated. REMED-GFX-140/145 gave every render-target bind cycle its own native pass in public order, but both kept a two-part recorder whose second part was ONE trailing swapchain pass: Vulkan's `RecordCommandBuffer` Phase 2 called `drawSpritesFor(nullptr, kAllSegments, …)`, a sentinel meaning "no segment filter"; SdlGpu's `EnsureFrameRendered` called `RenderQueuedDraws(…, kSwapchainSegment)` whose filter read `if (segment != kSwapchainSegment && ref.segment != segment) continue;` — the same escape by a different name. So `bind t; draw red; unbind; draw t at LEFT; bind t; draw green; unbind; draw t at RIGHT` executed as `t(red); t(green); backbuffer(LEFT, RIGHT)` and BOTH consumers sampled the final green. Nothing observable in `t` changes — GFX-140's `S6` covers exactly that sequence and passes — and the ordinary XNA pattern (render into targets, then compose onto the backbuffer) is already the order a trailing pass produces, which is why the class survived two segmentation fixes; what breaks is the reverse. Of the nine candidate causes, four were true (backbuffer commands carried no usable segment id, `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)` opened no segment, backbuffer clear values were frame-global, the presentation architecture assumed one backbuffer pass per frame) and five were not — backbuffer draws were never in a separate queue, swapchain acquisition was never the constraint, and viewport/scissor were already captured PER DRAW (GFX-062/064/068). **Fix: one ordered stream of bind cycles, backbuffer cycles included.** A `PassSegment` with no target IS a backbuffer cycle; `kAllSegments` was deleted outright and SdlGpu's filter reduced to `ref.segment != segment`. `GetOrCreateSwapchainRenderPass` supplies the load/store variant a second entry into the same acquired image needs, keyed on `(msaa, loadColor, loadDepth, loadStencil, storeForNext)`, with the all-clear/no-store combination returning `renderPass_`/`renderPassMsaa_` themselves so a single-cycle frame creates no variant and records byte-for-byte the pass it always did; variants differ only in load/store ops and initial layouts, which compatibility ignores, so **no pipeline and no framebuffer is created per segment**. Clears became per-aspect and per-segment on the backbuffer (`wantColor`/`wantDepth`/`wantStencil`), replacing the frame-global scalars whose last value used to colour the whole frame's backbuffer work; a synthetic final backbuffer segment keeps a frame that drew nothing to the swapchain clearing, storing and ending in `PRESENT_SRC_KHR`. SdlGpu gained `BeginBackbufferSegment` (construction, every unbind, and mid-frame flush reopen) and `RenderToSwapchain`, and its LAST backbuffer segment always gets its pass while empty earlier ones issue none. **One access bit had to be fixed with it:** `VK_ACCESS_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_READ_BIT` was missing from the entry subpass dependency of every Vulkan render pass — a `LOAD_OP_LOAD` colour attachment IS a colour-attachment read — so the new backbuffer LOAD variants would have multiplied REMED-GFX-144's recorded loadOp hazard class 53 → 104; the bit is added at ALL SIX sites, keeping Task 905's byte-identical masks, and that class measures **15 → 0** on GFX-140's fixture and **53 → 0** on this one while GFX-144's other class is untouched (5 → 5, 27 → 27) and stays open. Red-first shared `examples/backbuffer_pass_order_test.cpp` on all fourteen backends, with no Present, GetData, flush, fence, sleep or extra frame between any producer and its consumer: **VULKAN 17/29 → 29/29.** Under LD_PRELOAD interposers of the real entry points, identical binaries: `vkCmdBeginRenderPass` **83 → 137** and `SDL_BeginGPURenderPass` **61 → 115**, ends == begins in every run, and the recorded pass ORDER changes from `8x8,8x8,64x64` to `64x64,8x8,64x64,8x8,64x64` (Vulkan) and from `T,T,…,T,B` to `B,T,B,T,B,…` (SdlGpu) — while `vkQueueSubmit` 73 → 73, `vkQueuePresentKHR` 27 → 27, `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` 27 → 27, `vkWaitForFences` 52 → 52, `vkCreateFramebuffer` 46 → 46, `vkCreateImageView` 76 → 76, `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` 6 → 6, `SDL_SubmitGPUCommandBuffer` 16 → 16, `SDL_WaitForGPUFences` 5 → 5, SdlGpu swapchain acquisitions 5 → 5 and every SdlGpu `Create*` still equals its `Release*`. The only real additions are four bounded permanent Vulkan render-pass variants (`vkCreateRenderPass` 4 → 8) and one `vkCmdPipelineBarrier` per backbuffer cycle after the first (+54, matching the +54 passes exactly). **SdlGpu has no public backbuffer oracle at all** (no `ReadBackbuffer` override, so `GetBackBufferData` raises) — recorded, not papered over: the fixture was restructured so every backend ISSUES its whole sequence and only the pixel judgement is gated, turning 26 skipped sequences into 26 executed ones there, and SdlGpu is verified structurally with the semantics pinned byte-exactly on Vulkan and on EasyGL, which scored 29/29 BEFORE any fix and so proved every expectation in the file is XNA-correct rather than a guess. Khronos validation clean with the layer proved loaded on both; synchronization validation reports zero on SdlGpu and only GFX-144's remaining acquire class on Vulkan; the pre-existing SdlGpu `VUID-vkCmdDraw-None-09600` is A/B-identical 2 → 2. ASan and UBSan clean over Vulkan, SdlGpu and Software (runtimes proved linked by symbol). Regression gates: GFX-140 oracle **44/44**, GFX-145 oracle **39/39**, SdlGpu upload **17/17**, MRT **45/45**, GFX-136 **5/5**, GFX-134 **30/30**; `ctest -R '^Vulkan'` **168/169** and `ctest -R '^SdlGpu'` **72/73**, both misses pre-recorded (the SdlGpu one A/B-re-proven). Cross-backend controls, only Vulkan and SdlGpu production changed: **EASYGL / BGFX / WEBGPU / D3D9 / D3D11 / HEADLESS 29/29, SOFTWARE / SDL_RENDERER / ASCII / DX3 28/28**, CANVAS and D3D12 compile-verified (D3D12 faults under Wine/vkd3d identically on GFX-140's existing fixture, pre-existing). One genuine false positive found and removed: `vulkan_rt_roundtrip_test.cpp` inserted an artificial `GetBackBufferData` between producer and consumer — A/B-attributed to GFX-140, which had already made it unnecessary — plus three stale claims that documented the trailing pass as current architecture. All fourteen backend libraries build. Recorded separately and NOT fixed: Vulkan AND bgfx both replay all sprites then all 3D draws inside ONE bind cycle (check `O3`, a distinct root cause — two queues with no shared order — which SdlGpu's unified `drawOrder_` already avoids); WebGPU does not clip a BACKBUFFER SpriteBatch fill with `ScissorRectangle` at all though its render-target scissor works; WebGPU's backbuffer clear is load-op-only like Vulkan's; `VulkanGraphicsBackend::ClearDepth` records no clear request, so `Clear(ClearOptions::DepthBuffer, …)` is dropped entirely; and bgfx raises from `SetDepthTestEnabled`/`SetDepthWriteEnabled`. test `8cd84819`, Vulkan fix `5a541657`, SdlGpu fix `27387b08`, coverage `f209c3a6`, false positives `dae1a75e` | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-144` | **The Vulkan backend wrote the acquired swapchain image before `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` had handed it over — DONE (2026-07-28).** REMED-GFX-140 recorded TWO synchronization-validation hazard classes and fixed neither; they are separate findings and are reported separately here. **Class 1** (`READ_AFTER_WRITE`, a `LOAD_OP_LOAD` colour attachment read unsynchronized against the layout transition of the same `vkCmdBeginRenderPass`) was **already resolved before this task** by REMED-GFX-143's `VK_ACCESS_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_READ_BIT`, and GFX-143 required that to be re-measured rather than assumed: measured **0 pre-fix on both existing fixtures**, confirmed, and **no production change for it is made here**. **Class 2 is this task**, and it was a real missing dependency, not a false positive or a WSI limitation. `SubmitFrame` waits the acquire semaphore with `pWaitDstStageMask = COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT`, which blocks that stage and every LATER one and nothing earlier. The batch's first touch of the image is `vkCmdBeginRenderPass`, whose automatic `initialLayout` transition is a WRITE ordered solely by the `VK_SUBPASS_EXTERNAL → 0` subpass dependency — after the availability operations of its FIRST synchronization scope, before the visibility operations of its second. That first scope was `FRAGMENT_SHADER` + `EARLY_FRAGMENT_TESTS` + `LATE_FRAGMENT_TESTS`, **every stage of which precedes `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT`**, while `dstStageMask` demanded the transition finish before `EARLY_FRAGMENT_TESTS`, a stage the wait does not block — so the transition was free to run before the semaphore signalled, writing an image the presentation engine had not finished reading. The layer reported it once per acquired frame as `SYNC-HAZARD-WRITE-AFTER-READ` (`0x376bc9df`), `prior_access SYNC_PRESENT_ENGINE_SYNCVAL_PRESENT_ACQUIRE_READ_SYNCVAL`, `read_barriers` = `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT` + `BOTTOM_OF_PIPE`. **Fix: one added stage bit** — `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT` leads `deps[0].srcStageMask`, placing the transition at exactly the stage the wait blocks; `srcAccessMask` untouched, since the layer's own *"an execution dependency is sufficient"* is correct for a WRITE_AFTER_READ. **Attributed by measurement:** patching only the three SWAPCHAIN sites (`CreateRenderPass`, `CreateRenderPassMsaa`, `GetOrCreateSwapchainRenderPass`) already clears it 27 → 0 and 5 → 0; the three render-target sites never touch a swapchain image and carry the bit only so Task 905's byte-identical masks stay identical, exactly as GFX-143 did (widening a dependency's FIRST scope only adds ordering, so it cannot introduce a hazard). Recorded in passing: `GetOrCreateMRTRenderPass` was **already** not byte-identical (`FRAGMENT_SHADER` + `TRANSFER`), so that invariant was already partial. Counts, layer proved loaded, llvmpipe, repeat cap lifted (the default cap of ten silently turns a count into a floor): `backbuffer_pass_order` **27 → 0**, `rendertarget_pass_boundary` **5 → 0**, the new fixture **28 → 0** — 28 being exactly its 28 acquired frames — with standard validation, VUIDs and warnings **0 → 0** throughout and SdlGpu unchanged at 0. **Frame model documented and asserted, not described:** 2 frame slots, 4 swapchain images, one semaphore pair / fence / primary command buffer per SLOT and none per image, fence waited before the reset and reset only after a SUCCESSFUL acquire (so `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DATE_KHR` cannot strand it), `VK_SUBOPTIMAL_KHR` correctly treated as success. **Image-in-flight tracking is not required and none was added** — an image can only be re-acquired after a present that waited the `renderFinished` semaphore of the submission that rendered it, so the acquire already carries that guarantee and an `imagesInFlight_` array would be redundant state, not a fix. New `examples/vulkan_swapchain_sync_test.cpp` (**21/25 → 25/25**, the four red checks being exactly the four hazard classifications) enables synchronization validation **in process** via `VkValidationFeaturesEXT` plus `VK_EXT_layer_settings` (queried from the layer, not assumed), proves the layer loaded instead of trusting a zero, classifies on `pMessageIdName` rather than a diagnostic sentence, and asserts the MODEL: both frame slots used, **4 of 4** distinct image indices acquired, one acquire = one submit = one present (28/28/28), fence waits 31 = 28 + 3 readbacks, a real resize recreating the swapchain (2 → 3), zero per-frame growth in semaphores/fences/command buffers, and byte-exact pixels after each phase. A trap is recorded in source: the opt-in must precede Game CONSTRUCTION — the instance is created there, and asking from `Initialize()` yields an instance without the checks and a file that passes for the wrong reason (measured). No `vkDeviceWaitIdle` or queue wait per frame, no extra submit/present/frame, no new object; the only device-wide wait remains the bounded one inside actual swapchain recreation. Two false-positive-capable files found and both strengthened: `IndexedDrawDeferredTests.cpp`, the repo's only test making Vulkan validation fatal, passed VACUOUSLY whenever the layer was absent (empty list, no messenger) and now asserts `IsValidationActiveEXT()` first; `vulkan_rtcube_test.cpp` claimed a blue pixel *"proves the 6 cube-face passes completed cleanly"* on one frame, one pixel and a ±50 band — claim corrected to attachment routing and tightened to byte-exact `(0,0,255)`, measured identical before and after. Gates green: GFX-140 **44/44**, GFX-143 **29/29**, GFX-136, GFX-134's eight cube tests, GFX-093/095/096/112; `ctest -R '^Vulkan'` **169/170**, the miss being `Vulkan_DepthBias` A/B-re-proven pre-existing (3/4 → 3/4, same failing check). **REMED-GFX-129 not begun and unchanged** at the time of this task; **since closed 2026-07-28**, with this file's own run re-measured at 28/28 (three new checks) and its acquire-hazard count still 0. ASan and UBSan clean over all three fixtures with runtimes proved linked. Cross-backend controls, only Vulkan production changed: EASYGL 17/17 + 29/29 + 43/43, SOFTWARE 16/16 + 28/28 + 40/40, SDL_GPU 4/4 + 29/29 + 39/39, HEADLESS 3/3 + 29/29 + 30/30. | `REMED-GFX-145` | **SdlGpu collapsed every bind cycle of one render target into a single render pass per frame — DONE (2026-07-28).** `EnsureFrameRendered` walked `usedRenderTargetsThisFrame_`, the UNIQUE `SdlGpuRenderTarget2DState`s bound this frame, and gave each ONE `SDL_BeginGPURenderPass`/`SDL_EndGPURenderPass` inside which `RenderQueuedDraws` replayed every queued draw whose `DrawTarget` matched; `usedRenderTargetCubeFacesThisFrame_` did the same per unique `(cube, face)`. `DrawTarget` — resource pointer plus cube face — was the only grouping key the deferred queues carried, so two public cycles of one target shared one load action, one clear and one attachment set (the field's own doc comment said so: *"every Clear()/draw call recorded against the same target within one frame is accumulated into that target's single pass, regardless of how many times SetRenderTarget2D re-selected it"*). REMED-GFX-140's public oracle measured **19/37**. **Fix:** segment, not target identity, is the grouping key. `PassSegment` records one bind cycle — resource, cube face, MRT attachment set and the `Clear()` values issued inside it — and `passSegments_` keeps them in the order the cycles were opened, so replay is public order with no sort. `BeginRenderTargetSegment`/`BeginCubeFaceSegment` run on EVERY bind including a rebind of the resource, face or MRT set already current; `EndRenderTargetSegment` on every unbind and on both wrappers' destructors; `QueuedDrawRef::segment` is tagged in `PushDrawOrder`, the single choke point all nine draw families and `QueueSprite` route through, and `RenderQueuedDraws` filters on it. A → B → A is three passes; an empty segment issues none. **Two dependent defects had to be fixed with it:** (1) clear values lived on the TARGET (`QueueClear` → `state->clearColor`), so the frame's last `Clear()` supplied the colour for that target's one pass and every earlier cycle's was lost — they now belong to the segment, and the per-resource flags keep only their other meaning, "no pass has ever written this resource"; (2) the MRT attachment set lived on the primary's state (`mrtSiblings`/`isMrtSibling`), so binding `{a,b}` then `{a,c}` rewrote the first cycle's set and `b` — still flagged a sibling, no longer in anyone's list — **produced no pass at all**. `currentExtraMrtTargets_` is gone with it. **SDL_GPU 19/37 → 39/39**, with no flush, readback or `Present` between any two cycles. Under an LD_PRELOAD counter of the real SDL_gpu entry points, identical final test files: `SDL_BeginGPURenderPass` **64 → 102** (oracle), **31 → 78** (new 3D/upload file), **112 → 113** (MRT); begins == ends in every run; and `SDL_CreateGPUGraphicsPipeline` 4/3/17 → 4/3/17, `SDL_CreateGPUTexture` 46/19/19 → unchanged, `SDL_CreateGPUBuffer` 5/99/34 → unchanged, `SDL_CreateGPUTransferBuffer` 68/119/134 → unchanged, `SDL_AcquireGPUCommandBuffer` 68/38/104 → unchanged, `SDL_SubmitGPUCommandBuffer` 32/21/58 → unchanged, `SDL_WaitForGPUFences` 36/17/46 → unchanged — the required boundaries and not one extra permanent object, command buffer, submit or fence wait; every `Create*` equals its `Release*`, so resources return to baseline after disposal. Per check the boundary is exact: `S1` 1 → 2 native RT passes, `S8` 1 → 3, `S12` 2 → 4, `C1` 2 → 3, `R1` 2 → 17, `M2` 1 → 2 (and `b` now renders), while `S5` (two independent targets) and `X1` (one cycle) are unchanged. **The Vulkan arena defect has NO SdlGpu counterpart, and that was measured:** the 3D families allocate a per-command `SDL_GPUBuffer` and sprites index `spriteVertexBuffer_` by their own position in `spriteCommands_`, so new checks `U5`/`U6` — distinctive left/right GEOMETRY across A → B → A, indexed, non-indexed and sprite paths — pass pre-fix on the aliasing assertion and were red only for the segmentation. **No independent SdlGpu clear defect remained** (unlike Vulkan's REMED-GFX-129, since closed 2026-07-28): `clearOnPreserveTarget` is true here and `X2` passes because the second cycle is now its own pass with its own load op; `clearAfterDrawWins` stays false, the load-op-only limitation `X3` pins. Standard Khronos validation clean with the layer proved loaded (`VK_LOADER_DEBUG=layer`), and synchronization validation reports **zero** hazards with the mechanism proved live (the same settings run against Vulkan reports REMED-GFX-144's recorded 15). Directly relevant false positives found and strengthened: `sdlgpu_mrt_test.cpp` repeats eleven bind cycles over one target set but a `GetData` ends every one — its new check queues `{A,B}` then `{A,C}` with nothing between and is **44/45** pre-fix; the shared oracle's arena check `P6` needs `GetBackBufferData`, which SdlGpu cannot do, so new `P7` makes the same measurement through the Present path (37/37 → 39/39 here, +2 on every backend with readback); `rendertargetcube_usage_test.cpp`'s `Settle()`/`Q1` docs named the collapsing Vulkan-only and now record SdlGpu's route. Eight false-positive-capable files found, three strengthened, plus a new dedicated `examples/sdlgpu_pass_boundary_upload_test.cpp` (**11/17 → 17/17**) for the three questions the shared oracle cannot ask: the eight 3D draw families, upload isolation with real geometry, and depth/stencil load actions (`D1`: a farther quad rejected by the previous cycle's stored depth, then accepted after a depth-ONLY `Clear()` that leaves colour loading). Cross-backend controls, only SdlGpu production changed: **VULKAN 44/44, D3D9 43/43, D3D11 43/43, EASYGL 43/43, WEBGPU 42/42, BGFX 41/41, SOFTWARE / SDL_RENDERER / ASCII / DX3 40/40, HEADLESS 30/30**, CANVAS and D3D12 compile-verified. ASan and UBSan clean over all four SdlGpu render-target suites; `ctest -R '^SdlGpu'` **71/72** (only the A/B-re-proven pre-existing `SdlGpu_RenderState`), full `ctest` 5775 registered with 5 pre-recorded failures. All fourteen backend libraries build. | `REMED-GFX-146` | **WebGPU resolved `GraphicsDevice.ScissorRectangle` and `RasterizerState.ScissorTestEnable` from live state at flush time — DONE (2026-07-28).** The scissor counterpart of REMED-GFX-116, and **one root cause, not two**. All three render-pass recorders (`EnsureFrameRendered`, `RenderPendingDrawsToRenderTarget`, `RenderPendingDrawsToRenderTargetCubeFace`) issued exactly ONE `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetScissorRect` at the top of the pass, computed from the live `scissorEnabled_`/`scissorX_`/`scissorY_`/`scissorW_`/`scissorH_` members; all eleven deferred command structs (the ten 3D families plus `SpriteCommand`) carried no scissor state at all. Deferring the RECORDING is legal, resolving the value late is not: several rectangles in one bind cycle collapsed last-wins, and because `SetRenderTarget` resets `ScissorRectangle` to the newly bound target's full size on every bind (FNA parity), an ordinary cycle ALWAYS ended with the full rectangle live — so every already-queued draw was replayed unclipped. **The recorded BACKBUFFER finding needed no separate explanation.** REMED-GFX-143 recorded *"WebGPU does not clip a BACKBUFFER SpriteBatch fill with `ScissorRectangle` at all, though its render-target scissor works (GFX-140's `S11` passes)"* — that was the symptom of this same mechanism seen from two sides: its check `V2` restores the full rectangle before its single read, while `S11`'s render-target cycles are separated by a target switch, which IS the flush on this backend, so live state still matched. Measured, not inferred: under an LD_PRELOAD interposer of the real entry point the pre-fix run issued **52** native `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetScissorRect` calls of which **40** were the full render target `0 0 64 48`, and never any rectangle a draw had been issued under. **Fix:** `WebGPUScissorSnapshot` holds `ScissorTestEnable` and all four `Rectangle` components by value. Both halves travel together — a snapshot carrying only the rectangle would still be wrong, since a bind cycle always ends with the target's full rectangle live. `CaptureScissor()` is called by every `Queue*Draw()` and by `QueueSprite()`, at the public draw call, beside `CaptureViewport()` (11 capture sites, exactly matching the 11 command-vector `push_back` sites, so no family can omit it); `BeginPassScissor()` opens each recorder at the whole target — deliberately not the live rectangle — and `ApplyDrawScissor()` is the only place a captured value becomes native pass state. A disabled scissor test resolves to the whole attachment, which is what "disabled" means on an API with no separate scissor-test toggle. An enabled rectangle is clipped to the attachment in signed arithmetic with the edge sums formed in **64 bits** before anything reaches the unsigned native call, so a negative X/Y or an overhanging rectangle cannot wrap through `uint32_t`; Width/Height are measured from the UNCLAMPED origin so an off-target left edge keeps its on-target part instead of being shifted; ranges are clipped, never rejected. **Red-first, A/B against pre-fix production with the FINAL test file:** new shared `examples/deferred_scissor_capture_test.cpp` **4/47 → 47/47**, every sequence queued whole with no `GetData`, `Present`, flush, fence, sleep, extra frame or render-target switch between two draws and observed exactly once at the end, with the rectangle restored to the whole target first so "resolve at flush time" and "capture at queue time" predict different images. The four pre-fix passes are the controls. Geometry separates every wrong implementation: each draw under its own rectangle, all under the final one, ignored, applied as a VIEWPORT, X/Y dropped, Width/Height dropped, offset applied twice, Y flipped. **Cardinality, live backend counters, `examples/webgpu_scissor_cardinality_test.cpp` 23/23:** four rectangles in one cycle = **1** render pass, **1** submit, **5** native `setScissorRect` calls and **0** new pipeline variants; four identical rectangles = **2** calls; 32 distinct rectangles and the whole boundary set grow no cache; `ScissorTestEnable` transitions are not a pipeline dimension; repeated frames stable; the backbuffer pass follows the same rules; WebGPU validation silent throughout. Across the pixel fixture, native `setScissorRect` **52 → 143** while `setViewport` is **unchanged at 59** — REMED-GFX-116 untouched, and its own fixture stays **39/39**. **Two genuine cross-backend divergences measured, declared per backend and asserted exactly in BOTH directions rather than skipped:** a degenerate rectangle rasterizes nothing on WebGPU/Software/D3D11/D3D9 but not on Vulkan (`computeScissor` returns the whole framebuffer for `sw == 0 \|\| sh == 0`), EasyGL (`SetScissorRect` returns early on `w <= 0 \|\| h <= 0`, leaving the previous rectangle), bgfx or SdlGpu — two mechanisms, one observable; and Headless REJECTS an out-of-bounds rectangle with a diagnostic (HEADLESS-23) instead of clipping. **Cross-backend controls, only WebGPU production changed:** VULKAN / EASYGL / BGFX / SDL_GPU / SOFTWARE / D3D11 / D3D9 **47/47**, HEADLESS **46/46**. **Four false-positive-capable tests found, three strengthened, each A/B-proven:** `webgpu_graphicsstate_test.cpp` check E uses one rectangle and reads before restoring, so it passes either way — new check H is **7/8 → 8/8**; `backbuffer_pass_order_test.cpp` declared `backbufferSpriteScissorApplies` false for WebGPU and its `V2` measured **28/29 → 29/29** the moment the fix landed; `spritebatch_begin_rasterizerstate_scissor_test.cpp` (REMED-GFX-081) was registered for SdlGpu and Vulkan but never WebGPU — now registered, **4/4**, and it passes pre-fix too because its render-target round trip is itself the flush, which is exactly why it could never have found this; `deferred_viewport_capture_test.cpp` J1/J2 documented the live-state scissor as current behaviour and the claim is corrected. **Gates:** full WebGPU shard **47/48**, the one miss `WebGPU_Clear_Readback` A/B-proven byte-identical at 7/10 with the same three alpha-blend and sampler-address-mode checks either side; `WebGPU_Deferred_Viewport` 39/39, `WebGPU_Viewport_Cardinality`, `WebGPU_SpriteBatch_BlendState` (GFX-102), `WebGPU_RenderTarget_PassBoundary` (GFX-140), `WebGPU_Backbuffer_PassOrder` (GFX-143), `WebGPU_GraphicsDevice_OrderedClear`, every RenderTargetCube contract test all green; Vulkan scissor/viewport gates 4/4. ASan and UBSan clean (runtimes proved linked by symbol) over all three WebGPU fixtures — **0** reports. All fourteen backend libraries compile incrementally. No shader source and no generated artifact changed. | `REMED-GFX-147` | **EasyGL sampled a `RenderTarget2D` bottom-up while its readback and its ordinary textures were top-down — DONE (2026-07-29).** An OpenGL framebuffer's origin is bottom-left, so a render target's colour texture stores the logical image **bottom-up**: texel row `v=0` is the LAST logical row. `EasyGLRenderTargetBackend::GetData` already compensated (it reads with `glReadPixels`' bottom-left origin at `levelHeight - y - h` and then reverses the rows), which is exactly why the public readback contract has always been correct — so the colour storage and public readback were never the defect, and the fix is deliberately **not** a flip of `GetData`. **Sampling** did not compensate, so a rendered texture arrived vertically mirrored while an uploaded one holding the identical bytes did not. The EasyGL counterpart of REMED-GFX-067's bgfx finding, on all of SpriteBatch and the stock 3D effects at once. **Measured, not inferred:** the new `rendertarget_sampling_orientation_test.cpp` draws the render target and a `Texture2D` built from that target's own `GetData` bytes side by side, in ONE frame, read back ONCE, with identical rectangles, sampler and blend state and no custom UVs on either side; pre-fix **every one of the 34 failures reported `EXACTLY VERTICALLY MIRRORED`**, while storage, readback and ordinary sampling were byte-exact in the same run. **Fix:** one auditable predicate, `SampledRowOrderIsBottomUp()`, answers "did this content arrive by being RENDERED rather than uploaded?" for every EasyGL sampling path so they cannot drift. SpriteBatch maps `v -> 1-v` on its own CPU-generated quad (not in the sprite shader, because `Begin` may substitute an arbitrary user `ShaderEffect` whose GLSL this backend does not own; the vertex data is the only place both programs read), resolved **once per bound source, not per sprite**. The eleven stock 3D fragment shaders route every `sampler2D` fetch through one shared `cnaSampleUV()` helper kept in a single macro, driven by `uRtFlipV.xyzw` (units 0-3) and `uRtFlipVHi.x` (unit 4), each flag written beside the bind of the unit it describes and both uploaded unconditionally so a previous draw's flag cannot leak onto the next ordinary texture — the same per-slot shape bgfx uses for `u_rtFlipV`. A custom `ShaderEffect` is offered the flag if its GLSL declares it. **`v -> 1-v`, not `swap(v1,v2)`:** a swap only reverses rows *inside* the source rectangle and is correct only for a full-height source — which is how REMED-GFX-153 was found on bgfx. **Cost:** no copy, no readback, no re-upload, no extra pass or draw call, no program variant (the flag is a uniform, not a `#define`); +1 `glUniform4f` per stock 3D draw (+2 for the PBR pair), +0 GL calls per sprite, one `dynamic_cast` per bound source. **EasyGL 25/60 -> 60/60**, covering SpriteBatch (source/destination rectangles, rotation, origin, transform, viewport, scissor, Point and Linear, tint, alpha blend), all four `SpriteEffects` flip combinations composing rather than cancelling, six stock 3D paths including indexed geometry and both `DualTextureEffect` slots, target chains A->B->C proving no double flip, MSAA (applied 4x), mipmaps, and `GetData` byte-identical before and after sampling. Mesa GL debug output clean. **Two orientation-blind fixtures were found and one strengthened** — `easygl_render_target_test.cpp` sampled an RT as a texture but used a solid colour read at the centre pixel, a fixed point of a vertical mirror, so it could never fail; A/B proven to fail now on the pre-fix backend. Cross-backend controls all green and unchanged: SOFTWARE 59/59, HEADLESS 33/33, VULKAN 57/57, BGFX 61/61, SDL_GPU 51/51, WEBGPU 52/52. Full `cmake-build-debug` shard 6013/6019, the six failures A/B-proven pre-existing. ASan+UBSan clean. Four independent findings spawned and **declared per backend so each is pinned rather than tolerated**: **GFX-151**, **GFX-152**, **GFX-153**, **GFX-154**; plus **GFX-149** and **GFX-150** measured on the way. test `a5fb2416`, fix `d26d8acb`, coverage `7aa15613`, false positives `a7390a59` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-148` | **Software now evaluates the complete captured `BlendState`; Additive destination loss is fixed — DONE (2026-08-02).** Current-HEAD reproduction confirmed the recorded `(64,96,32,255)` source over `(36,92,173,255)` stored source-only `(64,96,32,255)` instead of `(100,188,205,255)`. A new asymmetric pixel matrix further measured only **5/28** correct before the fix: partially transparent Additive expected `(36,44,106,67)` but produced the hard-coded straight-alpha-over result `(33,36,92,102)`, while zero destination exposed wrong alpha `(25,13,51,64)` instead of `(25,13,51,16)`. CNA's public preset and FNA agree: colour and alpha each use `SourceAlpha`/`One` with `Add`, so `RGB=clamp(src.rgb*src.a+dst.rgb)` and `A=clamp(src.a*src.a+dst.a)`, followed by the established RGBA8 conversion. **Root cause:** `ApplyBlendState` collapsed all four factors and both functions to one Opaque/non-Opaque boolean; the fragment path then used `src*srcA+dst*(1-srcA)` for RGB and straight-over alpha for every non-Opaque preset. Destination loading, texture sampling, target storage, draw order and conversion were correct. **Fix:** Software retains all six blend values plus `BlendFactor`, snapshots them per public draw/SpriteBatch, and independently evaluates colour and alpha source factor, destination factor and function before one clamp and the existing byte conversion; exact Opaque keeps its fast identity path. Two Software fixtures that encoded the obsolete simplified AlphaBlend equation were corrected to premultiplied authoritative inputs/results, and the old Additive source-only carve-out was removed. Dedicated Software **28/28** over opaque/partial/zero/full alpha, zero/saturated destinations, repeated and mixed-colour draws, four standard presets, state transitions/capture, distinct destination pixels, all indexed/non-indexed/DrawUser paths, RenderTarget2D, three backbuffer frames, disposal and teardown. Equivalent controls: EasyGL/Vulkan/Bgfx/WebGPU **28/28**, SDL_GPU **26/26** (no backbuffer readback), Headless **2/2** declared capability boundary. ASan and UBSan are clean; relevant Software regressions pass. One unrelated stale RenderTarget2D exception-type fixture was assigned `REMED-GFX-198` and left OPEN. Only Software production changed; public API, other backend production and `audit/` are untouched. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-149` | **`Texture2D::GetData`'s `startIndex`/`elementCount` contract — DONE (2026-07-30). The ticket named one symptom of seven, and the worst one kills the process. REMED-GFX-128 is the same expression and is closed by the same fix.** The recorded finding was "the render-target fallback is gated on `startIndex == 0`". Measured, the shared `Texture2D::GetData(Color*, int startIndex, int elementCount)` broke the contract seven ways: **(1)** `startIndex` indexed the SOURCE (`src = (startIndex + i) * 4`) while the destination was written from `data[0]` — exactly inverted, and the exact opposite of the rectangle overload's `dst = startIndex + row * w + col` on the same object (that is REMED-GFX-128, which GFX-149 duplicated); **(2)** the render-target fallback rejected any non-zero `startIndex` with `std::runtime_error("no CPU-side pixel data available")`; **(3)** the same gate demanded `elementCount == total`, rejecting the legal EXCESS capacity FNA's own `GetData<T>(T[] data)` passes; **(4)** that fallback wrote from `data[0]`, ignoring `startIndex`; **(5)** `startIndex + elementCount > total` compared DESTINATION indices against the SOURCE pixel count; **(6)** an `elementCount` below the region silently returned a partial frame instead of rejecting — unlike the rectangle overload, `TextureCube`, `Texture3D` and `GetBackBufferData`, all four of which already rejected it; **(7)** `startIndex + elementCount` was `int` arithmetic, so it WRAPPED and the wrapped value passed every later bound. ASan names the line: `heap-buffer-overflow READ of size 1` at `Texture2D.cpp:397`; without ASan, SIGSEGV. A public API argument shape killed the process. **Authoritative semantics, established not assumed:** FNA pins the destination array and transfers to `AddrOfPinnedObject() + startIndex * elementSizeInBytes`, and the archived XNA reference documents `startIndex` as "index of the first element to get" — an ELEMENT index into `data`, never a byte offset and never a source texel. `elementCount` is the destination CAPACITY from `startIndex`: FNA passes it as `elementCount * elementSizeInBytes` and takes the transfer size from the REQUESTED REGION, and `GetData<T>(T[] data)` passes `data.Length`, which may exceed it — so excess is legal and untouched, and a capacity below the region is rejected before any transfer. Region = the complete level 0 for the whole-level overloads and this rectangle's own dimensions at this mip level for the rectangle overload; written range on success is exactly `[startIndex, startIndex + regionPixels)`. Element size is 4 bytes everywhere — the destination type is `Color` and `Texture::ValidateFormat` accepts only `SurfaceFormat::Color` project-wide, so there is no templated element type, no compressed block and no per-format pitch case; that is stated rather than invented as coverage. **Fix:** one shared `validateTransferWindow()` for both overloads (capacity against the requested region, destination window in 64-bit arithmetic rejected before use), the gate deleted, and both copy loops running over the REGION writing at `data[startIndex + i]`. Argument validation precedes the storage/capability decision, so an undersized or overflowing request keeps its specific error instead of being weakened into `NotSupportedException` — REMED-GFX-162's precedence, preserved and asserted on Headless. Adds `CNA_TEXTURE_TRANSFER_TRACE`, an env-gated trace mirroring `CNA_BACKBUFFER_READ_TRACE`. **One production file** (`Texture2D.cpp`); no public signature, header, enum, backend or shader changed, and no extra native copy, staging allocation, map, submit, wait, Present, frame advance or target switch — scratch is still sized to the region, validation is O(1), and the prefix/suffix cost nothing. **New `texture2d_getdata_transfer_range_test.cpp`** gives every destination element a DISTINCT guard `Color(i, i >> 8, 0xAB, 0xCD)` no pattern colour or readback can produce, so a write before `startIndex`, a write past the range, an unwritten element, a duplicated row, a wrong stride and a byte-versus-element mistake are each separately decidable and reported with exact indices and coordinates; the plain-texture pattern is invertible (R = column, G = row) on odd, non-square 13x7 / 11x5 resources, and a render target is measured against the already-correct rectangle overload rather than absolute colours so WebGPU's sRGB encoding and every orientation concern stay out of this ticket. **A/B pre-fix (Software, narrow restoration):** T2–T5 `out_of_range`; T7 no exception and a one-element partial frame; T9 `out_of_range`; T10 no exception with an out-of-bounds read; T11 SIGSEGV. **Post-fix:** SOFTWARE/EASYGL/VULKAN/WEBGPU/SDL_GPU/BGFX/D3D9/D3D11 **74/74**, HEADLESS **71/71**, SDL_RENDERER/DX3/ASCII **70/70** with absent mip storage declared and its rejection asserted, CANVAS cross-built, D3D12 cross-built with its runtime unavailable for the pre-existing DX-100 Wine-dxgi reason (identical on the untouched REMED-GFX-127 control). **Four false positives**, one of which ASSERTED the defect (`software_rendertarget_readback` E10 required `Throws<std::runtime_error>` three lines below D7/D8, which assert the opposite for the rectangle overload on the same target): 88/88 -> 91/91; `rendertarget_sampling_orientation` N4 was an `[INFO]` line that asserted nothing, now real (60/60); `texture2d_getdata_contract` A4's note was false and new A4b covers the whole-level overload on all fourteen backends (39/39 -> 40/40); five `Texture2DTests` decoder tests read ONE pixel of a 4x4/2x2 texture and now read the whole level and assert every pixel. ASan+UBSan clean on Headless and Software (39/15 and 39/16 sanitizer symbols linked; zero reports, zero leaks), and the SAME ASan build on the pre-fix source reports the `heap-buffer-overflow`, so the clean run is meaningful. Vulkan/WebGPU/EasyGL/SDL_GPU/bgfx native validation clean. Regression gates 49/49 (7 fixtures x 7 backends: GFX-127/130/134/161/162/165/166/167). `ctest -L Software` 43/43; `ctest -L Headless` 32/33 (`Headless_Smoke` pre-existing, out of scope); `ctest -R EasyGL` 275/277, both A/B-proven pre-existing with zero `Texture2D::GetData` references. Full Software `CnaTests` 5691/5739 against a 5692/5739 pre-fix baseline. All fourteen backend libraries compile incrementally. No new ticket ID created. test `0fc528da`, fix `f07f5020`, false positives `0cbc73ee`, coverage `83143dd1` | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-150` | **Software discarded the whole `SamplerState` — DONE (2026-07-30).** The ticket said "SpriteBatch interpolates when magnifying even with `PointClamp`". That is one symptom of a wider cause and it names the wrong layer: it is not SpriteBatch, and it is not only magnification. `SoftwareGraphicsBackend::ApplySamplerState(int slot, int, int, int, int)` **named none of its parameters** — filter, addressU, addressV and maxAnisotropy were all discarded — and `SoftwareSpriteBatchBackend`'s `textureFilter_`, `addressU_` and `addressV_` were **dead stores nothing ever read**. One function, `SampleBilinear`, served every textured fragment, so **every draw on this backend was LinearClamp** whatever the game selected: SpriteBatch, BasicEffect, AlphaTest, DualTexture, EnvironmentMap, Skinned and any custom effect alike, and the address mode was as absent as the filter. **The sampler was ignored entirely; the point coordinate was not merely miscomputed** — there was no point path to miscompute. **AUTHORITATIVE CONTRACT, established not assumed.** A destination pixel is sampled at its CENTRE `(x+0.5, y+0.5)`; that centre maps back to a texel-space coordinate `s`; point filtering returns `AddressMode(floor(s), size)` **unchanged**. `floor(s)`, **not** `floor(s - 0.5)`: the half-texel shift converts a position into the LINEAR path's lower-neighbour index, which is correct there and meaningless for selection, because a texel COVERS `[i, i+1)`. A coordinate exactly on a boundary selects the HIGHER texel. The address mode transforms the INTEGER index, after the floor — Clamp reaches the first and last texel and blends with nothing, Wrap tiles, Mirror tiles and flips with period `2*size`. **THE EXACT FAILING EXPRESSION:** `tx = u*texW - 0.5` followed by an unconditional bilerp. At the recorded pixel, destination (2,0) of an 8x4 texture magnified onto 16x8, `u = 0.15625` so `s = 1.25` and the contract owes texel (1,0) `(50,25,190)`; the pre-fix path took `1.25 - 0.5 = 0.75` and returned `0.75*(50,25,190) + 0.25*(20,25,40) = (42,25,152)` — the recorded value, all three channels. Interpolation was horizontal AND vertical; the 4 of 128 correct pixels were exactly where clamping collapsed both endpoints onto one texel, columns 0 and 15 crossed with rows 0 and 7 — `2*2 = 4`, which is why the count was 4 and not some other number. **FIX** = one authoritative `SampleTexture` separating filter selection, texel addressing, address-mode transformation and format decode. `ApplySamplerState` stores per slot; the four 3D call sites read slots 0 and 1 (`GraphicsDevice` re-applies every slot before each draw) while SpriteBatch passes its own `Begin`-resolved sampler **directly**, so a sprite batch can neither inherit the device's 3D state nor leak into it. Clamp is byte-identical to the previous behaviour; the linear path derives its neighbour pair from the RAW pre-address indices so the endpoints cannot collapse through a premature clamp. XNA filter ordinals 5..8 name different minification and magnification filters, so `TriangleMagnifies` classifies each triangle once per bound texture by the standard texels-per-pixel rate — exact for the affine SpriteBatch quads, a per-triangle estimate for perspective geometry. It selects a FILTER, never a mip level. **MIP BOUNDARY, declared:** `SoftwareColorSurface` exposes exactly one level, so sampling is always level 0 and no mipmapping system was introduced. **CUBE BOUNDARY, declared:** `SampleCubeMap` remains nearest-only with no cross-face filtering — a pre-existing documented simplification that happens to match Point, untouched here. Float-to-int conversion saturates in FLOAT space first, so a non-finite or extreme coordinate is defined rather than undefined behaviour. **NO public API, NO header outside this backend, NO shader, NO other backend and NO generated artifact changed** — two files, `SoftwareGraphicsBackend.{hpp,cpp}`. **CARDINALITY, measured not asserted:** a new env-gated `CNA_SOFTWARE_SAMPLE_TRACE` counts and dumps addressing; over the whole matrix, 130 triangles, 12485 point samples, 1504 linear samples, 18501 texel fetches — **exactly 1.0000 fetches per point sample and 4.0000 per linear sample**, with zero allocation and no extra draw, pass, frame, readback, texture copy or sampler application. **NEW `examples/point_sampling_contract_test.cpp`**, 26 legs comparing the COMPLETE destination image against an expectation computed by inverting the sprite/primitive geometry in double precision — no representative samples, no hash, no tolerance; uncovered pixels must still hold the clear sentinel. Where a scale unavoidably puts pixel centres on texel boundaries (an exact integer minification puts EVERY centre there) the leg accepts either neighbour and says so, and a blend of them still fails. Patterns are asymmetric, so a mirror, a transpose, a rotation, an off-by-one texel, a uniform fill and a stale buffer all fail distinguishably. Covers integer/non-integer/odd magnification, 1:1, minification, interior and one-texel source rectangles, fractional placement, transform scale and translation, all three flip combinations, rotation and origin, Clamp/Wrap/Mirror driven past `[0,1]` by an oversized source rectangle and by negative origins, the Point-vs-Linear and LinearWrap-vs-LinearClamp differentials, sampler transitions, two textures of different sizes in one batch, a RenderTarget2D source, alpha 0/64/128/200/255 under Opaque and AlphaBlend, the device `SamplerStates[0]` 3D path, a custom viewport, the TextureFilter ordinal table, and coordinates large enough to overflow the texel index. **SOFTWARE 80/134 -> 146/146**, byte-identical to EasyGL. **FALSE POSITIVES: 1 of 18 Software fixtures could see this at all.** Nine of the eighteen sample only a 1x1 or single-texel source, which is structurally blind to any filter. The one multi-texel sampling check, `software_effects_test.cpp` Check A, is titled "nearest-neighbor texture sampling" but **never selected a sampler** (so it ran under the device default, `LinearWrap`) and probed only (2,2) and (61,61), **both inside the corner cells where clamping collapses bilinear onto one texel**, with a +/-10 tolerance. A/B-proven by narrow file restoration: with `PointClamp` explicitly selected the original corner pair **still passes on the pre-fix backend**, while the added interior probe at (31,31) **fails** — which is exactly why the defect survived. It now selects PointClamp, compares byte-exactly, and carries its LinearClamp differential. It is also the ONLY existing test whose result changed, because honouring Wrap turns the device default from an effective clamp into real wrapping; new leg X measures that as a public differential and **EasyGL agrees with it**, so the change is the contract rather than a Software invention. **SANITIZERS**, runtimes proven linked (`libubsan.so.1`, `libasan.so.8`): UBSan clean on the full matrix and on `software_effects`, the build carrying `-fsanitize=float-cast-overflow`, with a negative control confirming that check does fire on an unguarded cast of the same magnitude; ASan clean on the matrix, `software_effects` and the render-target readback suite (146/146, 7/7, 91/91). Zero out-of-bounds texel access, zero overflow, zero invalid float-to-integer conversion, zero uninitialized weight, zero use-after-free. **REGRESSION GATES:** every Software graphics test green in the full `ctest` run; three failures (`XnbContainerFuzz`, `DoesNotSupportWireFrame`, `SetRenderTargets_FourTargets`) A/B-proven pre-existing and four Net/audio tests fail only under `-j2` and pass serially. GFX-119/124/125/148/149 remain separate and untouched. **CROSS-BACKEND CONTROLS**, only Software production changed: EASYGL 146/146, BGFX 146/146, D3D11 146/146 (Wine :99), HEADLESS 1/1 asserting the GFX-127 rejection, D3D12 cross-built only (DX-100). **THREE INDEPENDENT FINDINGS RECORDED, NOT FIXED: GFX-169 (Vulkan 140/146), GFX-170 (WebGPU and SDL_GPU 143/146 each), GFX-171 (D3D9 145/146)** — see their own rows. Commits: test `1a42a856`, fix `85386e41`, coverage `e20e77b3`, controls `d15b12f4`, docs (this). | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-169` | **Vulkan's stock 3D effects never received the public `SamplerState` — DONE (2026-07-30).** The ticket said the descriptor writes bind `defaultSampler_`. True, and only half of it: six builders take **NO sampler parameter at all**, so the sampler could be neither written into the descriptor **nor keyed in its cache**. Correcting only the write leaves a cache keyed on the image view alone, and the second draw with the same texture and a different sampler is handed the first draw's descriptor — measured, not theorised (leg K1 still failed with 64 in-block variations against a write-only fix). **This is the stock-3D half of a correction SpriteBatch already received:** `VulkanSpriteBatchBackend::FlushTexture`'s own comment records Task 665 fixing the identical defect there ("previously always used the texture's own pre-baked descriptor set, built once at texture-load time with a fixed default sampler, completely bypassing SetSamplerFilter/SetSamplerAddressMode"). `ApplySamplerState` was never the problem — it builds and caches a correct per-slot `VkSampler` from a complete XNA ordinal mapping, and **no enum-mapping defect was found**, so no ticket was raised for one. **THE COMPLETE INVENTORY**, established from the replay dispatch rather than from the ticket's count: `useAlphaTest -> descSet` (already correct, `GetOrCreateTexSamplerDescSet(view, slotSamplers_[0])`) and `useDualTexture -> dualTexDescSet` (already correct, and the model the fix follows) versus six broken — `useFogTex3D -> GetOrCreateFogTex3DDescSet` (slot 0), `useLitTextured -> GetOrCreateLitTexturedDescSet` (slot 0), `useSkinned -> GetOrCreateSkinnedDescSet` (slot 0), `useEnvMap -> GetOrCreateEnvMapDescSet` (slots 0 and 1), and `usePbr`/`usePbrSkinned -> GetOrCreatePbr(Skinned)DescSet` (slots 0..4 each). **15 combined-image-sampler bindings across 6 builders reached from 12 enqueue sites** (each builder is used by both the indexed and the non-indexed path). **`useFogTex3D` is set for ANY plain textured BasicEffect draw** — it is the fog-CAPABLE bundle, not "fog is on" — so the most ordinary textured 3D draw in the API bound a broken descriptor while the correct `descSet`, computed one line earlier, went unused. **PROVEN WITH A NEW `CNA_VULKAN_SAMPLER_TRACE`**, not inferred: slot 0 set to PointClamp resolves to `sampler=0x700000000070` with `default=0x1b000000001b`, and pre-fix `desc.FogTex3D key=0x4a000000004a slot=0 view=0x4a000000004a sampler=0x1b000000001b` — the descriptor got the DEFAULT, and **the key IS the image-view handle**, so both halves are visible in one line. Post-fix the same view under three samplers yields three keys and three descriptor sets. **FIX** = the `GetOrCreateDualTexDescSet` shape applied to all six: take the sampler(s) as parameters, fold them into the existing key hash, write them into the `VkDescriptorImageInfo`. Slot convention is DualTex's own, confirmed against EasyGL's `Texture0..Texture4` units. PBR snapshots slots 0..4 **by value** via `PbrSlotSamplersRawEXT()` rather than passing a pointer into member storage a later `ApplySamplerState` could mutate. **No public API, no shader, no other backend, no generated artifact, no descriptor caching disabled, no per-draw descriptor allocation, no CPU copy, no extra pass/draw/submit/wait/frame.** `defaultSampler_` remains for slots no `ApplySamplerState` has reached and for the fallback white/flat-normal textures. **RESULTS: VULKAN 32/65 -> 65/65** on the new `examples/stock_effect_sampler_contract_test.cpp`, whose oracle is modulation-independent (a 4x4 texture magnified 4x must give constant 4x4 blocks; lit families are driven ambient-only so the modulation is spatially uniform and the block structure measures the sampler, not the lighting model). **REMED-GFX-150's fixture went 140/146 -> 146/146 on VULKAN**, closing exactly the six legs that control had flagged. **CARDINALITY, measured:** 624 sampler applications produced only **4 `VkSampler` creations** (the sampler cache is untouched and still works), and each corrected family created **3 descriptor sets — one per distinct sampler state actually used, the minimum** (pre-fix: 1, because all samplers collapsed onto one). The two already-correct builders still report cache HITS (TexSampler 9, DualTexture 1), so reuse was not disabled. **VALIDATION:** `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` proven loaded (3 loader lines) and **zero** VUIDs, errors or warnings at 65/65. **SANITIZERS:** ASan and UBSan both 65/65 with the runtimes proven linked (`libasan.so.8`, `libubsan.so.1`) — no use-after-free, stale sampler pointer, invalid cache-key access, overflow or double release. **FALSE POSITIVES: 6 found, and they explain the survival exactly.** Of 191 Vulkan-registered fixtures only 19 touch `SamplerStates`, and **all six that are NAMED for the sampler contract (`easygl_sampler_state_effect_test`, `easygl_texture_filter_point_vs_linear_test`, the two `easygl_texture_address_mode_*_effect_test`s, `easygl_texture_anisotropic_effect_test`, `vulkan_texture_mip_filter_effect_test`) drive `DualTextureEffect` and nothing else** — the single stock family whose builder already took samplers. Vulkan's entire sampler-contract test surface was validating the one path that was not broken, while BasicEffect, lit-textured, EnvMap, Skinned, PBR and PBR-skinned went untested. They are correct tests of DualTexture and are left unchanged; the gap was coverage, and the new fixture fills it across all eight families. **One fixture defect of my own was found and A/B-proven:** leg K1's two draws each use a HALF-WIDTH viewport, so a texel is 2 destination pixels there, not 4 — measuring it with the full-width block size counted the genuine texel boundaries at x=2 and x=6 as failures, exactly 2 per row x 16 rows = the 32 a correct sampler still reported. Corrected, and against pre-fix production it still FAILS (64 variations), so it pins the defect. **No new ticket IDs were created.** GFX-170 (WebGPU/SDL_GPU) and GFX-171 (D3D9) remain open and their production is untouched.
