| `REMED-GFX-153` | **Bgfx's RenderTarget2D source-rectangle compensation mirrored the interval instead of the texture — DONE (2026-08-02).** The exact current-HEAD failure was reproduced before production changed: the final dedicated oracle passed **59/89** checks, with ordinary `Texture2D`, render-target readback, full-source rectangles, destination guards and symmetric selections all byte-exact, while every asymmetric partial `RenderTarget2D` selection matched the **whole-texture-mirrored source-region candidate** and not the final-destination flip, uncompensated-origin, wrong-row, poison, stale-generation or readback candidates. Top row selected logical row 6 and the final row selected row 0; a two-row case matched the mirrored-selection candidate **20/20** and the destination-flip/uncompensated-origin candidates **0/20**. **Exact mechanism:** REMED-GFX-067 correctly distinguished a render-target colour source from an ordinary uploaded texture and correctly gated on bgfx's `originBottomLeft`; the defect was only its CPU UV operation, `std::swap(v1, v2)`, which reverses the selected interval and equals an absolute origin conversion only for `[0,1]`. Source-rectangle normalization, shared SpriteBatch vertex/projection generation, destination-target orientation and readback were measured correct. **Narrow fix:** `SubmitSprite` now maps both absolute endpoints with `v = 1.0f - v` for render-target sources on bottom-left-origin renderers. Ordinary textures and non-bottom-left renderers are untouched; full-source rendering remains the same endpoint pair; public horizontal/vertical effects and rotation compose before the origin conversion. No shader, readback, other-backend production, public API, frame, Present, retry, wait or dummy bind changed. **Permanent coverage:** new shared `source_rectangle_orientation_test.cpp` is **89/89** on BGFX, EASYGL, SDL_GPU, VULKAN, WEBGPU and SOFTWARE and **3/3** on HEADLESS, covering asymmetric row/four-corner sources; ordinary versus target sources; target and backbuffer destinations; top/middle/bottom and final one-row rectangles; full/partial/scaled selections; horizontal/vertical/both effects; rotation; repeated target/backbuffer producer-consumer transitions; stale content; readback separation; and explicit/repeated disposal plus Game teardown. The two inherited false declarations were removed: `Bgfx_RenderTarget_SamplingOrientation` and producer-consumer H2 now assert exact equality. Focused Bgfx orientation/source/target/order/readback gates passed **33/33**; serial `^Bgfx` passed **176/179**, with only the same recorded DRI3-less requested-Vulkan MSAA/ClearOptions failures and Task 952 cube-depth failure. Linked ASan and UBSan both complete the dedicated **89/89** with zero memory-access or undefined-behaviour reports; leak-enabled ASan repeats the established bgfx/Mesa exit residual exactly (**103852 bytes / 453 allocations**). Bgfx diagnostics complete **89/89** with no new diagnostic class. GFX-138/154/155/157/158/163/179/181/195 remain green. `audit/` and every other backend's production are untouched. **No new finding ID was created.** Fix, fixture, false-oracle removal and closure are in this signed commit. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-154` | **The multisampled `RenderTarget2D` readback returned all zeroes on Bgfx — DONE (2026-07-31). THE TICKET SAID "FIRST READ"; IT IS ACTUALLY "WHICHEVER FRAMEBUFFER THE RENDERER STILL HAS CURRENT", AND THAT DIFFERENCE IS WHY A SECOND READ WAS BELIEVED TO CURE IT.** **Reconciled first:** `REMED-GFX-164` recorded the same all-zero MSAA readback on EasyGL AND Bgfx; on Bgfx both reach the same source handle, helper, blit view and result and neither reproduces without the other's conditions, so GFX-154 is canonical, GFX-164 keeps only its EasyGL scope, and the backlog counts ONE item. **Pre-fix:** the canonical sequence (4x MSAA, `DepthFormat::None`, Clear + asymmetric pattern, unbind, ONE `GetData`) returned **256/256 texels exactly `(0,0,0,0)`**; applied MSAA 4; active renderer **OpenGL 2.1**. **The second-read differential refutes the ticket's framing:** two reads of one target are BOTH zero and byte-identical, while reading two targets A,B,A,B gives **A exact on both reads and B zero on both** — binding B is what resolved A. **Root cause:** a renderer only VISITS a view owning a render item, and the readback view was left EMPTY, so its blit fell through to the tail `submitBlit` while the producer's framebuffer was still current; and `setFrameBuffer` is the ONLY caller of `FrameBufferGL::resolve()`, and only on a switch to a DIFFERENT framebuffer. The copy therefore read the freshly-created, zero-filled resolve texture and SUCCEEDED over untouched memory — the fabricated transparent-black result REMED-GFX-127/130 forbid. bgfx exposes no separate multisample handle, which is why every handle looked right. **Fix:** `bgfx::touch` the reserved readback view so the renderer reaches it, resolves the producer, and only then drains the blit — exactly what `ReadBackbuffer` has always done (Task 951) and this path never learned. Also replaced `AdvanceFramesUntil`'s magic four-attempt cap with a wait bounded by the completion generation `bgfx::readTexture` returns (`m_frameNum + 2`). **No priming read, discarded result, retry, extra public Present, extra public frame, dummy target switch or sleep; frame count per readback unchanged; the fix adds ONE empty submit.** **A/B: 6/34 legs -> 34/34, 109 PASS/64 FAIL -> 145/0.** New `examples/rendertarget_msaa_first_readback_test.cpp`, 34 process-isolated legs with a sentinel-guarded destination so "wrote nothing", "wrote zeroes" and "wrote out of range" stay separable. Matrix 0/1/2/4/8/16 samples, all four DepthFormats, all three usages, whole/rectangle/sub-rectangle/row/final-row/odd-13x7/startIndex/too-small shapes, and full lifetime coverage. **Five false positives re-armed** — `rendertarget_sampling_orientation_test` had encoded this very failure as a PASSING assertion plus a dummy-target nudge and a second read (61/62 -> 62/62); `rendertarget_depthstencil_usage` needed its contract field SPLIT because the multisampled CUBE route is still REMED-GFX-134's refusal (D8 SKIPPED -> measured, 29/29); `frontface_winding_test` re-armed itself, 127/127 with 0 skipped. Two defects in this task's own oracle were found and fixed before it was trusted. Diagnostics (new `CNA_BGFX_TRACE_DIAGNOSTICS`, since `traceVargs` had been discarding bgfx's whole warning stream) clean: 67704 trace lines, zero blit, readback, `readTexture`, framebuffer, resolve, view-ordering or destroyed-handle warnings, zero fatals. **CARDINALITY A/B-PROVEN:** with ONLY the touch removed and everything else identical, the trace reports the SAME completion generation 6 and the SAME `framesAdvanced=3` and the read is all-zero — so the touch is the sole cause of the fix and it adds **zero** frames; one empty submit is the entire cost. **SANITIZERS:** ASan 145/145 checks with **zero check-level failures and zero AddressSanitizer errors**; UBSan 145/145, exit 0, **zero runtime errors**; both runtimes proven linked by symbol (12 `__asan_report`, 14 `__ubsan_handle`). The LeakSanitizer residual has **zero CNA frames** (entirely bgfx's own render thread in `RendererContextGL::createUniform`) and is byte-identical at **103852 B / 453 allocations** to the value REMED-GFX-163 measured on an untouched binary. **CONTROLS, all 34/34 legs, exit 0, zero failures: BGFX 145, EASYGL 145, VULKAN 145, SDL_GPU 145, WEBGPU 142, SOFTWARE 145, HEADLESS 109** — Headless asserts the deterministic REFUSAL contract instead (it must throw and leave the destination untouched). bgfx **Vulkan UNTESTED** — this sandbox selects OpenGL. **EasyGL production unchanged, and its boundary was FALSIFIED rather than inherited:** the printed measurement showed EasyGL byte-exact, so the content is now ASSERTED there too (145/145) and REMED-GFX-164's EasyGL scope is narrowed to the never-covered-region case its own fixture still reproduces. Shard `ctest -R '^Bgfx' -j1` **166/172**, all six failures A/B-proven pre-existing against the unmodified backend; a seventh, `Bgfx_RenderTarget_SamplingOrientation`, failed post-fix and passed pre-fix precisely because it was the self-invalidating declaration, and is now green. **ONE NEW FINDING: `REMED-GFX-186`** (SDL_GPU SIGSEGVs reading mip level 1 of a mipmapped multisampled target; declared, not fixed). reconciliation `8f7e285a`, test `1b16aac6`, fix `8c632883`, false positives `4879ef81`, coverage/closure (this) | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-155` | **Bgfx executed a frame's views in numeric view-id order, not public submission order, so a BACKBUFFER consumer ran before its own same-frame producer — DONE (2026-07-29).** Not a visibility, synchronization or resource-identity defect. bgfx radix-sorts every draw by its view's SORT POSITION, which defaults to the numeric view id, and this backend's partition is `0` backbuffer / `[1,192)` render-target bases / `[192,255)` ordered segments / `255` reserved — so "ascending id" meant **the backbuffer runs first, then targets in id-ALLOCATION order**, and neither half is public order. **Measured, not inferred** (`CNA_BGFX_TRACE_VIEW_ORDER=1`, `DISPLAY=:101`, active renderer **OpenGL 2.1**): the canonical frame used views `1, 192, 0` in public order and executed them `0, 1, 192`; the consumer sampled an image nothing had rendered into and returned **0/32, all 32 texels `(0,0,0,0)`**, while the ordinary `Texture2D` control in the SAME batch was byte-exact. **A render-target consumer was correct only by accident:** a producer created before its consumer gets the lower id. The pre-fix failure map is that rule and nothing else — leg **C2** (backbuffer → target → backbuffer) and leg **G2** PASSED pre-fix because a same-frame rebind routes the LATER backbuffer batch onto an ordered segment id above every target (REMED-GFX-018/065), while the identical sequence whose consumer landed on base view 0 failed. **Fix: make the IDS ascend with public order rather than remap bgfx's sort.** REMED-GFX-018/065 already allocate ordered segment ids monotonically above every render-target base id; the whole correction is to honour that for the FIRST operation of every bind cycle too — a cycle may keep its target's base view only when that base is above every view already used this frame, else it takes the next ordered segment. Applied at the only two places a view becomes the active submission target, `SelectViewportSegment` (both draw paths) and `RecordClear`. **A first implementation using `bgfx::setViewOrder` (a full 256-id permutation, since `Frame::sort` inverts the table and a partial list is clobbered) was committed, measured and REPLACED** — identical public results, but it changes how bgfx sorts and decodes every draw's view field for a whole frame. Verified structurally as well as by pixels: with `CNA_BGFX_TRACE_VIEW_ORDER=1` every frame prints a **strictly increasing** id sequence, which under ascending-id execution IS the contract. **Cost:** one ordered segment id per out-of-turn bind cycle, which exhausted the 63-id pool in REMED-GFX-018/129's own gate, so the partition was rebalanced `kFirstSegmentViewId` **192 -> 64** — base ids are consumed per CONCURRENTLY LIVE render target (191 -> 63) while segments are consumed per bind cycle WITHIN ONE FRAME (63 -> 190); both still raise a clear error rather than wrapping. **Nothing was added:** no `bgfx::frame()`, Present, readback, wait, flush, submit-per-target-switch — and, in the shipped design, no `setViewOrder` either; `bgfx::frame()` per public frame stays **1**, max views in one frame stays **17**, and framebuffers/textures/programs/pipelines are unchanged. **New dedicated `rendertarget_backbuffer_consumer_test.cpp` 38/81 → 81/81**, twelve legs covering both consumer families, chains in both directions, a producer sampled twice, two producers in adverse orders, a target rebound between consumers, a cube bind cycle, SpriteBatch/3D interleaving, MSAA (applied 4) and mips (`LevelCount=4`), all three `RenderTargetUsage` values, and cardinality (eight bind cycles in one frame, eight consecutive frames). REMED-GFX-151's shared fixture **37/37 → 40/40**, legs D6 and I2 flipped from a declared boundary to an asserted contract. **Two false positives A/B-proven and strengthened:** `bgfx_render_target_sample_test.cpp` — the file NAMED for this exact sequence — printed "did not crash" with `main` returning 0 unconditionally, on the stale premise that bgfx had no readback path (**3/5 → 5/5**, pre-fix reporting ZERO texels of either producer band); and `bgfx_rasterizerstate_cullmode_test.cpp` carried two `device.Present();` calls, one commented *"Bgfx orders views by id; flush the off-screen view before the backbuffer samples it"* — a correct diagnosis of this defect written into test code as a workaround (**54/81 → 81/81** with both removed). Cross-backend controls, only bgfx production changed: **VULKAN 81/81** (GFX-151's fixture 44/44), **EASYGL 81/81**, **WEBGPU 84/84**, **SOFTWARE 77/77**, **SDL_GPU 4/4** (no `ReadBackbuffer`; REMED-GFX-152 blocks its 3D legs), **HEADLESS 3/3**. ASan and UBSan clean with both runtimes proved linked by symbol. All fourteen backend libraries build incrementally. **REMED-GFX-067 preserved and REMED-GFX-153 not started** (every leg samples a full-texture source rectangle, the case its `swap(v1,v2)` still handles); **REMED-GFX-154/138 untouched** (they concern readback, and no leg reads a producer). bgfx **Vulkan is untested** — this sandbox's bgfx selects OpenGL and a run reporting OpenGL is not a Vulkan result. `ctest -R '^Bgfx'` **142/147**, the five failures A/B-proven pre-existing against the pre-fix backend; four `CnaTests` failures on the same shard likewise A/B-proven pre-existing, and three `BuiltInVertexLayoutTest` shard timeouts pass standalone in 224 ms on both builds (display contention, not a result). Spawned **GFX-157** and **GFX-158**. test `aa48a315`, superseded fix `f9059270`, false positives `748624d9`, fix `64409791`, partition `c562521c` | HIGH | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-156` | **SDL_GPU and WebGPU: a `Clear()` issued AFTER a draw in the same bind cycle was lost — DONE (2026-07-29).** Both deliver a clear only through a native render pass's load action, and a pass has exactly one set of them, so the clear necessarily ran BEFORE every draw of the pass it belonged to; the canonical fixture scored **24/46** on SDL_GPU and **22/44** on WebGPU. **The symptom was identical and the cause was not the same cause.** On SDL_GPU `Clear()` was already an ordered command — REMED-GFX-145 gives every public bind cycle its own `PassSegment` carrying that cycle's clear request, and `drawOrder_` stamps every draw with its segment — but the unit that owns a load action was the BIND CYCLE, so a `Clear()` after a draw landed on the segment that draw was already in and `SDL_GPUColorTargetInfo.load_op` hoisted it in front of the draw it was meant to erase; "the last Clear wins" had the same single cause (one pass, one `clear_color`). On WebGPU `Clear()` was not in the command stream at all: three global `clearXPending_` flags and ONE value slot, read once when the cycle's single `WGPURenderPassDescriptor` was built, so two `Clear()`s collapsed to the second one's value and the ordered stream REMED-GFX-159 built carried draws only. **REMED-GFX-159 is what exposed the WebGPU half** — while the replay was family-grouped, `spritebatch_3d_order_test.cpp`'s clear leg SKIPPED behind the family-order gate, so a file with 80+ ordering checks measured no Clear behaviour on either backend. **Fix, both logical pass segmentation and never a fullscreen triangle:** SDL_GPU's `SegmentForOrderedClear()` closes the open segment and opens another over the SAME destination when `hasDraws` is set (set in `PushDrawOrder`, the one choke point every queued draw routes through) and folds the request in when it is not; WebGPU's `RecordOrderedClear()` appends an `OrderedClearCommand` — aspects AND values captured at the public call — plus an `OrderedKind::Clear` entry addressing it BY INDEX, and `BuildPassSegments()`/`ReplayOrderedSegments()` cut the stream into one native pass per observable Clear, collapsing three near-identical pass bodies into one loop over a `PassDestination`. `clearXPending_` is kept on both because it carries a second, unrelated meaning ("just recreated, holds undefined bytes"). **Exactly one extra native render pass per observable mid-cycle Clear and NONE for a leading one or a run of Clears** — no extra submit, wait, `Present`, frame, readback, geometry draw or order-keyed pipeline, verified from env-gated native order traces rather than inferred from pixels. One store action needed correcting: `SDL_GPU_STOREOP_RESOLVE` leaves multisample contents undefined and the next segment LOADs them, so `RESOLVE_AND_STORE` is taken when a later segment reloads the same colour resource (REMED-GFX-141's cube correction, extended to 2D). **24/46 → 46/46 and 22/44 → 44/44**, with VULKAN/BGFX/EASYGL/D3D9/D3D11 49/49, SOFTWARE 41/41 and HEADLESS 35/35 as controls; every draw entry point and every stock effect measured ACROSS the boundary by byte-exact parity against the same draw in a fresh pass. **SIX fixtures declared this property and five asserted the defective shape; the sixth SKIPPED instead**, and the two gates that could hide a regression are now `static_assert`ed so a future backend must restore the declaration rather than re-introduce a skip. Shards `SdlGpu` 57/58 and `WebGPU` 55/56, both failures A/B-proven pre-existing. Validation clean (SDL_gpu debug mode + Vulkan layer live; zero WebGPU uncaptured errors). ASan/UBSan clean with every leak A/B-classified byte-for-byte identical or lower. WebGPU and SDL_GPU stencil gating stay declared unsupported and no stencil work was started. test `82b62e92`, WebGPU fix `fb25723a`, SDL_GPU fix `e313bbfb` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-157` | **A bind cycle's SpriteBatch draws and its stock 3D draws were replayed GROUPED BY FAMILY instead of in public order — DONE (2026-07-29).** **The ticket's own statement was wrong in every particular and is corrected here.** It claimed "a stock 3D draw issued AFTER a SpriteBatch never reaches the target, on BGFX/VULKAN/SOFTWARE/EASYGL, WEBGPU clean". Measured: that draw was **CULLED, not lost**. REMED-GFX-155's leg I0 called `SpriteBatch.Begin(..., nullptr, nullptr)`, and a null rasterizerState means `RasterizerState.CullCounterClockwise` (FNA `SpriteBatch.cs`: `rasterizerState ?? RasterizerState.CullCounterClockwise`), which `PrepRenderState` assigns to the device and **never restores** — XNA-correct behaviour — and the leg then drew a quad of exactly the winding four backends cull under it. Proven three ways: giving the batch an explicit `CullNone` makes the identical draw land byte-exact; restoring ONLY `RasterizerState` after `End()` does the same; and the draw's fate after a SpriteBatch is byte-identical to its fate under an explicit `CullCounterClockwise`. **WEBGPU looked "clean" only because its front-face winding is INVERTED relative to the other four** (spawned GFX-160). **The real defect is the opposite direction and a different backend set:** `3D draw; SpriteBatch.Draw` inside ONE cycle came out inverted on **VULKAN and BGFX**, which replay all sprites and then all 3D draws; **SOFTWARE and EASYGL were always correct**; and **WEBGPU groups the other way round** (all 3D, then all sprites), so it inverts `SpriteBatch; 3D` instead (spawned GFX-159, not changed — this task was scoped not to touch WebGPU production). This is REMED-GFX-143's check O3 finding, which named the subject but only ever measured the direction that already worked. **Fix, Vulkan:** `drawSpritesFor`/`draw3DFor` already take REMED-GFX-129's half-open order range and `commandOrder_` is one monotonic counter shared by batches, 3D draws and clears, so each pass now splits its range at every family change and calls the existing lambdas once per same-family RUN; neither family's replay changes and the shared arena cursors still accumulate. **Fix, bgfx:** draws are submitted immediately into a view and bgfx's DEFAULT view mode radix-sorts a view's draws by sort key, discarding submission order — every view id is set to `ViewMode::Sequential` once at init, the within-view half of REMED-GFX-155's between-view ordering. **Nothing added:** no pass, submit, barrier, Present, flush, wait or extra frame; the only new cost is one pipeline bind per family switch and one small per-slice vector on Vulkan. **New dedicated `spritebatch_3d_order_test.cpp`**, whose oracle is a STAIRCASE — step i covers stripes [0, N-i), so one readback proves every draw happened AND that they happened in the issued order — with full-height stripes (Y-flip-proof), a 0/255-only palette (byte-exact under sRGB) and a PARITY oracle for the effect and draw-entry-point legs that needs no prediction of any effect's shading. **Vulkan 42/60 → 60/60, Bgfx 41/60 → 60/60**, Software 61/61 and EasyGL 60/60 already correct, WebGPU 39/39 and SdlGpu 40/40 with their boundaries declared, Headless 2/2. **Two false positives A/B-proven and strengthened:** leg I0 of `rendertarget_backbuffer_consumer_test.cpp` (Software 77→86, Vulkan/EasyGL/Bgfx 81→90, WebGPU 84→87) and check O3 of `backbuffer_pass_order_test.cpp`, which gained check **O4** for the untested direction and had "keeps public order" factored apart from "which family replays first". `ctest -R '^Vulkan'` **182/183** and `ctest -R '^Bgfx'` **143/148**, every failure A/B-proven pre-existing against `95bd73ac^`. ASan/UBSan clean on both backends with runtimes proved linked by symbol (the only ASan report is a 368-byte driver-side leak that reproduces identically in an untouched control fixture, with no CNA frame). Vulkan validation layer 1.4.309 live and silent. All fourteen backend libraries build. Spawned **GFX-159**, **GFX-160**, **GFX-161** and **GFX-162**. test `67b9fd1f`, fix `95bd73ac`, false positives `53b99dea` | HIGH | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-158` | **Bgfx: `bgfx::reset()` discards EVERY view's framebuffer binding, including the one just programmed for the render target bound right now, so a `RenderTarget2D` constructed and rendered into in the same public frame lost that frame — DONE (2026-07-29).** Not deferred resource creation, not a bgfx frame latency, not the mip chain, and not REMED-GFX-155's view ordering. `Context::reset` (bgfx_p.h) ends with `for (ii < BGFX_CONFIG_MAX_VIEWS) m_view[ii].setFrameBuffer(BGFX_INVALID_HANDLE)`, and bgfx resolves view state once, at `bgfx::frame()` — so operations already submitted against that view do not follow the target they were aimed at, they resolve against the BACKBUFFER, and the target is never written. This backend calls `bgfx::reset()` from `EnsureViewState()` the moment the SDL window's real size differs from the size bgfx was initialised with, which for a brand-new target's first bind cycle lands **between the bind and its first draw**. **Measured at the call site** (`DISPLAY=:101`, active renderer **OpenGL 2.1**): `bgfx::reset 800x480 -> 64x64 (currentViewId_=1 segmentTargetBaseId_=1)`, frame used views `1, 64`, and the target read back **(0,0,0,0)** — not even its own `DiscardContents` clear `(0,0,0,255)`, so the bind cycle's FIRST operation was lost too. **Everything the ticket recorded follows from that and none of it was the cause:** creating the target one frame earlier cures it because any public operation before the bind absorbs the reset while no target is bound (a bare `Clear` is enough, which is what a warm-up frame always contains); the texture and framebuffer are both complete before the frame's draws are submitted (`Context::renderFrame` executes `m_cmdPre` first); `mipMap=false` reproduces; and a target whose content lands on an ordered SEGMENT view — programmed AFTER the reset — survives the very same frame untouched, which is exactly why REMED-GFX-155's own fixture (whose `ProduceInto` clears before drawing) was green while `bgfx_rendertarget2d_mip_test.cpp` (whose draw is the cycle's first operation) needed a warm-up. **It is not really a first-frame defect either:** any reset does it — a genuine window resize, or a vsync change through `SetSwapInterval`, at any point in a program's life. **Fix: make the loss recoverable rather than avoid the reset.** Every view→framebuffer binding this backend programs goes through `Detail::SetViewFrameBufferEXT`, which mirrors it (all ten call sites); `Detail::ResetBackbufferEXT` replays the mirror straight after the reset at both reset sites. bgfx view state is context state that survives a frame and the only other writer is reset itself, so the mirror IS the intended state. `Detail::ForgetFrameBufferEXT` drops a framebuffer from the mirror before it is destroyed — 2D destructor, cube destructor, all three MRT destroys — so a recycled bgfx handle index can never be replayed as a resurrected binding. **Cost, measured with counters:** over the whole 16-leg run, **106** mirrored bindings (array stores) and **exactly 1** extra `bgfx::setViewFrameBuffer`, from **2** resets; frame advances, views, framebuffers, submits, readbacks and waits are all unchanged, and nothing was added — no `bgfx::frame()`, Present, GetData, wait, sleep, flush or dummy submit. **New dedicated `rendertarget_first_use_test.cpp`**, sixteen legs, 21/22 → 26/26 on bgfx. The resolution settles once per process, so only a process's FIRST bind cycle can observe the defect; `--leg=<id>` runs one leg alone to put it in first position, and **four separate ctest cases are red pre-fix and green after** — A1 (a draw first), A3 (a public `Clear` first, `PreserveContents`), A4 (the bind's own discard clear alone) and J (a stock 3D draw first), each **0/32 → 32/32**. A fifth, leg N, covers the second registration path (`BindAsRenderTargetFace`, one framebuffer per cube face) through the same reset. **Two false positives A/B-proven and strengthened**, both in `bgfx_rendertarget2d_mip_test.cpp`: a documented warm-up cycle (throwaway target + `Clear` + one-pixel `GetBackBufferData`) ahead of its first measurement, and a 20-attempt retry loop breaking on the first non-black read — without the warm-up and without the fix its check 1 reads `top=(0,0,0) bottom=(0,0,0)` on all twenty attempts, with the fix `top=(255,0,0) bottom=(0,0,255)` on a single read; both workarounds removed. That file's inherited claim that a RenderTarget2D sampled in more than one `Clear`+draw+`GetBackBufferData` cycle goes black after the first was **measured (leg C2) and does not hold on any backend** — the comment is corrected rather than repeated. **A/B against `4eba39fc^`:** pre-fix the five FirstUse cases and `Bgfx_RenderTarget2D_MipChain` fail and the same five pre-existing failures fail; post-fix only those five remain. `ctest -R '^Bgfx'` **150/155**, the five A/B-proven pre-existing (`RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`, `RenderTargetCube_MipChain`, `RenderTargetCube_MsaaResolve`, `RenderTargetCube_DepthFormat`, `GraphicsDevice_ClearOptions_Vulkan`). Cross-backend controls, only bgfx production changed: **VULKAN 26/26, EASYGL 26/26, WEBGPU 26/26, SOFTWARE 25/25** (cannot bind a cube face), **SDL_GPU 17/17** (no `ReadBackbuffer`; REMED-GFX-152 blocks its stock-3D legs), **HEADLESS 16/16**; D3D9/D3D11 cross-build but their Wine route cannot init SDL video on Xvfb `:101` (`No displays available`, identical for a pre-existing D3D9 window test) so they are **environment-unavailable, not passing**. ASan and UBSan **26/26 with zero reports**, runtimes proved linked by symbol, over the fixture plus the GFX-155/151/157 fixtures, the mip test, concurrent targets and the cube GetData contract. REMED-GFX-155 view order, REMED-GFX-157 family order and REMED-GFX-160 winding all preserved. All fourteen backend libraries build incrementally. bgfx **Vulkan is untested** — `CNA_BGFX_RENDERER=VULKAN` still reports `active renderer: OpenGL 2.1` here. test `fb7c1d17`, fix `4eba39fc`, false positives `1cbb2a10`, cube/boundaries `e9ff8a3a`, D3D controls `80cc9f02` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-159` | **A bind cycle's draws were replayed GROUPED BY FAMILY on all three WebGPU pass recorders — DONE (2026-07-29).** **The ticket is correct but INCOMPLETE, and the fuller statement is here.** Root cause: `EnsureFrameRendered`, `RenderPendingDrawsToRenderTarget` and `RenderPendingDrawsToRenderTargetCubeFace` each replayed a cycle as a FIXED LIST of eleven per-family loops — colored, textured, litTextured, alphaTest, dualTexture, envMap, instanced, pbr, skinned, skinnedPbr, then sprites — justified in a comment as *"3D draws first, 2D SpriteBatch/UI on top — matches typical XNA game draw order"*. A typical order is not a contract. **There was no order counter anywhere in the backend**, so the SOURCE order of those eleven calls decided execution order. **What the ticket misses: the ten 3D families were ordered against each other by the same mechanism**, so `textured BasicEffect; vertex-colour BasicEffect` inverted with no SpriteBatch anywhere — measured, on four effect pairs in both directions, each reverse ordering rendering correctly by accident. **Fix:** every command struct, every capture and every `Queue*Draw()` is unchanged; one stream of `(family, slot, public order)` references is appended at the public call and replay walks it, dispatching to per-family single-command `Issue*Draw` functions (the ten former loops, unchanged). References are INDICES — the family vectors grow while a cycle records and a pointer into one would dangle on reallocation. Three pieces of pass state each family could previously assume untouched are handled at a boundary: the sprite path's redundant-pipeline-bind skip is tracked across ALL families; the shared sprite vertex buffer is rebound once per sprite RUN; and the pass-level blend constant is restored before a 3D draw, so a 3D draw sees byte-identically what it saw before. **Nothing added:** no pass, submit, wait, barrier, flush, Present, target switch or extra frame, no pipeline variant keyed on order, no per-vertex or per-sprite allocation, and no sort. Measured: 8 and 16 alternating draws each cost exactly **1 render pass, 1 submit, 0 new pipelines, 1 native setViewport, 1 native setScissorRect** on backbuffer, target and cube face; the whole fixture issues an identical **191 draws over 101 passes** before and after. **Ordering was MEASURED NATIVELY, not inferred from pixels:** a new env-gated trace (`CNA_WEBGPU_TRACE_DRAW_ORDER`) records public enqueue number, family, slot, destination and native issue position, with the public position stored in the entry so a reordering replay reports the two columns differing instead of relabelling itself. Pre-fix, the five-alternation sequence enqueued 0,1,2,3,4 and issued **1,3,0,2,4**; post-fix **all 191 draws across 101 passes issue at exactly their public position**. **WEBGPU 27/69 → 67/67.** New leg N (cross-effect ordering) and new `webgpu_draw_order_cardinality_test.cpp` **21/21**, whose leg D7 covers the cube-face recorder by pixels in both directions (A/B-proven: D7a fails pre-fix with the sprite on top, D7b passes pre-fix). **Two false positives A/B-proven and strengthened:** `backbuffer_pass_order_test.cpp`'s `mixedQueuesKeepPublicOrder` (29/30 pre-fix → 30/30) and `rendertarget_backbuffer_consumer_test.cpp` leg I0 (86/87 → 87/87). `ctest -R '^WebGPU'` **54/55**, the one failure (`WebGPU_Clear_Readback`, alpha-blend and address-mode checks) A/B-proven pre-existing with byte-identical failures. ASan+UBSan clean with runtimes proved linked by symbol (39 `__asan_*`, 15 `__ubsan_*`): 0 UBSan runtime errors, and the exit leak is **63 allocations both pre- and post-fix** (10014 B → 9758 B), with the cardinality fixture — which stresses the interleaved path hardest — reporting the untouched control's exact 368 B / 4 allocs. WebGPU validation silent (0 uncaptured errors). Cross-backend controls unchanged: Software 70/70, EasyGL 69/69, Vulkan 69/69, Bgfx 69/69, SdlGpu 49/49, Headless 2/2, and real-D3D runtime controls on `:101` under Wine — D3D9 30/30, D3D11 30/30. All fourteen backend libraries build. **One independent finding recorded, not fixed:** WebGPU's mid-cycle `Clear` is load-op-only, so a `Clear()` after a draw in one cycle cannot wipe it — the counterpart of REMED-GFX-129's Vulkan work, deliberately not started. test `e822b15a`, fix `2910cacd`, false positives `fae3ccfa`, cube face `a1c8353f` | HIGH | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-160` | **The stock 3D front-face winding was inverted on WEBGPU ONLY, not on the four backends the ticket accused — DONE (2026-07-29). RECLASSIFIED: THE TICKET WAS EXACTLY BACKWARDS.** Its premise was wrong at the source. It claimed FNA's SpriteBatch lays its corners out `TL, BL, TR, BR`, making its first triangle `TL→BL→TR`, and that the accusing fixture's `TL→BL→BR` quad was "the SAME winding". FNA `SpriteBatch.cs` actually declares `CornerOffsetX = { 0, 1, 0, 1 }` and `CornerOffsetY = { 0, 0, 1, 1 }` — **TL, TR, BL, BR** — and `GenerateVertexInfo()` builds `Position0..3` from exactly that progression, so with `GenerateIndexArray()`'s `j, j+1, j+2 / j+3, j+2, j+1` the sprite triangles are **`TL→TR→BL`** and **`BR→BL→TR`**, both **CLOCKWISE as displayed**. Since `SpriteBatch.Begin` defaults its rasterizer state to `RasterizerState.CullCounterClockwise` and sprites are visible, **clockwise-as-displayed is XNA's FRONT face and each enum names the face it CULLS**. The fixture's `TL→BL→BR` quad is the OPPOSITE winding — an XNA **BACK** face — so the four backends culling it under `CullCounterClockwise` were **correct all along**. **Two independent FNA3D drivers agree by different routes:** the OpenGL driver's `XNAToGL_FrontFace = { -, GL_CW, GL_CCW }` with `glCullFace(GL_BACK)` (GL window space is y-UP, so GL's "CCW" is displayed-CW), and the D3D11 driver's `FrontCounterClockwise = 1` combined with a swapped cull enum `{ NONE, CULL_BACK, CULL_FRONT }` — a double inversion netting to the same contract. CNA's own public docs (`RasterizerState.hpp`, `CullMode.hpp`) already said the same. **Root cause, WEBGPU:** every 3D pipeline sets `frontFace = WGPUFrontFace_CCW` and WebGPU decides facing from the signed area in FRAMEBUFFER space (Y **down**), so its front face is the displayed-CCW one; `ToWGPUCullMode` then mapped `CullClockwiseFace→Front` and `CullCounterClockwiseFace→Back`, both backwards. **Fix = swapping those two arms**, nothing else: `frontFace` untouched on every pipeline, no geometry rewritten, no index buffer reversed, no pipeline variant added or removed, no pass/submit/draw/allocation added, identical cost. It escaped notice because the **SpriteBatch pipeline hardcodes `WGPUCullMode_None`**, so only the stock 3D path could show it. **New dedicated `frontface_winding_test.cpp`:** two triangles in DISJOINT quadrants wound opposite ways, so one readback classifies both; reversing a triangle's three indices keeps coverage identical so "culled" is never confounded with "moved"; the two never-covered quadrants are asserted clear in every leg (mirror detector); `CullNone` asserted first (dead-probe detector); complementarity asserted as its own check (cull-all/cull-nothing detector). Covers backbuffer, RenderTarget2D, MSAA, destination transitions, seven draw entry points incl. nonzero `vertexStart`/`startIndex`/`baseVertex` and `TriangleStrip`, four stock effects, A→B→A state transitions, a distinct-but-equal `RasterizerState`, depth/blend independence, and a SpriteBatch reference leg; the transform leg compares the world matrix's **determinant sign** against the observed facing and probes the transformed centroid, so an intentional mirror is never compensated twice. **WEBGPU 52/119 → 119/119.** Unchanged and already correct: **SOFTWARE 119/119, EASYGL 119/119, VULKAN 127/127, BGFX 119/119, SDL_GPU 17/17** (render-target legs; no public backbuffer readback), **HEADLESS 3/3**. **REAL-D3D RUNTIME CONTROLS RUN AND GREEN** on `DISPLAY=:101` under Wine/DXVK: the project's own pre-existing `cna_test_d3d9_rasterizerstate_cullmode` and `cna_test_d3d11_rasterizerstate_cullmode` each **6/6 PASS**, and both already measure exactly this contract over BOTH windings and all three modes — `CullCounterClockwiseFace` (default) keeps CW and culls CCW, `CullClockwiseFace` does the reverse. Those two tests pre-date this ticket and were passing throughout, which is independent runtime proof on XNA's own native API that the four accused backends matched the contract and WebGPU did not. D3D9/D3D11/D3D12 source agrees (`D3DStateMapping`: `FrontCounterClockwise = FALSE` with `CullClockwiseFace→CULL_FRONT`; D3D9 maps `D3DCULL_CW`/`D3DCULL_CCW` directly). **Three false positives A/B-proven and strengthened, all one class** (a quad whose winding was asserted from a hand derivation rather than measured, drawn under a cull mode then inverted to keep it visible): `webgpu_graphicsstate_test.cpp` checks A/B — **the origin of the production defect**, whose comment derived "CCW in NDC" correctly then concluded "CLOCKWISE in raster space ⇒ ordinary XNA front-facing quad" (the name a GPU API attaches to that sign is defined in ITS OWN y-down space, so it means displayed-CCW, while XNA's enums name the DISPLAYED orientation) — now four checks over BOTH windings, 8→10; the same file's scissor checks E/H, which built a fresh `RasterizerState` (defaulting to `CullCounterClockwiseFace`) and so silently required a BACK face to stay visible — `DrawWindingQuad`'s default is now the FRONT-facing quad; and `webgpu_instanced3d_test.cpp`, whose `kSmallQuadIdx` was the back-facing order while the test never sets a `RasterizerState` at all. REMED-GFX-157's own leg M5 constant `kCcwCullsSpriteWoundQuad` — which declared four backends defective — is deleted and M5 now asserts the contract unconditionally. **ASan+UBSan clean** with runtimes proved linked by symbol: SOFTWARE 119/119 exit 0 with ZERO reports on both; the WebGPU ASan total is a wgpu-native-internal process-exit leak measured **byte-identical (1257822 B / 729 allocs) with the swap applied and reverted**. `ctest -R '^WebGPU'` **53/54**, the one failure (`WebGPU_Clear_Readback`: sprite alpha blend, AddressMode Wrap/Mirror) A/B-proven pre-existing. All fourteen backend libraries build incrementally. Boundaries declared, not absorbed: SOFTWARE rejects `TriangleStrip`; EASYGL and BGFX MSAA render targets read back all-zero rather than the clear colour; HEADLESS does not reject the backbuffer readback (REMED-GFX-162). Independent findings recorded: **bgfx asserts on an MSAA render target that also carries a depth format**, and **WEBGPU/SDL_GPU reject a correctly sized `GetBackBufferData` for a 64x64 backbuffer**. test `ca42099a`, fix `804dd446`, false positives `8121d59d` | HIGH | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-161` | **WebGPU: the FIRST `GraphicsDevice.GetBackBufferData` of a process left part of the caller's buffer unwritten — DONE (2026-07-30), resolved by REMED-GFX-165's shared fix.** Measured once per run and never again: the first backbuffer readback returned successfully while leaving the fixture's poison prefill in place; every later read in the same run was correct. **Root cause conclusively proven to be the SHARED (not WebGPU-local) rectangle-less dimension source that REMED-GFX-165 already corrected**, not a native copy/map/CPU-copy defect. Pre-fix, `GraphicsDevice::GetBackBufferData` sized a rectangle-less region from `backend_->GetViewportSize()`, evaluated BEFORE the read's own `EnsureFrameRendered` realises the surface — so on the FIRST frame WebGPU's aspect-scaled logical viewport differs from the authoritative backbuffer, and when it is NARROWER the region is undersized and a trailing block of the destination stays poison (when WIDER it is instead rejected — REMED-GFX-165's own symptom; same root cause, opposite rounding). **A/B-reproduced deterministically:** with the pre-fix `GetViewportSize()` sizing restored, a 64×32 first read reported `viewport=53×32`, sized the region 53×32=1696, returned success and left elements idx [1696..2047] (352 elements, coord (32,26)..(63,31)) at the sentinel; a 37×23 first read reported `viewport=38×23` and was rejected as "too small". On the CURRENT code (which sizes from `PresentationParameters`) both are byte-exact, and the second read was always correct because the first read's own `EnsureFrameRendered` updates the physical size. **No WebGPU/SDL_GPU/Vulkan/EasyGL/Bgfx production change was made or needed.** New `backbuffer_first_read_test.cpp` (13 process-isolated sentinel legs: canonical/second/clear-only/several-draws first reads, explicit-rectangle full/one-row/final-row/one-pixel, prefix+suffix guards, 63/64/65 row-pitch boundaries, and the 64×32 REMED-GFX-157 repro size) — 13/13 on WebGPU, Software, Vulkan, EasyGL, SDL_GPU (proxy preserved) and Bgfx (its sub-rectangle read declared a boundary), Headless declares its non-rasterizing boundary. The now-unnecessary `kFirstBackbufferReadIsWarmUp` discarded read was removed from `spritebatch_3d_order_test.cpp` (WebGPU leg A now measures the first read directly, 84/84). WebGPU validation clean (no uncaptured-error/device-lost); ASan+UBSan clean (only an external 368-byte/4-alloc driver leak, A/B-identical to the untouched dimension fixture, no CNA frame); `ctest -L WebGPU` 60/61 (the one failure `WebGPU_Clear_Readback` pre-existing and unrelated). No new ticket created. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE (root cause = REMED-GFX-165; measured 2026-07-29 by REMED-GFX-157, closed 2026-07-30) |
| `REMED-GFX-162` | **Headless `GraphicsDevice.GetBackBufferData` FABRICATED a frame instead of rejecting — DONE (2026-07-30).** REMED-GFX-127/130 made the texture and render-target readbacks REJECT on a backend that rasterizes nothing, rather than hand back scratch as a rendered frame; the BACKBUFFER readback was never brought under that contract. **The prior "neither rejects nor writes" description was imprecise — the red-first oracle proves it WROTE:** `HeadlessGraphicsBackend::ReadBackbuffer` filled the caller's buffer with the last `Clear()` colour for every pixel and returned success, so a caller could not tell a non-rasterizing device from a real black/last-cleared frame (A/B-reproduced: a 37×23 read reported `success` and MUTATED all 851 requested elements from their per-element guards to the clear colour (17,34,51,255), leaving a non-zero-startIndex prefix/suffix intact — a fabricated frame, not an untouched buffer). **Fix = Headless-only, one production method:** `ReadBackbuffer` now `throw`s `System::NotSupportedException` (the exact type the shared layer already throws for a Texture/RT readback a backend cannot satisfy) instead of fabricating. The refusal is raised at the END of `GraphicsDevice::GetBackBufferData`'s validation chain, so **capability rejection stays LAST** — a null destination still throws `std::invalid_argument`, an out-of-bounds/overflow-shaped rectangle `std::out_of_range`, and an undersized element count `std::runtime_error`, all BEFORE the backend is consulted, matching the texture path's precedence exactly. **No shared production, no rasterizing backend, no public API, no `GraphicsCapability` enum touched** (Architecture B via `SupportsCapability` was rejected: SDL_RENDERER/DX3/Canvas/Ascii return `false` for every capability yet have real readback, so a shared-layer capability gate would wrongly reject them). **GetBackBufferData is Color-only** — no templated element type, so no per-type matrix. New process-isolated `backbuffer_headless_reject_test.cpp` (12 fork+exec legs: rectangle-less/full-rect/sub-rect/one-pixel/non-zero-startIndex/oversized reads + repeated + null/undersized/negative-rect/beyond-rect/INT_MAX-rect precedence, with distinct per-element destination guards proving prefix/range/suffix integrity): **HEADLESS 12/12** rejects + leaves the destination completely untouched; **SOFTWARE/WEBGPU/SDL_GPU/VULKAN/BGFX/EASYGL 12/12** succeed and fully write (Bgfx sub-rect a declared GFX-165 boundary; Bgfx's double-buffered second read is content-free per GFX-161's A3). **Three false positives strengthened**, all having relied on the fabricated success: `spritebatch_3d_order_test.cpp`'s `kBackbufferReadRejectsWhenNonRasterizing` (was `false` for Headless — declared "does not reject", now `true` for every backend), `backbuffer_readback_dimension_test.cpp`'s `CheckSizedOk` (now asserts the Headless rejection instead of a fabricated-size success), and `backbuffer_first_read_test.cpp`'s `Readable` (its silent "oracle unavailable" boundary is now an asserted rejection). ASan+UBSan clean on Headless AND Software (runtimes proved linked; zero leaks/UB); EasyGL logic clean with `detect_leaks=0`, its 100956 B/449-alloc GL-driver leak A/B-identical in an unrelated control. Full **Headless suite 31/32** — the sole failure `Headless_Smoke` is pre-existing (an unrelated `DrawIndexedPrimitives` `primitiveCount` abort, no backbuffer readback), red at baseline before any change. No new ticket ID created. test `b157db77`, fix `83590c80`, false positives `5f3bc0ed`, docs (this). | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-163` | **A depth-backed MULTISAMPLED `RenderTarget2D` killed the process on Bgfx — DONE (2026-07-31). THE FIX ALREADY EXISTED ONE SCREEN AWAY, ON THE CUBE PATH; THE 2D SIBLING NEVER LEARNED IT.** **Duplicate reconciled first:** `REMED-GFX-184` was this same defect re-derived two days later by GFX-180's controls — same construction, backend, constructor, assertion and signal. GFX-163 is canonical, GFX-184 is retired as a DUPLICATE with its text kept for provenance, and the backlog counts ONE item. **PRE-FIX FATAL:** `RenderTarget2D(dev, 16, 16, false, Color, Depth24, 4, DiscardContents)` -> **SIGTRAP, exit 133, core dumped**, `bgfx.cpp(5116): ASSERT isOk() -> ErrorAssert: 0x02006762 'Frame buffer depth MSAA texture cannot be resolved…'`, inside `bgfx::createFrameBuffer` -> `Context::createFrameBuffer` -> `isFrameBufferValid`. Active renderer **OpenGL 2.1** (Xvfb :101). Colour carried `RT_MSAA_X4 | SAMPLER_U_CLAMP | SAMPLER_V_CLAMP`; depth carried `RT_MSAA_X4` **alone**. **BGFX CONTRACT read from the installed source:** RT and RT_MSAA_X2..X16 are values 1..5 of ONE 3-bit field at shift 36 (an MSAA flag already implies RT), `m_numSamples = 1 << satSub(field,1)`; `isFrameBufferValid` exempts a field of 1 and otherwise REQUIRES `RT_WRITE_ONLY` or `MSAA_SAMPLE` because multisampled depth cannot be resolved — and separately requires colour and depth to report the SAME sample count, so reducing depth's count would only trade one assertion for another. **ROOT CAUSE = the missing `BGFX_TEXTURE_RT_WRITE_ONLY`**, A/B-proven: adding it alone takes 6/31 legs to 31/31. WRITE_ONLY (not MSAA_SAMPLE) is this texture's own truth — CNA never samples, reads back or resolves a target's depth — and bgfx's own Vulkan swapchain uses exactly that combination. Applied only at 2+ samples, because bgfx's GL renderer swaps a texture for a renderbuffer when `writeOnly` is set and the 1x path must not change. **FIX:** two shared helpers (`BgfxColorAttachmentFlags`, `BgfxDepthAttachmentFlags`) replacing duplicated inline arithmetic at BOTH target types, so the rule cannot be learned by only one again; the cube refactor is behaviour-preserving with **byte-identical output** pre/post on five binaries. No depthless or non-MSAA fallback, no sample-count reduction, no extra frame/wait/Present/retry/readback, no public API change, one production file. **MATRIX (requested->applied):** 1x->0, 2x->2, 3x->2, 4x->4, 8x->8, 16x->16, 64x->16, every DepthFormat, mips included. **DEPTH FUNCTIONAL AT 4x WITH A FALSIFYING CONTROL:** D2 reverses the public draw order and the near quad still wins, while D3 disables the test and the farther quad wins — so D2 cannot pass for the wrong reason; stencil, depth-write-off, depth-only and colour-only Clear all pass. **ORACLE never reads the multisampled target** (GFX-154/164 own that); leg S3's four-cell calibration immediately refuted a wrong "MSAA does not resolve" hypothesis and located a FIXTURE defect instead — a counter-clockwise, silently culled sampling quad. **New 31-leg process-isolated `rendertarget_msaa_depth_contract_test.cpp`, no expected-fatal gate.** **Controls all 31/31, exit 0: BGFX 81, EASYGL 81, SDL_GPU 81, VULKAN 80, SOFTWARE 78, WEBGPU 76, HEADLESS 67.** **THREE FALSE POSITIVES RE-ARMED**, each of which had declared this abort instead of measuring it: `bgfx_rendertarget2d_msaa_test` (every MSAA target was `DepthFormat::None`), `rendertarget_depthstencil_usage_test` (`msaaDepthRT2D` false on BGFX -> true, 29/29), `present_lifecycle_contract_test` (`kMsaaTargetMayHaveDepth` false on BGFX -> true, 82/82). **ASan and UBSan 31/31, zero errors**, runtimes proven linked; the leak residual has zero CNA frames and is byte-identical (103852 B / 453 allocations) in an untouched bgfx fixture. **Shard `^Bgfx` 165/171**, all six failures A/B-proven pre-existing; **gates 18/18** (GFX-181, GFX-155, GFX-156, GFX-158, cube MSAA face, present lifecycle). **ONE NEW FINDING: `REMED-GFX-185`.** docs `ed7ce5df`, test `3c7fe524`, fix `86b8127b`, coverage `da2fb96c`, closure (this). | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-164` | **EasyGL active multisampled render-target readback now resolves current content and preserves exact public alpha — DONE (2026-08-02).** The Bgfx half remains reconciled into `REMED-GFX-154`; no Bgfx production changed here. **EXACT CURRENT-HEAD REPRO:** `frontface_winding_test.cpp` W1msaa still read the never-covered opaque-black quadrants as `(0,0,0,0)` and skipped that destination, while the complete GFX-154 sequence was byte-exact on EasyGL. The distinguishing operation was not Clear versus draw: W1msaa called `GetData` while the 4x target remained bound, whereas GFX-154 unbound it first. **MEASURED STAGE:** the resolve texture is RGBA8 (8/8/8/8); non-MSAA active reads and all post-unbind reads preserve exact RGBA; target sampling after a producer/consumer transition is exact; but a pre-fix active-MSAA matrix was **183/210**, with each read exposing the preceding resolve generation (a fresh target therefore exposed untouched transparent-black storage). `CNA_EASYGL_TARGET_TRACE` showed no `rt2d.resolve` before that direct read and the resolve only at the later unbind. The raw backend transfer itself returned **0/64** current texels before public `Color` delivery, excluding RGBA/BGRA conversion and `GetData` indexing; the same content became exact after unbind, excluding rendering, Clear, draw, attachment format and storage corruption. **ROOT CAUSE:** EasyGL renders MSAA colour into a multisample renderbuffer but exposes a single-sample texture for sampling/readback, and resolved it only from `UnbindAsRenderTarget`; direct readback of the active producer therefore read stale resolve storage. **NARROW FIX:** the existing resolve blit is factored into one helper. `GetData` invokes it exactly once only when that multisampled attachment is currently bound, then restores the same single-target or recorded MRT draw FBO so rendering can continue; an idle target retains the established unbind resolve and incurs no extra blit. The existing one `glReadPixels` remains the only read; no second read, public frame, Present, retry, sleep, dummy bind or wait was added. **PERMANENT COVERAGE:** new EasyGL-only `EasyGL_GFX164_BoundMsaaAlpha` is **223/223**, covering non-MSAA and real 4x MSAA; RGBA8 selection; clear-only and drawn content; transparent/opaque black, zero RGB with alpha 37, and nonzero RGB with alpha 1/93/173/255; raw RGBA versus public delivery; full/repeated/rectangle reads; startIndex 5 and protected suffix; target sampling; repeated bind/unbind; continued single/MRT rendering after direct reads; unrelated-source reads; ordinary Texture2D and backbuffer controls; disposal, live-target teardown and a clean OpenGL error drain. The serial EasyGL shard is **289/291**; only the long-recorded `EasyGL_DeviceValidation` (`SetVertexBuffers(16)`) and `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil` failures remain, with exact pre-existing signatures and no readback route. Linked ASan+UBSan passes **223/223** with no address or undefined-behaviour report; leak-enabled A/B runs repeat the established Mesa-only **100956 B / 449 allocation** exit residual with zero CNA frames. Representative GFX-154 controls pass **34/34** on Bgfx, SDL_GPU, Vulkan, WebGPU, Software and Headless (Headless asserts deterministic refusal). GFX-127/134/142/149/154 and producer/consumer order remain green; ordinary textures/backbuffer and every other backend's production are untouched; `audit/` untouched. **No new finding ID was created.** | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-165` | **WebGPU and SDL_GPU reject a correctly sized `GraphicsDevice.GetBackBufferData` — DONE (2026-07-30).** With a backbuffer that `PresentationParameters` reports as 64x64, a full-surface read of exactly 64*64 `Color` elements throws `GetBackBufferData: data array too small for requested region` on both backends, while the same call is byte-exact on Software, EasyGL, Vulkan and Bgfx. Either the real swapchain is larger than the reported backbuffer (a `PresentationParameters` disagreement, which would be the more serious reading) or the size check itself is wrong. Consequence: a portable caller sizing its buffer from the public `BackBufferWidth`/`BackBufferHeight` cannot read the backbuffer on these two backends at all. On WebGPU this is distinct from REMED-GFX-161, which concerns the CONTENT of a first successful read. Recorded as a boundary by `frontface_winding_test.cpp`, whose render-target legs carry the contract there.  **Mechanism identified 2026-07-29 while closing REMED-GFX-167 (recorded, not fixed):** a rectangle-less `GetBackBufferData` sizes its region from the BACKEND's live viewport (`backend_->GetViewportSize`), not from `PresentationParameters`. On WebGPU that viewport is stale until the first `SetRenderTarget` round trip, so a leg that never binds a render target is rejected while an otherwise identical leg that does bind one is accepted — measured with the public `Viewport` AND `PresentationParameters` both reporting 64x64 at that same moment. The explicit-rectangle overload is unaffected and is what REMED-GFX-167's fixture uses. **RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (confirmed on a 37x23 backbuffer: `PresentationParameters` and the physical surface both 37x23, `GetViewportSize` 38x23 -> 851 rejected against 874).** The size check was never wrong and the swapchain is NOT larger: the rectangle-less path sized from the aspect-scaled live viewport, not the authoritative backbuffer. Shared fix: `GraphicsDevice::GetBackBufferData` now takes the rectangle-less region from `PresentationParameters` and validates an explicit rectangle against the real backbuffer bounds (throwing before any native copy) -- byte-neutral on the four already-correct backends. SDL_GPU had a SECOND layer (no backbuffer read at all, the swapchain being write-only per SDLGPU-39): fixed with a LAZY self-owned proxy the deferred-frame model allows to be created on first read, so it costs nothing until used (refuting SDLGPU-39's per-frame-cost objection). New `backbuffer_readback_dimension_test.cpp` 8/8 on all seven native backends; GFX-165 workarounds removed from the GFX-166/167/168 lifetime fixtures; ASan/UBSan and native validation clean; only WebGPU/SDL_GPU production changed. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-166` | **The draw Vulkan lost was the PRODUCER, and the resource destroyed was that producer's DESTINATION — DONE (2026-07-30).** The ticket's framing ("loses a queued draw whose SOURCE was destroyed") named the wrong command and the wrong role, and both corrections were measured. `~VulkanRenderTargetBackend` called REMED-GFX-074's `PurgeDeferredWorkForTarget(this)`, which erased every `activeBatches_`/`pending3D_`/`pendingClears_` entry whose `rt == this` — justified in the source as "a destroyed target is unobservable ... so discarding its unflushed draws is observationally identical to XNA's eager model". That is false exactly when another still-queued draw SAMPLES the target, i.e. for the whole render-to-texture pattern. **Proven with a new env-gated `CNA_VULKAN_LIFETIME_TRACE`, not inferred:** leg A3 pre-fix records `enqueue.sprite order=4 rt=0x..b668` (the producer), `enqueue.3D order=6 rt=(nil)` (the consumer), then `purge.DROPPED rt=0x..b668 sprites=1 draws=0 clears=2` — three commands destroyed — and still `record.draw3D order=6 ... boundSet=0x65` and `frame.submitted gen=1`. So the consumer draw IS issued, its descriptor set IS bound, and the image and view are alive in REMED-GFX-075's retirement queue (`retire.insert gen=0 views=2 images=1 fbs=1 descSets=2 poolSets=1`, freed at gen 2). Nothing dangles and no draw is missing: the sampled image simply has no content. That is why the destination read back `(0,0,0,0)` in some legs and UNDEFINED GARBAGE in others — `(184,160,0,217)`, `(128,0,0,14)`, `(232,16,0,177)` — which a draw dropped to black could never produce. An ordinary `Texture2D` was NEVER affected (leg A2 passes pre-fix), so the blanket per-backend declaration inherited from REMED-GFX-152 was over-applied. **Fix: a destination is no longer the wrapper's backend object.** `VulkanTargetPassEXT` is a separately allocated, IMMUTABLE description of one render pass (framebuffer, render pass, extent, MSAA, depth format, load-op shape, depth/stencil group, mip image/levels/layer); every field is final at the owning target's construction, so a copy taken there stays correct after `ReleaseVulkanResources()` has handed the handles to the retirement queue — which is precisely the window a command outliving its wrapper must be replayed in. The deferred queues, `currentRT_` and SpriteBatch's `activeRT_` hold `shared_ptr<VulkanRTSource>`, so a queued command CO-OWNS its destination: nothing dangles (so `PurgeDeferredWorkForTarget` is DELETED, not weakened), nothing is discarded (so the producer records and the consumer samples real content), and the pass address is a stable identity while any command names it, so a later target landing on the freed wrapper's heap address cannot alias a dead one — which is what leg F1 measures. **The pass owns NO Vulkan handles, deliberately:** handle lifetime stays REMED-GFX-075's frame-generation retirement, which already covers the CPU record window and in-flight GPU reads; giving the pass ownership would have created a second, differently-timed release path for the same handles. The MRT proxy is the one destination that DOES own its framebuffer, so its generation-gated `retiredMrtProxies_` list is KEPT (now shared) rather than replaced — the queues are cleared inside `RecordCommandBuffer`, before the command buffer referencing that framebuffer is submitted; the two gates answer two different questions. `RenderTargetCube`'s six value-member FaceProxies became six shared face passes carrying the same `depthGroup` (the cube's colour image), so REMED-GFX-142's whole-cube flush group is unchanged, and the two byte-identical mip cascades collapsed into one implementation differing only by array layer. Since a `RenderTarget2D` is no longer itself a `VulkanRTSource`, `SampledRenderTargetGroupEXT` asks the concrete type via `dynamic_cast` — never a static cast between siblings (REMED-GFX-152). **Cost: exactly one thing was added, and it is the work the API asked for.** Leg A3's trace, pre vs post: `enqueue.3D` 1/1, `enqueue.sprite` 1/1, `enqueue.clear` 4/4, `frame.submitted` 2/2, `retire.insert` 3/3 with BYTE-IDENTICAL payloads, `retire.FREE` 3/3, `cache.evict` 4/4, `rt2d.create`/`dispose` 1/1 — and `purge.DROPPED` 1 -> 0, `record.segment` 0 -> 1, `record.sprite` 0 -> 1. No extra submit, Present, frame, wait, CPU copy, target switch, descriptor set or retirement entry; the only delta is the producer render pass that was previously discarded. **Tests: `deferred_source_lifetime_test.cpp` 6/14 legs -> 17/17 legs, 0 crashes, 24 checks.** Leg E2 gives the cube slot a real oracle (E1 only asserted "replays safely"); leg J1 now MEASURES MSAA instead of declaring it (`PreferMultiSampling` is set only in J1's own child, since a Vulkan RT can engage MSAA only when the backbuffer did) and proves the RESOLVED texture is what the command retains; leg N1 covers both destruction orders of the consumer's effect and its source; leg L1 releases the source in the frame AFTER the submit and PASSES pre-fix, stated as a guard on the fix rather than a reproduction. REMED-GFX-152's fixture went 18 -> 20 legs: its own `kDeadSourceStillSampleable` VULKAN declaration deleted (M1's two dead-source checks were 0/32 pre-fix), plus M2 (every stock-effect family) and M3 (every draw mode) as destroyed-RT-versus-live-`Texture2D` difference tests — 5 families and all 5 draw modes at 0/32 pre-fix, `deadRt=(0,0,0,0)` against `liveTex=(20,25,40,255)`. **Two false positives found in the fixtures that should have caught this.** REMED-GFX-075's scene S3 contains `(void)readRt(*rtSrc)`, commented "force rtSrc content into its image" — that call is a FLUSH, which consumed the producer while the source was still alive, so the purge had nothing left to drop; its comment asserted the right thing about the CONSUMER and missed the PRODUCER. New scene S3b is S3 with that line deleted, and had to be made byte-EXACT: written with the existing loose `IsBlue` predicate it PASSED against the unfixed backend, because a purged producer leaves the image UNDEFINED and the recycled device memory happened to be "blue enough" — 0/3072 with a unique tint compared on all four channels. REMED-GFX-074's scene S6 asserts only `check(true, "did not crash")`, the one shape of check a silent non-crashing wrong result always walks past; S6b adds what that fixture can own (the next target must still be exact) and says that it too passes pre-fix. **Validation clean with the layer PROVEN loaded** (`Loading layer library libVkLayer_khronos_validation.so`, 11 layer references) and synchronization validation requested: zero VUIDs across all 17 legs. **ASan and UBSan clean**, runtimes proved linked by symbol (39 `__asan_*`, 15 `__ubsan_*`): 0 reports, 0 runtime errors, 17/17 and 20/20 legs under each. The leak A/B is byte-identical pre- and post-fix on every leg measured — 368 bytes in 4 allocations, all in `<unknown module>` — so the residual is the driver's exit leak and no byte was added. **`ctest -R '^Vulkan'` 186/187**; the one failure (`Vulkan_DepthBias`, "DepthBias=-1e6 (flat)" 3/4) is A/B-proven pre-existing, byte-identical with this task's two production files reverted. GFX-039/107/109/113/118/125/127/130/134/136/140/142/144/151/157/160 gates all green in that shard. **Seven cross-backend controls, only Vulkan production changed:** VULKAN/WEBGPU/SDL_GPU/SOFTWARE/HEADLESS/BGFX/EASYGL all **17/17** lifetime legs and **20/20** producer-consumer legs, 0 crashes; WebGPU's GFX-167 contract stays green and EasyGL's GFX-168 stays independently declared with EasyGL production untouched. D3D9/D3D11/D3D12 cross-build only. **Spawned one independent finding:** on VULKAN, `EnvironmentMapEffect`, `SkinnedEffect`, `PbrEffect` and `SkinnedPbrEffect` render NOTHING through a stride-20 position+texcoord stream (0/32, 0/32, 0/32 and 1/32 non-black), where identical code on SOFTWARE measures all nine families at 32/32 — so REMED-GFX-152 leg B1's family comparison has been vacuously green for those four on this backend. Found only because the new comparison refuses to pass on two equally EMPTY images. test `3e5436fb`, fix `177c8b9f`, matrix `123870c1` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-167` | **WebGPU replayed every deferred draw through a RAW POINTER to the sampled resource's backend object, so a source destroyed while its draw was still queued was a heap-use-after-free at `Present()` — DONE (2026-07-29).** **The ticket's own framing was wrong twice over, and both corrections were measured, not argued.** It is NOT teardown: the crash is on the MAIN thread inside `Present()` -> `EnsureFrameRendered()` -> `ReplayOrderedSegments()` -> `ReplayDrawsInOrder()` -> `IssueTexturedDraw()`, after the leg's checks have already printed — which is exactly why it *read* as shutdown. And it is NOT "specific to the backbuffer": what is load-bearing is that **nothing FLUSHED** between the consumer draw and the destructor. WebGPU renders a render-target destination at `SetRenderTarget()`, so only a backbuffer destination leaves the queue alive across the source's death. **Measured under ASan, not inferred:** `heap-use-after-free on address 0x508000068528` at `WebGPUGraphicsBackend.cpp:7228`, `freed by thread T0` in `~WebGPURenderTargetBackend()` at `:1746` via `RenderTarget2D::~RenderTarget2D()`, allocated in `CreateRenderTarget2D()` at `:6656`; without a sanitizer, SIGSEGV (139) with the program counter `n/a (n/a + 0x0)` — in no mapped module, the freed object's vtable slot (systemd-coredump; there is no gdb here). **Scope: nineteen slots across nine families** — `SpriteCommand`, `Textured`, `LitTextured`, `AlphaTest`, `DualTexture` (2), `EnvMap` (a 2D slot + the cube one), `Pbr` (5), `Skinned`, `SkinnedPbr` (5) — every one a `const IWebGPUSamplable*` justified in the header by "is owned by long-lived game/content state ... so it is guaranteed to still be alive". Nothing guarantees that. **Fix: resolve ONCE, at the public draw call.** Commands carry a `WebGPUSampledTextureEXT` (the already-resolved `WGPUTextureView` + a `shared_ptr` to a `WebGPUSampledResourceEXT` holding one native reference on the view AND its texture), closing both halves at once: replay never dereferences a wrapper, and the handles outlive it. `wgpuTextureRelease`/`wgpuTextureViewRelease` are refcount decrements, not the destructive `wgpuTextureDestroy`, so a held reference keeps the resource genuinely USABLE rather than merely addressable — proven by pixels, not by absence of a crash. Returning a VALUE from `ResolveSamplable()`/`ResolveCubeSamplable()` (their `dynamic_cast` and degrade-to-unbound behaviour unchanged) is what made the conversion compiler-checked: a missed site cannot compile. **A hazard the fix itself introduced, found and closed:** the eleven family vectors are plain MEMBERS, destroyed AFTER the destructor body releases queue/device/adapter/instance — so a populated vector would release textures with the device gone. `DiscardQueuedCommands()` is now called FIRST in the destructor. Declared honestly as ordering correctness: the reachable path (a frame abandoned on surface-acquisition failure) is not deterministically forceable through the public API. **Cost: nothing per draw.** Each samplable backend builds its keep-alive ONCE at construction, so copying into a command is a refcount increment and allocates nothing. No extra pass, submit, Present, frame or wait — held by the three existing WebGPU cardinality gates (`DrawOrder`, `Viewport`, `Scissor`), all green. Only the SAMPLED pair is kept alive; a render target's depth/MSAA attachments stay with the wrapper, because a pass destination is built and consumed synchronously while the target is bound. **New process-isolated `deferred_source_lifetime_test.cpp`**, 14 legs each in its own child, classifying PASS/FAIL/**CRASH with the signal named**/TIMEOUT. The oracle is PIXELS everywhere it exists, so a backend that silently DROPS such a draw fails as loudly as one that faults. **WEBGPU 4/14 legs and 10 SIGSEGV -> 14/14, 0 crashes, 18 checks.** Leg K1 reproduces the reported symptom exactly: `[PASS] ... completes` prints, then the process dies. Leg B1 (source destroyed AFTER the unbind) passes even UNFIXED — that ordering is precisely why the pre-existing lifetime leg never caught this. **Three fixture defects found by RUNNING it:** leg E1 measured nothing (a stride-20 quad is routed away from the env-map family, which needs stride 32 — it PASSED against the unfixed backend until corrected); the pixel probe sat 0.0625 texel off centre, so Software and Vulkan reported `(21,27,51,248)` for `(20,25,40,255)` on the LIVE control (a filtering artefact, fixed by 9 pixels per texel so the probe is byte-exact under point AND linear); and legs B1/C1 assumed a flush-at-unbind that a whole-frame recorder does not do. **ASan and UBSan clean** (0 reports, both runtimes proved linked by symbol), and the leak A/B is byte-identical pre- and post-fix on the legs that survive both — 1247252/714, 1248928/717, 1251544/721 — so no leaked byte and no allocation was added; the residual is wgpu-native's own exit leak. **Native validation clean:** zero uncaptured errors and zero device-lost events across every leg of both fixtures. **Seven cross-backend controls, only WebGPU production changed:** WEBGPU/SOFTWARE/EASYGL/BGFX/VULKAN/SDL_GPU/HEADLESS all **14/14 legs, 0 crashes**; Vulkan declares GFX-166 rather than folding that separate defect in, and SDL_GPU declares its missing backbuffer readback rather than pretending to measure. `ctest -L WebGPU` **57/58**, the one failure (`WebGPU_Clear_Readback`) A/B-proven pre-existing, as are all 8 failures in the full 5834-test shard. All fourteen backend libraries build incrementally. **Spawned GFX-168** (EasyGL faults inside `SetRenderTarget` when the previously bound `RenderTarget2D` has been destroyed) and added the mechanism for the already-open GFX-165. test `5f93db60`, fix `b83bf20d`, hardening `19b6fb34`, teardown order `63f1f9f1` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-168` | **EasyGL dispatched the next `SetRenderTarget` through a destroyed render target — DONE (2026-07-30).** The ticket's shape was right; its explanation of *why nothing caught it* was the part that had to be established. `EasyGLGraphicsBackend` held the bound destination as raw pointers to render-target BACKEND OBJECTS (`currentRt2D_`, `currentRtCube_`, `currentMrtTargets_[4]`) and the next transition called `UnbindAsRenderTarget()` on the outgoing one — a virtual call — to run its pending MSAA resolve and mip regeneration. **Measured, not inferred:** ASan reports `heap-use-after-free`, `READ of size 8`, at `EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp:2179` — the line `if (currentRt2D_ && currentRt2D_ != rt) currentRt2D_->UnbindAsRenderTarget();` — at **offset 0 of a 128-byte region**, i.e. the object's vtable pointer, freed in `~EasyGLRenderTargetBackend()` via `Texture2D::~Texture2D()` <- `RenderTarget2D::~RenderTarget2D()` and allocated in `CreateRenderTarget2D()`; without a sanitizer, **SIGSEGV (139), core dumped, program counter `0x0` in no mapped module**, on the main thread, called from `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget(RenderTarget2D*)`. It is NOT the destructor that faults and NOT teardown. A new env-gated `CNA_EASYGL_TARGET_TRACE` supplies the part a crash address cannot: `rt2d.destroy obj=0x..1e0 fbo=1 color=3` then `set2d.enter cur2d=0x..1e0` — the wrapper gone, the backend storage gone, GL framebuffer 1 and texture 3 deleted with it, the record still naming the freed address — **and no `rt2d.unbind` line at all**, which proves the method body never ran and the fault is the dispatch itself. **The shared layer cannot help, by design.** `RenderTarget2D::Dispose()` refuses a bound target with `InvalidOperationException` and `RenderTargetCube::Dispose()` carries the identical guard — but both destructors are `= default` and never route through `Dispose`, so the guarded path is unreachable-by-design and the unguarded one is the only way in. What is load-bearing is ONLY that the target was still bound when it died: legs A1 (unbind, sample, release) and A2 (release after `SetRenderTarget(B)`) pass against the unfixed backend. **Fix: the binding record gets its own allocation.** `EasyGLBoundTargetEXT` is separately allocated, held by the backend as a `shared_ptr` and by every render target it creates as a `weak_ptr`, so a dying target clears its own slots directly — never calling back into a graphics backend that may itself already be gone, and never touching anything if the backend went first. That reverse order is not reachable through CNA's `Game` harness (`GraphicsDevice_` is a `Game` base member, destroyed after every subclass member), so it is stated as ordering correctness rather than a reproduced fault; a globally held target reaches it, which is why the ownership is weak. The record holds identity and extent only — **no GL handles, no ownership, no retirement list** — so there is nothing to grow and nothing to free later. **Detaching rather than finalizing is a measured claim.** `UnbindAsRenderTarget` resolves `msaaColorRbo_` into `colorTex_` and regenerates its mip chain, but both write into members the same destructor destroys, and the only public routes to that content (`GetData`, sampling as a `Texture2D`) need the live wrapper — so a target destroyed while bound has no reachable observer, and legs D1a/E1a assert the converse by requiring the resolve and level 1 to be byte-exact when the target IS unbound first. **MRT is the one exception and is treated as one:** a detach clears one SLOT, and leg L1's trace shows `mrt.detach slot 0`, then `set2d.enter mrtCount=2 mrt0=0 mrt1=0x..c90`, then the survivor's own `rt2d.mipgen levels=4`. **`Present()` bounds the whole window, and leg P1 asserts it** rather than assuming: `GraphicsDevice::Present()` refuses a bound target from `renderTargetBound_`, a BOOL no destructor touches, so the refusal is identical for a live and a destroyed target — which is why three first-draft legs written as "destroyed while bound, then Present" had to be rewritten; that sequence is unreachable on every backend. **New process-isolated 18-leg `bound_target_lifetime_test.cpp`: 3/18 legs and 15 SIGSEGV -> 18/18, 0 crashes, 43 checks, 0 declared boundaries.** Under ASan+UBSan, runtimes proved linked by symbol, **2/18 with 32 `heap-use-after-free` -> 18/18 with 0 reports and 0 runtime errors** — the un-sanitized pass count is HIGHER because leg H1 survives without a sanitizer, the freed vtable pointer happening to still dispatch, which is exactly this defect's character. Reuse is measured, not hoped for: M1's 121 targets reuse ONE backend-object heap address **9 times** and every one of them receives GL framebuffer name **1**, so a stale pointer would have ALIASED a live target rather than merely dangled. **Cost: one trace event, the detach.** On A1 and A2 the entire event stream is BYTE-IDENTICAL pre and post with addresses normalised — 3 creates, 3 binds, 3 unbinds, 3 destroys, 10 transitions in, 10 out, zero detaches — and no extra Present, frame, wait, readback, CPU copy or GL object anywhere. Leak A/B byte-identical at 100956 bytes / 449 allocations pre and post, including on M1's 121 targets, with the whole residue in `libGLX_mesa.so`. GL validation clean at the transition that would raise it: the trace drains `glGetError()` per event and **917 traced transitions all report `glerr=none`**. **The one fixture that named this case detoured around it** — `easygl_bound_resource_dispose_test.cpp:98-105` tested the `Dispose()` refusal then wrote `dev.SetRenderTarget(nullptr);   // unbind before leaving scope` — strengthened with all three binding shapes released by their DESTRUCTOR (pre-fix: its original 10 checks all PASS, then SIGSEGV on the first new one; post-fix 17/17), plus the cube's own refusal, which nothing tested before. An audit of the other 33 EasyGL render-target fixtures found NONE covering the case: every one routes its work through a helper owning a complete bind cycle, or keeps its targets in class-member lifetime. **Seven cross-backend controls, only EasyGL production changed:** EASYGL/VULKAN/SOFTWARE/BGFX/WEBGPU/SDL_GPU/HEADLESS all **18/18 legs, 0 crashes**; every other backend already honoured the contract, and Vulkan's GFX-166 and WebGPU's GFX-167 fixtures stay green with neither backend touched. `ctest -R '^EasyGL'` **270/272**; both failures (`EasyGL_DeviceValidation`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`) are A/B-proven pre-existing and neither touches render-target binding. All fourteen backend configurations build the CNA library incrementally. **Three independent findings recorded:** VULKAN returns all-zero for mip level 1 of an MRT member while regenerating a lone target's chain correctly; BGFX cannot read back a resolved MSAA target or any mip level 1; and `easygl_rendertargetcube_properties_test.cpp:21-26` carries a stale comment claiming `RenderTargetCube::Dispose` has no bound guard, which it has had since REMED-GFX-142. test `87006715`, fix `b2823e56`, controls `014c4799` | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
