| `REMED-GFX-199` | **D3D12's instanced PSO bakes in the bound render-target format without keying on it — OPEN.** `GetOrCreateInstancedPsoEXT` sets `desc.RTVFormats[0] = boundColorFormat_` and, after REMED-GFX-123, caches by `InstanceDataStepRate` alone. Every *ordinary* D3D12 draw path resolves its pipeline through `psoCache_.GetOrCreate(device, rootSig, psoDesc, boundColorFormat_, boundDsvFormat_)` — the format **is** part of that cache's key — so the asymmetry is inside one backend, not across backends: an instanced draw into an off-screen target whose `SurfaceFormat` differs from the first one ever bound reuses a PSO built for the earlier format. Distinct from REMED-GFX-077, which documents this same PSO's hardcoded *blend/write* state; the render-target format is a different axis and is not covered by that note. Found while keying the cache for REMED-GFX-123's step rate and deliberately left out of that task's narrowest correction. **Inspection-only**: D3D12's windowed runtime is still blocked by REMED-BUILD-012 (A/B-proven under REMED-GFX-123), so no pixel reproduction exists yet. | LOW | GRAPHICS | OPEN |
| `REMED-GFX-200` | **`VertexBufferBinding.VertexOffset` never reached any backend on the ordinary (non-instanced) draw routes — DONE (2026-08-02).** The recorded scope was exactly right and the loss point is exactly where it said: `GraphicsDevice::DrawPrimitives` and `DrawIndexedPrimitives` each build a fresh `GpuDrawParams`, call `FillGpuDrawParams`, and never read the bound `VertexBufferBinding`, so `p.vertexBufferOffset` stayed at its `0` default and every ordinary draw rendered from element zero on **every** backend. **REPRODUCED FIRST, ON UNFIXED HEAD:** the new shared oracle ran **2/17** on EasyGL, and the two that passed were exactly the two zero-offset controls — a `VertexOffset=3` indexed draw produced a frame *byte-identical* to its own zero-offset control (slot 6 band 3, the prefix decoy), while the same request with a nonzero `startIndex` or `baseVertex` lit slot 2, one offset-width left of the requested slot 3. The instanced route lit slot 4 correctly in the same binary, which is the asymmetry named. **THE FIX IS ONE SHARED CORRECTION, NOT A PER-BACKEND SWEEP.** Both ordinary routes now read binding 0 through one `CurrentVertexBufferOffset()` helper — the identical rule `DrawInstancedPrimitives` already applied inline, now single-sourced with no behavioural change there — and carry the offset in the element-unit field each route already owns: `vertexStart` for the non-indexed route, `baseVertex` for the indexed one. That is not a shortcut: `VertexOffset` shifts the stream base while `vertexStart`/`baseVertex` index that same stream, both in whole vertex elements against the same declaration stride, on **the one per-vertex stream `Draw*PrimitivesEx` hands a backend** — so their sum is the first record fetched, and delivering it through that channel reaches every backend's *already-remediated* single stride multiplication at the native binding (REMED-GFX-020/060/117/119), in elements, with that stream's own stride, applied exactly once each. `startIndex` is untouched — it selects index elements, which a binding offset never displaces. `GpuDrawParams::vertexBufferOffset` is deliberately left at **0** on the ordinary routes and its contract is now documented at the backend boundary, because populating it *as well* would apply the public offset twice on EasyGL/D3D11/D3D12; the instanced route keeps that field because its per-instance stream carries an independent offset `baseVertex` cannot express. REMED-GFX-113's and REMED-GFX-118's range validation now counts the offset too, matching what the instanced route has done since REMED-GFX-118. **NEW SHARED PUBLIC ORACLE** `OrdinaryDrawBindingOffsetTests.cpp`, a two-axis slot/band cell map: the column says *which records* a draw consumed and the band says *which bytes they were*, so a dropped offset (slot 6 band 3, or one width left), a doubled `baseVertex`/`startIndex` (slot 6 band 0), a byte-for-element multiplication (48 records into a 24-record buffer → nothing lights at all), a wrong stream (band 2), stale contents after an update (band 0 where band 1 was written) and a stale previous binding offset each light a **different cell**. Every assertion is `requested cell lit AND frame total == that cell's count`, so geometry landing between cells is rejected too — no colour tolerance and no process-survival fallback. The zero-offset controls assert the prefix decoy's own cell rather than "nothing rendered", so they fail if the fixture ever stops rendering. Covers offset 0 and nonzero, offset×`startIndex`, offset×`baseVertex`, all three at once, 16- and 32-bit indices, static and dynamic buffers, a rewrite between draws, two streams with different offsets, A→B→A reuse, ordinary→instanced→ordinary, disposal with handle reuse, both `SetVertexBuffer` overloads, the offset-inclusive range gate, and backbuffer **and** RenderTarget2D destinations. **MEASURED: EasyGL 2/17 → 17/17.** Backends, all post-fix: **Vulkan 17/17**, **Bgfx 17/17**, **WebGPU 17/17**, **D3D11 17/17** (Wine **builtin** D3D11, not DXVK — DXVK 2.6.0's `dxgi` still breaks SDL video init under Xvfb and `:0` was forbidden), **D3D9 17/17** on **genuine DXVK 2.6.0** (`dxgi=b;d3d9=n` — builtin `dxgi` only for SDL's display enumeration; the script's own DXVK gate confirms no WineD3D fallback), **SDL_GPU 14/14** and **Software 16/16** (the backbuffer and/or instanced groups compile out there by capability, not by silent skip), **Headless** compiles the shared code and runs its 63/63 gates with the pixel oracle correctly compiled out. **D3D12: clean cross-build plus correct-by-inspection** — its ordinary route already consumes `params.vertexStart`/`startIndex`/`baseVertex` as `StartVertexLocation`/`StartIndexLocation`/`BaseVertexLocation`, which is the exact channel this fix uses, and **no D3D12 code changed**; its windowed runtime stays blocked by REMED-BUILD-012, **A/B-proven** by two *pre-existing* windowed D3D12 fixtures (REMED-GFX-113's and REMED-GFX-118's) dying at the identical point with the identical exit 5 while the offscreen `cna_test_d3d12_smoke.exe` is ALL PASS through real vkd3d. **REGRESSION GATES:** EasyGL full `CnaTests` **5780 passed / 5787**, the two failures A/B-classified — `XnbContainerFuzzTest.MutatedRealModelFixture…` fails **identically with this change reverted and rebuilt** (a content-load `SetData` declaration/stride throw, no draw call involved) and `TwoProcessLoopbackTest.HostMigration…` passes in isolation both with and without the change (a 30 s full-suite-load timeout in an ENet test). Draw/binding gates: EasyGL **178/178**, Vulkan **110/110**, Bgfx **104/104**, SDL_GPU **104/104**, D3D11 **107/107**, Headless **63/63**, D3D9 `NonIndexedDrawRange` **11/11** + `VertexBufferBinding` **14/14** + instanced **17 of 18 executed, 0 failures** (the 18th is unreachable under Xvfb+RADV's documented `VK_ERROR_SURFACE_LOST_KHR` cascade, an environment limit that also truncated the broader D3D9 filters — reported, not glossed). **SANITIZERS, all zero reports:** Software ASan **47/47**, Software UBSan **47/47**, EasyGL ASan **61/61**, Vulkan UBSan **58/58**. `DrawUser*` is **proven** an unaffected control from current source, not assumed: those overloads take raw `vertexData` pointers, carry no bound binding, and `currentVertexBuffers_` is referenced nowhere in them. | `REMED-GFX-201` | **Ordinary draws handed the backend only vertex stream 0 — DONE (2026-08-03).** The recorded scope was right and the loss point exactly where it said: `Draw*PrimitivesEx` take one `const IVertexBufferBackend&` and `GraphicsDevice` passed `currentVertexBuffer_`, so bindings 1..15 reached no backend on the ordinary routes. Classifying it found the loss is **deeper** than recorded: eight of the nine rasterizing backends discard the `VertexDeclaration` outright (`SetVertexDeclaration(const VertexDeclaration&) override {}`) and infer their native input elements from the **byte stride** alone, so a second stream had neither a binding to arrive on nor a layout to be described by. **AUTHORITATIVE CONTRACT ESTABLISHED FROM SOURCE, NOT FROM CNA'S SHAPE:** FNA's `DrawPrimitives`, `DrawIndexedPrimitives` and `DrawInstancedPrimitives` all call the *same* `PrepareVertexBindingArray` and hand the driver the complete binding array, one `(buffer, declaration, stride, elementOffset, instanceFrequency)` per slot; FNA3D's OpenGL driver computes `vertexStride * (vertexOffset + baseVertex)` **per stream with that stream's own stride** and its D3D11 driver sets `InputSlot = i`, `offset = vertexOffset * stride`, `AlignedByteOffset = element->offset`. Composition is by **SEMANTIC, not position**: both drivers drop a repeated `(usage, usageIndex)` because `GetVertexAttribLocation` returns -1 — *"Stream not in use!"* — so a second stream that merely repeats stream 0's declaration contributes nothing. That rule was got wrong first, and **REMED-GFX-200's own two-stream fixture caught it**, failing on Vulkan until composition was corrected; the test then went green unmodified. **REPRODUCED FIRST, ON UNFIXED HEAD:** the new three-axis oracle `OrdinaryDrawMultiStreamTests.cpp` ran **3/19 on EasyGL** — column = which records stream 0 supplied, band = which bytes, colour = which records **stream 1** supplied — and every pixel case rendered **nothing at all** (`total lit: 0`), because stream 0 is POSITION only (12-byte stride, a stride no backend has a layout for) and stream 1 is COLOUR only (4 bytes), whose concatenation is byte-for-byte the packed 16-byte `PositionColorStream`. Colour codes are the six `{0,255}` RGB corners, so the classifier is exact, not tolerant. **ARCHITECTURE:** `GpuDrawParams` carries a fixed-capacity `GpuVertexStreamBinding[16]` plus `vertexStreamCount` and `combinedVertexStride`, by value, with no heap allocation on any draw and no pointer into mutable `currentVertexBuffers_`; `combinedByteBase` bridges CNA's combined-offset element tables to FNA3D's stream-local `AlignedByteOffset`, and `MapCombinedOffsetToStream`/`CombinedVertexStrideOr`/`HasMultipleVertexStreams`/`VertexStreamByteOffset` all degenerate to the identity for one stream. The ordinary routes fold the **smallest** per-vertex binding offset into `vertexStart`/`baseVertex` and hand each stream the non-negative remainder — the only split that keeps every native byte offset >= 0 (folding binding 0's would make a smaller-offset stream negative, which Vulkan/D3D12/WebGPU cannot express) and byte-identical to REMED-GFX-200 whenever one stream is bound. REMED-GFX-113's range gate is now per-stream and names the offending slot, so a **short secondary stream is rejected even when stream 0 is long enough**; argument validation precedes the capability gate so the public exception is the same everywhere. **IMPLEMENTED ON EASYGL** (every stream bound into the VAO at locations continuing after the previous stream's, each with its own VBO/stride/byte offset, single-stream layout restored after the draw, no VAO per draw) **AND SOFTWARE** (each combined offset resolved to its owning stream at fetch time — no interleaved temporary, no per-vertex allocation, addressing bounded by the *shortest* stream). **HONEST BOUNDARY EVERYWHERE ELSE:** new `GraphicsCapability::MultiStreamVertexInput` is the one capability whose default is **false**, so a backend must opt in by name; Vulkan/Bgfx/WebGPU/SDL_GPU/D3D11/D3D12/D3D9/Headless report false and `GraphicsDevice` rejects a multi-stream ordinary draw with `System::NotSupportedException` **before native submission**, never truncating the binding list or collapsing streams. `UnsupportedBackendRejectsMultiStreamDeterministically` asserts that in **both directions on every backend** and flips to the positive assertion the moment a backend claims the capability, so the 14 declared skips cannot outlive the gap. **MEASURED: EasyGL 3/19 -> 20/20, Software -> 19/19.** REMED-GFX-200 **17/17** on EasyGL, Vulkan, Bgfx, WebGPU, D3D11 (Wine **builtin** D3D11, not DXVK) and D3D9 (**genuine DXVK 2.6.0**), 14/14 SDL_GPU, 63/63 Headless. Draw/binding gates green on all nine (EasyGL 517, Bgfx 509, WebGPU 503, Software 500, Vulkan 498, SDL_GPU 485, Headless 440 — zero failures). Sanitizers: Software ASan 358 and EasyGL ASan+UBSan 372 with **zero** reports, Software UBSan 358 with three pre-existing `GetHashCode` int overflows unchanged since 2026-05-24. Principal suites (the common interface changed): EasyGL **5801 passed / 1**, Software **5731 passed / 4**, all five A/B-proven pre-existing. D3D12 cross-builds clean; its windowed runtime stays blocked by REMED-BUILD-012, **A/B-proven** by two untouched windowed fixtures dying at the identical point in the same run. No public API removed, no shader, no bytecode, no extra draw/pass/submit/frame/wait, no CPU or GPU stream merge. Spawned REMED-GFX-202..209. REMED-GFX-199 untouched and still OPEN. Signed `272c2261`/`2f224081`/`f1e65507`. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | DONE |
| `REMED-GFX-202` | **The instanced route carried its own parallel two-stream channel — DONE (2026-08-03).** The first shared loss was one missing line: `GraphicsDevice::DrawInstancedPrimitives` never called `FillVertexStreamBindings`, so `GpuDrawParams::vertexStreamCount` stayed 0 and the entire public `VertexBufferBinding` array was reduced to binding 0's `VertexOffset` plus the **first** `instanceFrequency > 0` binding (the loop `break`s). Every per-vertex binding at slot >= 1, every per-instance binding after the first, every slot number, every declaration and every per-stream stride were discarded before any backend saw them — which is why all six instanced backends hardcoded a two-element native array and substituted their own fallback strides. **FNA is authoritative and unambiguous:** `DrawPrimitives`, `DrawIndexedPrimitives` and `DrawInstancedPrimitives` all call the same `PrepareVertexBindingArray(baseVertex)` → `FNA3D_ApplyVertexBufferBindings`, and an instance stream is simply a binding whose `InstanceFrequency` is greater than zero. Reproduced first on the unfixed tree with a new four-axis oracle (two per-vertex streams at 12 and 4 bytes, two per-instance streams splitting the world matrix at strides 48/16 and frequencies 1/2, so each stream owns an independently readable screen axis): **Headless 2/5, where a short secondary per-vertex stream, a short second per-instance stream and a four-stream mixed-frequency draw on a `MultiStreamVertexInput = false` backend all reported `Actual: it throws nothing`** — accepted and submitted — and EasyGL 3/12. `instanceVb`, `instanceVertexOffset`, `instanceFrequency` and `vertexBufferOffset` are gone and `sizeof(GpuDrawParams)` fell 5712 → 5688 bytes. **The fold is 0 on this route, unlike the ordinary ones:** its backends already have a per-binding native offset channel (exactly what REMED-GFX-122/123 built), so every stream keeps its whole public `VertexOffset` and `baseVertex` stays the caller's value — FNA3D's own D3D11 driver verbatim — which is why every REMED-GFX-118/122/123 gate stayed byte-identical; folding would additionally be wrong because `baseVertex` must not advance a per-instance stream. Software overrides no instanced path at all — that boundary is declared and asserted rather than fabricated. All seven native backends green (EasyGL 68, bgfx 49, Vulkan 45, WebGPU 45, Software 41, SDL_GPU 25, Headless 8); D3D9 4/4 and 7/7 plus D3D11 159/164 under Wine on `:99`, its five failures A/B-proven identical pre-fix; four sanitizer configurations clean; seven principal suites run with every failure one of four already-recorded pre-existing tests. All 22 pre-existing instancing tests bound the 1+1 shape at slots 0/1 — 100 % blind to everything this ticket covers. REMED-GFX-203..208 stay separate and are narrowed, not widened. Spawned REMED-GFX-210/211/212/213. **2026-08-03 reconciliation: this ticket and REMED-GFX-201 completed the shared architecture; their eleven descendants REMED-GFX-203..213 no longer form an automatic immediate execution chain and are routed to the root-level `plan_postaudit.md` — REMED-GFX-203..210 DEFERRED (Disposition: POST-AUDIT PLAN), REMED-GFX-211/212/213 held OPEN as POST-AUDIT CANDIDATE — CHECKPOINT TRIAGE REQUIRED.** | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |

| `REMED-GFX-209` | **`GraphicsDeviceCapabilityTest.DoesNotSupportWireFrame` asserted one backend's gap unconditionally — DONE 2026-08-04. A/B-proven a test assumption and not a production regression: the test file is byte-identical to `75b61fa4`, and every backend's `SupportsCapability` WireFrame arm is unchanged between `75b61fa4` and HEAD. **THE CONTRACT WAS THEN MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED, ON ALL TEN BACKENDS** with a new asymmetric-triangle pixel oracle (256x256 `RenderTarget2D`, one non-indexed `DrawPrimitives`, `CullMode::None`, one interior probe 40 px from every edge and one disjoint 25x25 probe per edge): **Software / Vulkan / bgfx / SDL_GPU / D3D9 / D3D11 report true AND render a real wireframe** (interior 0/1089, all three edges lit in the exact ink colour, total 556-607 against Solid's 18176); **WebGPU reports true and renders SOLID** — the WireFrame frame is byte-identical to the Solid one, which is `WEBGPU-115`'s already recorded, accepted deviation and therefore NOT a new finding; **Headless reports true and rasterizes nothing at all**; **EasyGL reports FALSE and renders a correct wireframe anyway** — the one genuinely new result, spawning `REMED-GFX-219`. **D3D12 cross-builds and is deliberately NOT called clean:** its device creation aborts under Wine for every device test in this file including the untouched `SupportsThreeD`, A/B-proven pre-existing. **DECISIVELY, NO BACKEND REJECTS A WIREFRAME REQUEST**, so the prompt-sketched "deterministic rejection" arm has an empty registration set and was not manufactured; what replaced the universal assertion is a per-backend capability arm, a POSITIVE PIXEL ORACLE, a DOCUMENTED-DEVIATION oracle for WebGPU that asserts the deviation is real and byte-identical to Solid (so it fails the day `WEBGPU-115` lands), a RECOVERY oracle run on every measured backend, and an HONEST SKIP plus an EXACT native-draw cardinality assertion on Headless. **THE ORACLE'S TEETH WERE PROVEN BY THREE INJECTIONS, not claimed:** forcing WebGPU into the positive arm fails on "filled 1089 interior pixels ... that is a solid fill, not a wireframe"; culling the geometry fails the Solid control at total 0 (and revealed that EasyGL's GL_LINES emulation survives triangle culling, which is why `CullMode::None` is load-bearing); moving one vertex zeroes exactly the two edge probes that touch it and names them individually. **FALSE-POSITIVE AUDIT:** 7 files / 138 tests inspected across every `FillMode`/`CullMode` mention in `tests/`; **1 defective (this one), 1 strengthened (this one), 0 new tickets.** The blind spot the audit names concretely: all three pre-existing wireframe pixel tests are bgfx- or point-topology-scoped and none compares WireFrame against a Solid control, so WebGPU's silent solid fill passed every one of them. | LOW | GRAPHICS | DONE |

| `REMED-GFX-211` | **Vulkan, bgfx and WebGPU ignore `VertexBufferBinding.VertexOffset` on the instanced route — DONE 2026-08-03 (Vulkan, bgfx and WebGPU scopes all DONE; checkpoint blocker RESOLVED). WEBGPU SCOPE DONE: the third backend had the same two-loss-point shape, and its per-vertex loss point was VULKAN's rather than bgfx's. In `WebGPUGraphicsBackend::DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx`, `command.vertexData` takes the WHOLE per-vertex buffer into a command-owned CPU vector and `IssueInstancedDraw` uploads it to a fresh buffer bound by `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetVertexBuffer(pass, 0, buf, /*offset=*/0, size)` — so binding 0 has no per-binding native offset channel and the only term reaching a decoded index is `wgpuRenderPassEncoderDrawIndexed`'s own `baseVertex`. The loss point was therefore `command.baseVertex = params.baseVertex`, now `params.baseVertex + perVertexOffset`; both are signed `int32_t`, so negative-`baseVertex` behaviour is unchanged, the index buffer is untouched and `startIndex` stays an index-element offset on `command.firstIndex`. The PER-INSTANCE loss was where the ticket said: `command.instVbData.assign(instShadow.begin(), instShadow.begin() + instanceCount * instVbStride)` took `instanceCount` consecutive records from record zero; its source base is now `vertexOffset * instVbStride`, this stream's own stride, never binding 0's, never advanced by `baseVertex`. Readings on `9df8770d`, pre → post: `instance-offset-ignored` → `instance-offset-honoured` (pre-fix cells (0,0) (1,0) (2,1) (5,3) — records 0..3, the decoy's own cell lit and live record 4's lost), `vertex-offset-ignored` → `vertex-offset-honoured` (pre-fix (4,2) (5,2) (6,3) (7,3), every instance rendering the mesh decoy), `both-offsets-ignored` → `both-offsets-honoured` (pre-fix (4,2) (5,2) (6,3), 768 lit). WebGPU moved out of `CNA_INSTANCED_OFFSET_DEFECT_MEASURED` into `CNA_INSTANCED_BINDING_OFFSET_ORACLE` in both instanced test files, which turns REMED-GFX-122/123's existing matrix on for it instead of duplicating it; that emptied the measured-defect set, so the macro and its four now-dead arms were DELETED and the triage legs keep only their `#else` UNCLASSIFIED arm for the still-unmeasured D3D9. Controls after the change: EasyGL 45/45, Vulkan 37/37, bgfx 42/42, WebGPU 37/37, Headless 5/5 (public transport only); `ctest` in `cmake-build-webgpu` 5918/5924 with all six failures recorded pre-existing. Only WebGPU production changed. REMED-GFX-212's WebGPU boundary is preserved and untouched. BGFX SCOPE DONE: both bindings' offsets now reach their own stream, exactly once, and the loss points are again two separate expressions in one function, `BgfxGraphicsBackend::DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx`. The PER-INSTANCE loss is `std::memcpy(idb.data, instVb.cpuData.data(), copyBytes)` — the copy took its source from record zero, so `instanceStream->vertexOffset` was never read; the fix moves the source base to `instVb.cpuData.data() + vertexOffset * instStride`, this stream's own stride, never binding 0's. The PER-VERTEX loss is `bgfx::setVertexBuffer(0, vb.handle, range.vertexStart, range.vertexCount)`, whose `vertexStart` carried `params.baseVertex` alone. bgfx has no draw-time base-vertex argument at all, so `_startVertex` is the ONLY term that reaches a decoded index — which is exactly why REMED-GFX-107/118 already deliver `baseVertex` through it, and why the ordinary bgfx indexed route was already correct: `params.baseVertex` there ALREADY contains the folded binding offset (REMED-GFX-200/201), while the instanced route folds nothing into it by design (REMED-GFX-202). The whole public offset therefore has to be added to that same term here, and it now is: the fetched element is `VertexOffset + baseVertex + index`, the bound remainder shrinks by exactly as much, the index buffer is untouched and `startIndex` stays an index-element offset. `RejectUnsupportedStreamCombination` guarantees exactly one per-vertex stream on this route, so the fold advances that stream and no other. The wireframe sibling rebases its expanded indices to absolute elements and then binds from zero, so it takes the same addend through `ExpandWireframeIndices`'s `baseVertex` parameter instead — once, not twice; REMED-GFX-118's assertion that the wireframe path keeps a zero-based binding stays green. Pre-fix readings reproduced on `cb1d2398` with the pre-fix cell maps recorded, and post-fix readings taken on the same fixture: leg A `instance-offset-ignored` → `instance-offset-honoured`, leg B `vertex-offset-ignored` → `vertex-offset-honoured`, leg C `both-offsets-ignored` → `both-offsets-honoured`. bgfx moved out of `CNA_INSTANCED_OFFSET_DEFECT_MEASURED` and into `CNA_INSTANCED_BINDING_OFFSET_ORACLE` in BOTH instanced test files, which turns the whole REMED-GFX-122/123/211/213 permanent matrix on for it; two further legs were added for the two matrix cells nothing yet covered — both offsets nonzero at frequency 3, and frequency 2 → 1 → 2 inside a SINGLE frame. bgfx 41/41 on the two instanced fixtures with 7 skipped (its declared `MultiStreamVertexInput = false` boundary, REMED-GFX-204) and 320/320 across the wider draw/vertex regression set with 21 skipped for the same boundary; EasyGL 45/45 and Vulkan 37/37 as controls; WebGPU still measures its own defect verbatim. Active bgfx renderer measured and reported: **OpenGL 2.1** — the Bgfx Vulkan renderer was NOT exercised and is UNTESTED here. bgfx diagnostics via `CNA_BGFX_TRACE_DIAGNOSTICS=1` are byte-identical between a frame of instanced draws and a device-creation-only run (66 lines each, all texture-format capability probes and EGL traces), so zero diagnostics are attributable to this path. ASan 42/42 and UBSan 42/42 with both runtimes proven linked, UBSan reporting zero runtime errors; the ASan leak total is Mesa/`libGLX` plus one bgfx render-thread `createUniform`, with no CNA frame in any stack and a HIGHER per-test leak rate on the non-instanced control (87.7 kB/test over 17 tests) than on the instanced run (60.1 kB/test over 49). Only bgfx production changed. VULKAN SCOPE DONE: both bindings' offsets now reach their own stream, exactly once. The two loss points were separate expressions in one function, `VulkanGraphicsBackend::DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` — the per-instance one is the `std::memcpy(d.instVbData.data(), instVb.GetMappedPtr(), …)` the triage named, and the per-vertex one is NOT the neighbouring `vb.GetMappedPtr()` copy at all but `d.baseVertex = params.baseVertex`, which dropped the geometry binding's whole offset term. That distinction decided the fix: the per-vertex copy takes the WHOLE buffer into the deferred arena and binds it at the draw's own packed arena offset, so binding 0 has no per-binding native offset channel to carry an offset in — but `vkCmdDrawIndexed`'s own `vertexOffset` argument is added to every decoded index, which is exactly the term the stream owes, and the route binds exactly one per-vertex stream, so folding it into `baseVertex` advances that stream and nothing else, exactly once. The per-instance side needed the copy's SOURCE base moved to `vertexOffset * instStride` — this stream's own stride, never binding 0's. Pre-fix readings reproduced on `d750c10a` and post-fix readings taken on the same binary: leg A `instance-offset-ignored` → `instance-offset-honoured`, leg B `vertex-offset-ignored` → `vertex-offset-honoured`, leg C `both-offsets-ignored` → `both-offsets-honoured`. Vulkan moved out of `CNA_INSTANCED_OFFSET_DEFECT_MEASURED` and into `CNA_INSTANCED_BINDING_OFFSET_ORACLE` in BOTH `InstancedDrawMultiStreamTests.cpp` and `InstancedDrawRangeTests.cpp`, which turns REMED-GFX-122/123's complete `RunBindingOffsetAndFrequencyOracle` on for Vulkan rather than inventing a parallel one, and seven new permanent legs cover the matrix the triage did not: baseVertex and VertexOffset as different multiples of a group (five distinct failure frames), a nonzero startIndex on both 16- and 32-bit indices, frequency 3 / frequency-above-instance-count, four queued draws under four different offset+frequency triples with the public bindings cleared before replay, a return to zero and back around ordinary draws across four frames, wrapper death with address reuse, and dynamic buffers. Vulkan 35/35 with 7 skipped (its declared `MultiStreamVertexInput = false` boundary, REMED-GFX-203); EasyGL runs the identical new legs 43/43 as the independent reference, which is what proves the legs rather than the implementation. | `REMED-GFX-212` | **`VertexColorEnabled` means different things per backend on the instanced route — DONE 2026-08-03 (Vulkan and WebGPU scopes both DONE; checkpoint blocker RESOLVED).** THE AUTHORITATIVE FNA ANSWER, cited from source: `GraphicsDevice.DrawInstancedPrimitives` (`GraphicsDevice.cs:1257`) is `ApplyState()` + `PrepareVertexBindingArray(baseVertex)` + the FNA3D call and touches no effect state; `BasicEffect.OnApply` (`BasicEffect.cs:490-511`) derives its shader index from fog/vertex-colour/texture/lighting with **no instancing term**; and every vertex-colour permutation of `BasicEffect.fx` multiplies `vout.Diffuse *= vin.Color` (line 85 and seven siblings), in a file containing **zero** instancing constructs. The same vertex shader runs for both routes, so `VertexColorEnabled` is draw-call-independent. **REPRODUCED FIRST, ON UNFIXED HEAD `199c9b7f`:** the new permanent oracle `tests/.../InstancedVertexColorTests.cpp` ran **1/9** on Vulkan and **1/9** on WebGPU, and the one that passed on each was exactly the `VertexColorEnabled = false` control. With `DiffuseColor = (0.500, 1.000, 0.250)` and four distinct COLOR0 records, both backends' ORDINARY route produced the analytic products `(128,128,16) (64,255,16) (32,128,64) (128,255,64)` while their INSTANCED route produced `(128,255,64)` in all four columns — DiffuseColor itself, i.e. COLOR0 ignored, not applied twice, not swizzled and not read un-normalized. **THE TWO LOSS POINTS ARE THE SAME CLASS BUT DIFFERENT EXPRESSIONS, CLASSIFIED INDEPENDENTLY — case C, and A only as its consequence.** Neither backend *lost* a colour attribute it had; each selects a dedicated instanced shader family that never declared one. Vulkan: `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D` unconditionally binds `kInstanced3dVertSpv`, whose whole colour body is `fragColor = pc.diffuseColor;` and whose only per-vertex input is `aPos` at location 0, and its `VkVertexInputAttributeDescription attrs[5]` therefore declares one per-vertex attribute and four per-instance ones. WebGPU: `CreateInstancedResources`'s single inline WGSL module declares `VertexInput { @location(0) position }` and `output.color = u.diffuseColor;`, and `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D` builds `std::array<WGPUVertexAttribute, 1> vertexAttrs`. `vertexColorEnabled` already ARRIVED at both (`FillInstancedPushConst`'s `pc[31]`, `FillExtUniforms`' `out[31]`) and was simply never read. **THE FIX IS THE SPLIT THE ORDINARY ROUTE ALREADY MAKES.** Both APIs require every user-defined vertex shader input to be backed by a vertex attribute description, so a position-only geometry stride and a position+colour one need different modules — exactly the colored3d/textured3d split the ordinary route uses. Vulkan gains `instanced_colored3d.vert.glsl` (regenerated into `spirv_shaders.hpp` through `compile_shaders.py`, 60 added lines and nothing removed) whose mixing expression is byte-for-byte `colored3d.vert.glsl`'s `(pc.vertexColorEnabled > 0.5) ? aColor * pc.diffuseColor : pc.diffuseColor`; WebGPU gains a second WGSL module whose expression is character-for-character `colored3d.wgsl`'s `select(u.diffuseColor, input.color * u.diffuseColor, vertexColorEnabled > 0.5)`. Each backend selects its variant from its own stride table — `PackedColorOffsetForStride` / `InstancedPackedColorOffsetForStride`, strides **16** and **24** at offset **12**, the same `R8G8B8A8_UNORM`/`Unorm8x4` element `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D`'s and `GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D`'s `attrs[1]` already bind — and adds it at shader location **1**, which cannot collide with the instance matrix at 4..7. **PIPELINE IDENTITY:** `VertexColorEnabled` is deliberately NOT a key dimension on either backend — it travels in the push constant/uniform, as it does for the ordinary colored pipeline. Vulkan's Instanced3D key additionally folds the RAW per-vertex stride (`FoldPerVertexStrideIntoKey`, bits 53..63, free above `FoldDepthFormatIntoKey`'s 45..52): `MakeExt3DKey` buckets 16 and every unrecognised stride together, which was harmless while every stride produced the same position-only layout and is not once the stride decides whether a COLOR0 attribute exists. WebGPU's salt already carried the exact stride. Measured: a cold stride-16 instanced draw builds **1** variant, toggling `VertexColorEnabled` builds **0**, alternating it four times in one cycle builds **0**, a stride-24 declaration builds its **own** variant, repeating every sequence builds **0** more, and the whole run ends at exactly **2** — 14/14 Vulkan checks and 18/18 WebGPU checks. No extra draw, pass, submit, frame, wait or readback: WebGPU stays at 1 render pass and 1 queue submit per render-target cycle whether one draw or four are issued and whether the flag is set or not. **VERIFICATION:** 9/9 on Vulkan, WebGPU, EasyGL and bgfx; 46/46 Vulkan instanced tests with `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` proven loaded and **zero** VUIDs; zero WebGPU uncaptured errors and zero device losses; ASan and UBSan clean on both backends. `REMED-GFX-211`'s offsets and `REMED-GFX-213`'s frequencies still hold WITH the colour, on 16- and 32-bit indices, and two queued draws in one cycle keep their own captured `VertexColorEnabled` and their own captured geometry across a buffer rewrite and a wrapper destruction. Only Vulkan and WebGPU production changed; bgfx and EasyGL were not touched. **TWO INDEPENDENT FINDINGS SPAWNED, NEITHER FIXED HERE:** `REMED-GFX-214` (WebGPU's ORDINARY route throws on a stride-24 draw with `TextureEnabled = false`) and `REMED-GFX-215` (bgfx's instanced shader emits the RAW COLOR0, ignoring DiffuseColor and VertexColorEnabled — the exact mirror image of this ticket, invisible to the triage oracle because its DiffuseColor was white).** | P1 | GRAPHICS | DONE |

No renderer-specific step-rate mechanism is exposed through this route either. | `REMED-GFX-214` | **WebGPU's ORDINARY route rejects a stride-24 `VertexPositionColorTexture` BasicEffect draw when `TextureEnabled` is false — OPEN.** Found while measuring `REMED-GFX-212`'s second COLOR0-bearing declaration; not caused by it and reproduced identically on unfixed HEAD `199c9b7f`. `WebGPUGraphicsBackend::DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` sends stride 20/24 to `QueueTexturedDraw` only when `params.texture0 != nullptr`, so a stride-24 draw with the texture off matches no branch and falls through to `DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`, whose `QueueColoredDraw` throws `std::invalid_argument` — "CNA WebGPU: DrawColoredPrimitives requires a stride-16 (VertexPositionColor) vertex buffer". XNA accepts that combination and FNA renders it with the vertex-colour-no-texture permutation, and Vulkan's ordinary route already handles it (`GetOrCreatePipelineFogTex3D`'s stride-24 arm with `colored_textured3d`, whose FS substitutes `vec4(1.0)` for the sample when `textureEnabled` is 0). A LOUD failure, not a silent wrong result, so it is a different class from `REMED-GFX-212` and is not folded into it. The boundary is measured and PRINTED by `InstancedVertexColorTest.PackedColorTextureStrideConsumesGeometryColorOnBothRoutes`, which records the exact rejection text rather than skipping. Smallest fix: route stride 20/24 with a null `texture0` to the textured pipeline against the default white texture, exactly as Vulkan does, rather than to the stride-16-only colored path. **TRIAGED 2026-08-04 (`plan_postaudit.md` §4.4.6) — CHECKPOINT BLOCKER: NO, DEFERRED.** Classified from source; no build and no run were required or performed. `DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` gate stride 20/24 on `params.texture0 != nullptr`, so with the texture off the draw matches no branch and falls through the documented "unmatched stride/texture combination" tail to `DrawColoredPrimitives`. `QueueColoredDraw`'s **first statement** throws `std::invalid_argument`, **before** `ColoredDrawCommand command;` and before every encoder call — so nothing is queued, no buffer is written, no pass is opened: no partial submission, no device loss, no memory corruption, and the caller receives a deterministic exception whose text an existing test records verbatim. No other permutation of the same input misrenders silently: every route for this stride is gated on the same condition and reaches the same tail. No capability claims it either way, which costs nothing here because the failure is loud. | MEDIUM | GRAPHICS | OPEN |
| `REMED-GFX-215` | **bgfx's instanced route ignored `BasicEffect.DiffuseColor` AND `BasicEffect.VertexColorEnabled`, emitting the raw per-vertex COLOR0 — DONE (2026-08-03); checkpoint blocker RESOLVED.** The exact mirror image of `REMED-GFX-212`: Vulkan and WebGPU kept DiffuseColor and dropped COLOR0, bgfx kept COLOR0 and dropped DiffuseColor. **THE FALSE POSITIVE, PROVEN ON BOTH AXES:** `REMED-GFX-212`'s triage certified bgfx a correct control using a WHITE DiffuseColor with `VertexColorEnabled = true`, and under a neutral DiffuseColor `COLOR0 × white == COLOR0`, so the defective shader produced byte-identical pixels to a correct one. The new `InstancedDiffuseColorTests.cpp` asserts that degeneracy arithmetically AND re-measures it live: its `true/neutral/instanced-route` leg passed on the unfixed shader, and only the `false/neutral/instanced-route` half — which triage never ran — caught it. A non-neutral `DiffuseColor = (0.80, 0.35, 0.55)` separates every failure mode by at least 20 in some channel. **AUTHORITATIVE CONTRACT, cited:** FNA `Common.fxh:36-45` opens every BasicEffect vertex shader with `vout.Diffuse = DiffuseColor`; `BasicEffect.fx:78-88` (`VSBasicVc`) adds exactly one line, `vout.Diffuse *= vin.Color`, repeated at lines 99/142/157/183/212/238/267; `BasicEffect.fx:66-73` (`VSBasic`) never mentions `vin.Color`; `BasicEffect.fx:336` (`PSBasic`) is `pin.Diffuse`; and `GraphicsDevice.cs:1257` shows `DrawInstancedPrimitives` touching no effect state, so there is no instancing term anywhere in the shader index. **ROOT CAUSE:** `vs_instanced3d.sc`'s entire colour body was `v_color0 = a_color0;` and it declared only `u_vp`/`u_depthBias` — no diffuse uniform for either term to arrive in — while `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` set only those same two uniforms. **FIX:** the architecture the ordinary route has carried since Task 364 — ONE program whose `a_color0` is gated by `u_vertexColorEnabled3D` and multiplied by `u_diffuseColor` — applied to the existing instanced program. No new program variant, no new cache key, no public API change, no extra draw/submit/view/frame/wait/readback/Present. Both uniforms are written unconditionally per instanced draw, because bgfx uniform values persist until overwritten. **MEASURED AFTER:** COLOR0 `(255,128,64) (64,255,128) (128,64,255) (255,255,255)` → `(204,45,35) (51,89,70) (102,22,140) (204,89,140)` with the flag true, and `(204,89,140)` in all four columns with it false; alpha 0.5 gives `(102,22,18,128) (26,45,35,96) (51,11,70,64) (102,45,70,32)`; the ordinary route reads identically in every leg. **VALIDATION:** 11/11 new legs (0/11 before), 9/9 `REMED-GFX-212` legs with bgfx's measured-defect exemption deleted, 2/2 cardinality legs (25 programs / 35 shaders / 40 uniforms unchanged across the whole matrix; exactly one native submit per public draw, equal to the ordinary route's); full bgfx suite 5850/5854 with the 4 residuals proven pre-existing by restoring the pre-fix production files; ASan 22/22 with zero memory-safety errors and leaks measured at exactly 103852 B/453 allocations **per GraphicsDevice** regardless of colour state, every frame in `libGLX_mesa`/`libdrm`, none in this repo; UBSan 22/22 with zero runtime errors; bgfx diagnostics 780 warnings, all `TextureFormat::*` init-time capability probes, zero fatals and zero shader/program/uniform/attribute diagnostics. (2) `SetData` then called `MakeBgfxLayout(stride)`, a hardcoded switch over 52/20/24/32/48/56/68 with a Position+Color0 fallback. (3) The layout was rebuilt only when `stride_in_bytes != stride`, which cannot see a re-declaration at the SAME stride. **MEASURED NATIVE LAYOUTS, read from `BgfxVertexBufferBackend::layout`, before → after:** `positionOnly12` (Pos@0, stride 12) `16, Pos@0, Color0@12` → `12, Pos@0, no Color0`; `colorPosition16` (Col@0, Pos@4) `16, Pos@0, Color0@12` → `16, Pos@4, Color0@0`; `positionTextureColor24` (Tex@12, Col@20) `Col@12, Tex@16` → `Col@20, Tex@12`; `positionColorPadded32` (Pos@0, Col@12, stride 32) `32, Pos@0, NO Color0, Tex@24` → `32, Pos@0, Color0@12`. Four of seven were wrong; the three that were right were right by coincidence of stride. **THREE DISTINCT MECHANISMS separated by the oracle** (a pixel count cannot name them, so each column reports colour, lit count against a four-step staircase, and top row, against a **non-black** clear so a lost colour attribute is distinguishable from absent geometry): `positionOnly12` = record desynchronization (lit 811/2311/4502/2558 vs 5760/4320/2880/1440, tops 8/26/15/24, **colour correct**); `colorPosition16` = nothing rasterized (position read from the colour bytes as floats); `positionTextureColor24` = **geometry exact, colour (0,0,0,63)** — pure attribute offset; `positionColorPadded32` = **geometry exact, colour (0,0,0,255)** — attribute absent. **FIX:** one declaration→`bgfx::VertexLayout` translator. It never assumes where `add()` landed, because bgfx's advance is per-renderer (`s_attribTypeSize`: Int16/Half×3 is 6 bytes on GL, 8 on D3D/Vulkan/WebGPU) — it skips to each declared offset, then verifies bgfx's own advance matched the declaration's byte span. `asInt` is false for every format because `varying.def.sc` declares `vec4 a_indices`, not an ivec4. Overlapping elements, a repeated semantic, an unmappable usage or format, an element outside its own stride, and a renderer/declaration size disagreement all raise `System::NotSupportedException` **before anything is created or submitted** — never a substituted guess. `MakeBgfxLayout(stride)` survives only as the pre-declaration default. **VALIDATION:** 12/12 bgfx legs (1/10 before); all 7 built-in vertex types reproduce the replaced switch's layout **byte for byte**; full bgfx suite 5862/5866 with the 4 residuals **byte-identical to the pre-fix run** (`diff` of the failure sets is empty); ASan 53/53 zero memory-safety errors with leaks measured at 103852 B/453 allocations **per GraphicsDevice**, linear, all frames in `libGLX_mesa`/`libdrm`; UBSan 53/53 zero runtime errors; bgfx diagnostics 1260 warnings, **all** `TextureFormat::*` init probes, **zero** fatals and **zero** layout/attribute/stride/program diagnostics. | `REMED-GFX-217` | **Vulkan, WebGPU, Software, SDL_GPU, D3D9, D3D11, D3D12 and Headless discard the `VertexDeclaration` and select a native vertex layout by stride — OPEN.** The same class as `REMED-GFX-216`, found by running its declaration oracle on every backend rather than only on the one the ticket named. `IVertexBufferBackend::SetVertexDeclaration` is pure virtual so that no backend can silently drop a declaration, yet each of these leaves it an empty override: `VulkanGraphicsBackend.hpp:576`, `WebGPUGraphicsBackend.hpp:467`, `SoftwareGraphicsBackend.hpp:38`, `SdlGpuGraphicsBackend.hpp:655`, `D3D9Buffers.hpp:47`, `D3D11Buffers.hpp:37`, `D3D12Buffers.hpp:46`, `HeadlessGraphicsBackend.hpp:179`. **MEASURED on a real display** with `DiffuseColor = (0.80,0.35,0.55)`: `colorPosition16` (Col@0, Pos@4, stride 16) renders **nothing** on Vulkan, WebGPU and Software; `positionTextureColor24` (Tex@12, Col@20) renders **exact geometry with colour (0,0,0,63)** on Vulkan and Software — the texture coordinate's `0.5f` bytes read as a normalized ubyte4; `positionColorPadded32` renders exact geometry with the colour attribute **lost entirely** (plain DiffuseColor `(204,89,140)`) on Vulkan and Software. Software additionally **refuses** stride 12 loudly ("unsupported vertex stride (only 16/20/24/32/52 supported in v1)") and WebGPU refuses five of the seven with `REMED-GFX-214`'s stride-16 message — those refusals are honest capability boundaries, not this defect. Silent wrong results on supported public draws, so this is the same P1 class as `REMED-GFX-216`; it needs per-backend scoping at triage. Not fixed under `REMED-GFX-216`, which was explicitly forbidden from touching another backend's production. `VertexDeclarationLayoutTest` asserts the measured deviation on each of these backends and requires it to remain visible, so every arm fails the moment its backend is corrected. **TRIAGED 2026-08-04 (`plan_postaudit.md` §4.4.2/§4.4.4) — no production change, no build, no run.** **SCOPE CORRECTED TO SEVEN BACKENDS: Headless is removed.** It rasterizes nothing — `DrawPrimitivesEx` validates its arguments and records a trace — so there is no native layout to get wrong and its empty override cannot produce a wrong result; it already reports `MultiStreamVertexInput = false` on exactly this reasoning. **THE EIGHT DO NOT SHARE ONE OBSERVABLE FAILURE, and the accepted-stride set is what separates them.** **Vulkan is the widest surface: it has no stride guard anywhere**, and `MakeExt3DKey`'s `default: s = 0` silently buckets every unrecognised stride with 16. Software accepts 16/20/24/32/52 and refuses the rest loudly; D3D9/11/12 accept the eight strides of the shared `D3DCommon` table and throw outside it; **WebGPU and SDL_GPU accept only stride 16 without a bound texture**, so six of the oracle's seven declarations never reach native submission and their silent-wrong surface is `colorPosition16` alone. **The native binding stride comes from the buffer, not from the table**, on every backend except bgfx — which is why bgfx desynchronized whole records on `positionOnly12` and these did not: only the attribute offsets are wrong. **DECLARATION CLASSES SEPARATED:** built-in declarations match the table by construction and are **correct on all ten backends** (every example, `SpriteBatch` and stock effect uses only these, which is why this was never visible); *same stride, different content* (`colorPosition16`, `positionTextureColor24`) is silently wrong but needs no new shader, because the family already has the right semantics; *same semantics, unusual padding* (`positionColorPadded32`) is worse — at stride 32 the table's semantic set is Position+Normal+TexCoord, so the declared Color has no attribute to land in, and correcting it needs the shader family chosen from the declaration rather than the stride. Vulkan renders `positionOnly12` correctly today **only** because `VertexColorEnabled` is false and the out-of-record Color@12 is never read. D3D9/11/12 are source-derived, not measured (no D3D display), which does **not** leave the classification at REVIEW: the loss point is the **shared** `D3DCommon` table whose public consequence is already measured at pixel level on Vulkan and Software. **CNA PERMITS BACKEND-SPECIFIC REJECTION** — `GraphicsCapability::MultiStreamVertexInput` (default false, `GraphicsDevice::ValidateVertexStreamCapability` throws `System::NotSupportedException` before native submission, and its own doc names `SetVertexDeclaration` as the analogy) and `REMED-GFX-216`'s bgfx translator both establish it, so **no new public API is needed**. **CHECKPOINT DISPOSITION: not a blocker in the "must be fully implemented" sense; full per-backend translation is LARGE and is deferred to modularization with `REMED-GFX-203` … `-208`. What the checkpoint requires is ONE bounded guard task** (Strategy C) — one shared predicate called from the seven empty `SetVertexDeclaration` overrides, touching no public API, no `GraphicsDevice.cpp`, no `IGraphicsBackend.hpp` and no `GraphicsCapability.hpp`, hence zero contention with the 19 integration branches. **The predicate must be asymmetric:** reject when the declaration names a semantic the stride's table entry lacks, or places a shared semantic at a different offset or format. A strict equality test is NOT safe — it would reject `positionOnly12`, which renders correctly on Vulkan today. `VertexDeclarationLayoutTest` already accommodates rejection: its `deviatesElsewhere` arm is guarded on `r.rendered`. Known consequence, stated up front: `Xnb::VertexDeclarationReader` and the public `DrawUserPrimitives`/custom-`VertexBuffer` surface go from silently wrong pixels to a deterministic exception on seven backends. **CHECKPOINT BLOCKER RESOLVED 2026-08-04 by `REMED-GFX-DECL-GUARD` — the ticket itself stays OPEN for the seven native translators.** The bounded action is recorded here rather than as a new finding, because it creates none: it is remediation of THIS ticket. One pure header-only predicate, `include/CNA/Internal/Graphics/VertexDeclarationFidelity.hpp`, decides whether a declaration can be represented faithfully by the layout a backend actually programs, and each of the seven calls it at the top of `DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx`/`DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` — before any pipeline, PSO, `ID3D11InputLayout` or `IDirect3DVertexDeclaration9` is created, before any command is queued and before any submission. **Header-only, and that is a link fact rather than a style choice:** each backend is its own static library linked against `cna_backend_graphics_common` and SharpRuntime, not against the CNA library, so a translation unit under `src/CNA/Internal/Graphics/` is invisible to them and the alternative was a new shared build target this task was forbidden to add. **No CMake file changed.** **THE RULE IS ASYMMETRIC — R0** native record advance equals the declared stride; **R1** no native fetch reads bytes a declared element owns under another semantic/usage index/offset/format; **R2** every declared element has a native attribute with the same usage AND usage index, at the same offset, in the same format; **R3** every declared element lies inside the declared stride; **R4** declared elements do not overlap. A native attribute the declaration does not name is **not** a violation, which is precisely why Vulkan's `positionOnly12` still renders: its fallback binds `Color0@12`, the declaration names no colour, and the two never meet. A strict equality rule would have removed it. **THE UNLISTED-STRIDE BEHAVIOUR IS MEASURED PER ROUTE, not assumed:** Vulkan ordinary falls back to Position@0+Color@12 (`positionOnly12` renders correctly today), Vulkan and WebGPU instanced are position-only (`PackedColorOffsetForStride` lists 16 and 24 only), and Software/WebGPU-ordinary/SDL_GPU/D3D9/D3D11/D3D12 refuse the stride themselves — there the guard **abstains** and every pre-existing rejection message is byte-identical after this task. **GUARDING AT `SetVertexDeclaration` WAS THE OBVIOUS SITE AND IS WRONG:** per-instance streams carry declarations the per-vertex stride table has never described (the instancing fixture's own stream is stride 64 with `TextureCoordinate1..4`), and a buffer does not know at upload time which role it will play. The overrides now only REMEMBER the declaration; the verdict is taken at draw time on the geometry stream. **MEASURED, before → after, on the three colliding declarations:** `colorPosition16` accepted-and-blank → `System::NotSupportedException` naming `Color0@0` and the native offset 12, on Vulkan/Software/WebGPU/SDL_GPU/EasyGL/D3D9/D3D11/D3D12; `positionTextureColor24` accepted with colour `(0,0,0,63)` → exception naming the texture coordinate's offset; `positionColorPadded32` accepted with the colour lost → exception naming the semantic the stride-32 entry does not bind. **Every currently-correct case is unchanged**, including all seven built-in declarations, `positionOnly12` on Vulkan, `positionTexture20`, `positionColorTexture24` and every loud pre-existing out-of-table refusal. **bgfx is the control** and still renders all three correctly, which is what makes these refusals a per-backend capability boundary rather than a claim that the declarations are invalid. **VALIDATION:** new `VertexDeclarationFidelityTest` (24 backend-independent legs asserting the rule itself, accepted cases as hard as rejected ones) and new `DeclarationGuardTest` (refusal rasterizes nothing; valid-after-refusal renders; static/dynamic × index16/index32; `DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` reaches the same boundary; bgfx translator control). `VertexDeclarationLayoutTest`'s `deviatesElsewhere` arms now assert deterministic rejection instead of a measured wrong picture, and SDL_GPU was added to the oracle. Guard suites 34/34 on Software, Vulkan, WebGPU, SDL_GPU, D3D9 and D3D11, 35/35 on EasyGL, 37/37 on bgfx, 24/24 on Headless. Full principal suites: Software 5743/5790, Vulkan 5842/5873, EasyGL 5884/5890, WebGPU 5865/5896, SDL_GPU 5781/5804, bgfx 5887/5917, Headless 5687/5733 — **every failure A/B-proven pre-existing** (`XnbContainerFuzzTest`, `CnjEffectTest`/`CnjStockEffectTest` GLSL-on-a-non-GL-backend, `GraphicsDeviceValidationTest.SetRenderTargets_FourTargets_DoesNotThrow`, `REMED-GFX-209`'s own `DoesNotSupportWireFrame`, and one 30 s networking test), the Vulkan set proven by restoring `VulkanGraphicsBackend.*` from HEAD and reproducing all four. D3D9 and D3D11 **RUN under Wine on `:99`** at 34/34; D3D12 cross-builds and its first three legs prove the guard (`colorPosition16` rendered pre-guard and rejects post-guard) before a **pre-existing** stride-20 crash under Wine, A/B-proven by reverting `D3D12GraphicsBackend.*` and reproducing the same crash point. ASan/UBSan clean with runtimes proved linked: software-asan 101/101, software-ubsan 101/101, vulkan-asan 120/120, easygl-asan+ubsan 143/143, **zero** AddressSanitizer reports and **zero** runtime errors. Diagnostics clean: Vulkan validation layer loaded and **0 VUID**, WebGPU 0 uncaptured/device-lost, SDL_GPU 0, EasyGL 0 GL errors, bgfx 0 fatals. No extra draw, submit, pass, frame, wait, readback or allocation on an accepted draw; a refused draw creates none of them and leaves the render target byte-identical to its clear. | HIGH | GRAPHICS | OPEN (checkpoint blocker RESOLVED) |
| `REMED-GFX-218` | **EasyGL binds vertex attributes by their INDEX in the declaration's element list instead of by semantic — OPEN.** A different mechanism from `REMED-GFX-217` reaching the same class of wrong pixels, which is why it is a separate ticket. EasyGL is the one backend that does consume the declaration (`EasyGLVertexBufferBackend::SetVertexDeclaration` stores `GetVertexElements()`), but `ApplyLayout` then assigns `const auto location = static_cast<unsigned int>(i)` — the element's position in the list (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp:3477`). The shader's input locations are fixed by its own source, so any declaration whose element ORDER differs from the shader's input order binds every attribute to the wrong slot, even though every offset, format and stride is correct. Related to `REMED-GFX-201`'s finding that XNA composes streams by SEMANTIC and not by position, one layer lower. **MEASURED:** `colorPosition16` (Col@0, Pos@4) renders **nothing**; `positionTextureColor24` (Tex@12, Col@20) renders exact geometry with colour `(102,45,0,255)` — a different wrong colour from Vulkan's and Software's, which is the evidence that the mechanism differs; `positionColorPadded32` loses the colour attribute. The built-in declarations all happen to list their elements in the shaders' input order, which is why this has never been visible. Not fixed under `REMED-GFX-216`. Asserted as a measured deviation that fails when corrected. **TRIAGED 2026-08-04 (`plan_postaudit.md` §4.4.5) — no production change, no build, no run. CLASSIFIED MEDIUM, NOT SMALL.** The tempting one-line fix — replace `location = i` with `location = SemanticSlot(usage)` — **is wrong, for three independently sufficient reasons found in the source.** (1) **There is no global semantic→location function to write:** EasyGL's stock shaders bind location 1 to `aColor` at stride 16, to `aUV` at stride 20 and to `aNormal` at stride 32, and location 5 is `aColor` in the skinned families — the correct location depends on the **bound program family**, not on the semantic alone. (2) **`ApplyLayout` runs at UPLOAD time**, from `SetData` (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp:3644/3683/3701`), where no program is bound and no `GpuDrawParams` exists; the family is known only at draw time, in `SelectProgram(stride, params)` — four call sites (5877, 5949, 6083, 6116). (3) **The index convention is load-bearing and, on one path, intentional:** `ConfigureDeclarationAttributes`, `FirstLocationForStream` (`REMED-GFX-201`) and `PerVertexLocationCount` (`REMED-GFX-202`) all concatenate locations by element **count**, and the `ShaderEffect` custom-program path documents "location N == Nth field of the ported HLSL input struct" as its contract with the caller — a custom GLSL shader may declare inputs CNA has no semantic for, so mapping by semantic there would **break** custom shaders. The correct fix is therefore per-family semantic placement on the **stock** path only, at draw time, leaving the custom-`ShaderEffect` index contract untouched and not disturbing `REMED-GFX-201`/`-202`'s location arithmetic — deferred to modularization. **CHECKPOINT DISPOSITION: covered by the same bounded Strategy-C guard as `REMED-GFX-217`**, applied inside `SelectProgram` for the stock families only. Guarding EasyGL is not optional at the checkpoint: guarding the other seven and not EasyGL would leave EasyGL the **only** backend silently rendering the wrong thing, a worse divergence than today. **CHECKPOINT BLOCKER RESOLVED 2026-08-04 by `REMED-GFX-DECL-GUARD` — the ticket itself stays OPEN for per-family semantic placement.** No new finding was created; this is remediation of THIS ticket. The guard compares the declaration, element by element in declaration order, against the **selected stock program's own ordered input list**, transcribed from the `layout(location=N) in ...` lines of the shaders `Ensure*Program()` compiles — `colored3d` `[aPos, aColor]`, `textured3d` `[aPos, aUV]`, `colored_textured3d` `[aPos, aColor, aUV]`, `lit_textured3d` `[aPos, aNormal, aUV]`, `pbr3d` `[aPos, aNormal, aTangent, aUV]`, `skinned3d` `[aPos, aNormal, aUV, aBoneWeights, aBoneIndices, aColor]`, `pbr_skinned3d` `[aPos, aNormal, aTangent, aUV, aBoneWeights, aBoneIndices]`. **THE CASCADE IS NOW SINGLE-SOURCED:** `SelectProgram`'s twelve-way selection was factored into one `SelectStockProgramShape(stride, params)` that both the program selection and the guard read, so the program a draw is bound to and the input shape it is checked against cannot drift apart — the one failure mode a transcribed table would otherwise have. **THE RULE IS ASYMMETRIC IN ITS OWN WAY:** only the locations the declaration actually fills are checked. A declaration shorter than the program's input list leaves the remaining inputs unbound, which is GL's constant-attribute case — a MISSING input, not a reinterpretation of declared bytes — and is exactly what EasyGL's correct `positionOnly12` rendering relies on today. **`params.customEffectBackend != nullptr` skips the guard entirely**, so a custom `ShaderEffect` keeps its documented element-index convention. Proven, not asserted: a dedicated control renders `colorPosition16` through a `ShaderEffect` declaring `aColorIn` at location 0 and `aPosIn` at location 1, and reads back each column's own **unmultiplied** record colour — which is only possible if location 0 really did bind the declaration's first element. **MEASURED, before → after:** `colorPosition16` rendered nothing → `System::NotSupportedException` naming `colored3d`, element 0 and `aPos`; `positionTextureColor24` rendered colour `(102,45,0,255)` → exception naming `colored_textured3d` and element 1; `positionColorPadded32` lost the colour → exception naming `lit_textured3d` and element 1. `ApplyLayout` and `REMED-GFX-201`/`-202`'s `FirstLocationForStream`/`PerVertexLocationCount` arithmetic are untouched. EasyGL guard suite 35/35; full EasyGL principal suite **5884/5890 with its single failure (`XnbContainerFuzzTest`) pre-existing and shared with every other backend**; ASan+UBSan 143/143 with zero reports; zero GL errors. | HIGH | GRAPHICS | OPEN (checkpoint blocker RESOLVED) |
| `REMED-GFX-219` | **EasyGL reports `GraphicsCapability::WireFrame` as false while its own renderer produces a correct wireframe — OPEN, NEW 2026-08-04, found by `REMED-GFX-209`'s corrected oracle.** `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::SupportsCapability` (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp:2187`) returns `false` for `WireFrame` with the comment *"GLES3 (EasyGL's underlying API) has no wireframe fill mode at all"*, and the header repeats it (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.hpp:776`). That claim is stale: `ApplyRasterizerState` already sets `wireframe_ = (fillMode == 1)` (`EasyGLGraphicsBackend.cpp:2999`) and all four triangle draw routes re-expand into `GL_LINES` through `DrawWireframe` (`:5853`, `:5893`, `:5981`, `:6062`) — the emulation landed in `a55397f7` on 2026-06-30, **two and a half weeks before** the capability query was written in `33d6540b` on 2026-07-17. **MEASURED, not derived**, by `GraphicsDeviceCapabilityTest`'s new pixel oracle on a real display: EasyGL renders **total 559 lit pixels, interior 0/1089, all three edge probes lit at 25 px each in the exact ink colour `(255,96,32,255)`**, against Solid's 18176 — a wireframe indistinguishable in quality from Vulkan's `VK_POLYGON_MODE_LINE`, and byte-identical across a WireFrame -> Solid -> WireFrame transition. **This is an accepted-path wrong RESULT, not a capability boundary:** the public `GraphicsDevice::SupportsCapability` query answers `false` about something the public `RasterizerState.FillMode` path performs correctly, so a game that gates its wireframe mode on the query disables a feature that works. **Independent of `REMED-GFX-209`**, which is a test-contract defect and changes no production file; independent of `WEBGPU-115`, which is the opposite error (reports true, renders solid) and is already recorded. **Scope: SMALL** — return `true` from EasyGL's `WireFrame` arm, correct both stale comments, and correct `plan_graphics.md`'s coverage-table row for `FillMode::WireFrame`, whose EasyGL cell reads *ℹ️ EasyGL N/A (GLES3)* and records the same non-existent boundary. `GraphicsDeviceCapabilityTest.WireFrameCapabilityReportIsThisBackendsOwn` asserts the CURRENT `false` so the baseline stays truthful and **fails deliberately** the moment this ticket lands, which is where the fix must also flip that arm. Checkpoint blocker: NO — the renderer is correct, only the report is wrong, and it under-reports rather than promising a false success. Integration blocker: NO. | LOW | GRAPHICS | OPEN |

### REMED-GFX-018 renderer-route addendum — DONE 2026-08-02

The final Bgfx shard miss belonged to the existing REMED-GFX-018 fixture; no new or duplicate ID was
created. On a DRI3-less Xvfb, `CNA_BGFX_RENDERER=VULKAN` requested Vulkan but bgfx failed Vulkan
presentation initialization and activated OpenGL 2.1. The fixture nevertheless selected its depth
probes from the request, so the active OpenGL route received Vulkan `[0,1]` clip-depth positions and
reported 15 false lower-bracket failures (**217/232**). Bgfx ClearOptions production was correct for
the renderer actually executing the draws.

The fixture now takes the clip convention from active `bgfx::Caps`, reports requested and active
renderers explicitly, and runs two complete initialization/teardown cycles. Requested OpenGL with
active OpenGL passes **233/233 twice**. Requested Vulkan with active OpenGL first passes the same
complete active-OpenGL oracle **233/233 twice**, then returns deterministic CTest unavailable code
77; it is a skip, never a Vulkan pass. If bgfx genuinely activates Vulkan, the same route executes
and may pass only as active Vulkan. The serial shard is **181 passed, 1 explicitly unavailable, 0
failed**. No production backend, other backend, extra frame/Present/retry/wait, or `audit/` file
changed. Detailed evidence is in `REMEDIATION_PROGRESS.md`.

### Current GRAPHICS recommendation (inventory checkpoint, 2026-07-25)

No GRAPHICS remediation is in progress. Historical “recommended next” lines in individual closure
reports are not a live queue. GFX-004, GFX-039, GFX-040, GFX-043, and GFX-054 are DONE; this
closure intentionally makes no successor selection. GFX-012 is DONE, so the GFX-092 close-time
recommendation of GFX-012 is superseded. GFX-085 and GFX-086 remain deferred capability
boundaries, not production defects.

## P2 (44) and P3 (28)

Listed in `MASTER_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md` in ID order under their priority headings. Rather than
duplicating them here, the useful cross-cuts:

### Completed P2 remediation

| ID | Resolution |
|---|---|
| `REMED-GFX-030` | Software depth-state correctness — **DONE and VERIFIED (2026-07-26)**. The raster path previously discarded `DepthBufferWriteEnable` and reduced every enabled depth test to `LessEqual`; accepted fragments also overwrote depth even when writes or testing were disabled. Software now snapshots enable/write/function for each intended draw, evaluates depth before colour, writes stored depth only after a passing enabled test with writes enabled, and maps all eight public `CompareFunction` values without fallback. `None` remains test/write disabled, `Default` remains enabled/enabled/`LessEqual`, and `DepthRead` now tests existing depth without modifying it. Float depth storage is unchanged; complete active-target clears, GFX-079 viewport-depth mapping, and GFX-083 constant/slope bias behavior are preserved. The public contract is **3/4→4/4** and the permanent Software matrix is **19/38→38/38**, covering disabled/enabled/write-disabled modes, strict/inclusive equality, all comparisons, failed-fragment immutability, A→B→A state capture/restoration, complete clears, backbuffer/RenderTarget2D transitions, and target isolation. Full Software shard **15/15**; focused ASan+UBSan **6/6**; all 14 backend libraries compile at `-j4` or less. Regression `28f2590b`, fix `f8aa0be8`. |
| `REMED-GFX-051` | SDL_GPU depth-bias pipeline state — **DONE and VERIFIED (2026-07-26)**. Installed SDL **3.5.0** exposes constant factor, slope factor, clamp, and an explicit enable bit only in graphics-pipeline rasterizer state; it has no dynamic depth-bias command. CNA now snapshots both public bias floats with each deferred draw, normalizes signed zero and non-polygon topologies to disabled state, enables bias only when a triangle factor is nonzero, maps CNA/XNA factors directly to SDL's native `constant*r + slope*m` factors with clamp fixed at zero, and keys every pipeline family by the explicit enable bit and exact enabled values. Dynamic viewport/target identity remain excluded. The permanent SDL_GPU Vulkan regression is **27/57→57/57**, validation-clean, and covers zero, positive/negative constant and slope, combined bias, flat/sloped triangles, line normalization, indexed/non-indexed draws, A→B→A restoration, deferred source mutation/destruction, backbuffer/compatible `RenderTarget2D` objects, depthless/Default/DepthRead, viewport depth range, stock/custom Effect and SpriteBatch families, and exact cache cardinality (10 RT variants; one backbuffer-format variant; 3 stock-SpriteBatch and 2 custom-effect variants). Full SDL_GPU suite **38/38**; focused ASan and UBSan **57/57** each; GFX-030 public depth contract passes on Vulkan, Software, D3D9, and D3D11; practical Vulkan/EasyGL/Bgfx/Software/D3D9/D3D11 controls preserve the established results; all 14 backend libraries compile at `-j4` or less. Regression `d931d012`, fix `4a8eadf1`. |

## By affected backend

A backend's count is not a quality ranking — the most-tested backends surface the most defects. EasyGL
(218 test files) and Vulkan (70) are the two most heavily exercised, and unsurprisingly appear most.

| Backend | Tasks | Notable |
|---|---|---|
| **Vulkan** | 9 | Widest single-backend defect count: Y-flip (4 families), SpriteBatch transform, RT scissor, ambient/emissive, fog, skinned normal, + the Texture2D crash |
| **D3D11 / D3D12** | 8 | Mostly via shared `D3DCommon` — one fix closes both. D3D12 additionally: stencil/scissor inert, occlusion overwrite, 1-CTest coverage gap |
| **EasyGL** | 6 | Default Linux/Emscripten backend. UAF, MRT, skinned normal, PBR normal variant |
| **Bgfx** | 7 | Cull mode, ClearOptions, fog, skinned normal, env-map, 2 unannotated failing tests |
| **SdlGpu** | 7 | Fog absent entirely, GLSL dialect, constructor leak (**GFX-028 DONE: transactional ownership**), skinned normal, env-map, cube mips, depth bias |
| **WebGPU** | 4 | Texture2D crash (non-catchable panic), SpriteBatch clip space, skinned normal, env-map |
| **D3D9** | 3 (+`GFX-060` post-audit) | Object-space fog in custom shaders, PBR skinned normal. Vendored stock effects immune by construction. **`REMED-GFX-060`** (DONE): draw paths dropped `DrawPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedPrimitives` vertex offsets (15 sites, the D3D9 counterpart of GFX-020) — runtime-verified on Wine+DXVK9 |
| **SdlRenderer** | 3 | Fullscreen crash, stale test expectations, depth-decision ambiguity |
| **Software** | 3 | Depth write/function inert, rotation formula (shared with Dx3), Texture3D |
| **Dx3** | 2 | Rotation defect, resize destroys-before-replace |
| **Headless** | 3 | primitiveCount, Texture3D, WireFrame capability |
| **Ascii / Canvas** | 0 | No MEDIUM+ findings. Both confirmed clean of the `RegisterForWindow` bug |
| **ALL / shared** | 30+ | XNA-facing layer and `IGraphicsBackend` — the highest-leverage fixes |

| Source | Tasks traced |

| `AUDIT_CROSS_CUTTING_FINDINGS.md` | 78 |

| Per-file `*.audit.md` reports only (**not in any synthesis doc**) | **3** |

Those last three matter: `REMED-CONTENT-006` (stack-exhaustion DoS + two dead security controls),
`REMED-GFX-043` (`DrawUserPrimitives` declaration dropped), and part of `REMED-TEST-005` were
recovered **only** by the exhaustive per-file sweep run while building this plan. They are a gap in
the audit's synthesis layer, not in its per-file work — the evidence was correctly recorded, it just
never propagated upward. See `REMEDIATION_TRACEABILITY.md` § Synthesis gap.
